Explicit dimensions that differ from the frame stretch the model; the preview shows a note. Export frames at in-game size to keep the art at 1:1.

To import a pack without changing the sprite's in-game size and offset, add --source <pack folder> --keep-geometry. A pack.json in the pack folder can set "keep_geometry": true and other import settings; see the importer's header comment.

Core player animation IDs include idle, walk, run, jump, fall, land, crouch, slide, dash, air_dash, wall_slide, wall_cling, attack, attack_alt, shoot, hurt, die, and spawn. Optional imported IDs can be remapped through Config.player.animation_overrides in the commented developer/config/game_config.lua; do not edit mvtk/animation.

The importer's --target-height and --upscale options are explicit resampling options for art drawn at another size. Prefer exporting at the intended in-game size. Player Size below stretches the existing art for size experiments.

Resize the player#

Each player model's files define its normal in-game size. Keep Player Size at 1.00 (100%) for art exported at that size. Change the single player_scale value to experiment with a larger or smaller body and model. Prepare & Check warns when your player's standing body is unusually tall or short for your tiles and suggests a Player size you can choose in Game Settings.

  1. Stop the game and open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Player. Set Player size (1 = 100%) between 1/7 and 8 using its numeric field.
  2. Leave scale_movement = true to scale speeds, jump height, gravity, and traversal ranges with the body. Set it to false when you want to retune those movement values by hand.
  3. Click Save in Game Settings to write the scale and rebake the player sprite size and all player collision resources. If you edited the config file directly, stop the game and run Project ▸ Prepare & Check instead.
  4. Run a normal Build, then use a fresh playtest to check floors, walls, ladders, one-way platforms, low roll/slide gaps, melee reach, and jump feel.

Values such as collider halves, speeds, jump height, gravity, and ranges in player_settings.lua and in the Game Settings fields are world-pixel numbers at Player Size 1.00. With the scale_movement = true default, runtime applies player_scale exactly once to movement and traversal values. Changing the config value does not require Reload Editor Scripts, but direct config edits still require the explicit Prepare & Check bake.

Add custom enemy art and place an enemy#

Recommended: link the original art from Tiled. Keep one snake_case folder per skin outside the Defold project, for example enemies/moss_bug/ beside your original maps. Each state's frames need the same size. Sprite strips and sheets must be sliced into individual PNGs before linking them.

  1. Add individual numbered PNG frames to that folder, using <state>_<number>.png. Every linked skin needs idle_01.png; additional states can use walk_01.png, walk_02.png, attack_01.png, hurt_01.png, and death_01.png.
  2. Make art face right; the runtime flips it when moving left.
  3. In Tiled, place templates/enemies/enemy.tx.
  4. Set its optional art file property to a numbered frame in the original folder, such as enemies/moss_bug/idle_01.png. Export copies the folder's frames; its name supplies the skin ID.
  5. Choose enemy_type, the first property, for synced art. Keep enemy_type=default to use the linked folder's ID. A deliberately chosen preset still supplies its stats when Archetype is none.
  6. Choose Archetype for stats and behaviour independently of art: knight and bat can use the same skin with different health and movement. none keeps the matching preset; art-only IDs use the commented default_archetype in developer/config/enemy_defaults.lua.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check. It syncs art and bakes each placed enemy into an editable room-collection gameobject.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Runtime summons use their own synced art and collision body; tune Enemy cap per room in Game Settings ▸ Enemies and elements ▸ Enemies (0 means unlimited).

To make a larger or smaller enemy:

  1. Select the placed Enemy in Tiled.
  2. Expand Stats in Properties.
  3. Set Size to a positive multiplier, such as 2 or 0.5; -1 inherits the archetype scale.
  4. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  7. Open the room collection to inspect the enemy instance's scale.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  10. Choose Play Game with an empty save slot.

Size keeps the enemy's feet where they are at Size 1, including flying enemies. It multiplies the archetype's scale and the elite scale; collision bodies, shot height and ledge margins follow the result. The existing enemy point lift is unchanged. An archetype scale other than 1 can still shift baked feet; runtime ground summons settle using their full body scale. Check 2 and 0.5 enemies with physics debug to compare their bodies, floor placement and shots. To resize an NPC, resize the NPC rectangle and its art in Tiled.

To add another Archetype choice:

  1. Add a commented entry to developer/config/enemy_types.lua.
  2. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check. Its first stage refreshes the dropdown.
  3. Reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled to see the new entry.

After editing, adding, or removing original frames, export the room again, then Prepare & Check and Build. Links into the Defold project are refused; use an external original or the folder workflow below. If two original folders have the same skin ID, the exporter keeps the existing project copy and warns. Give unrelated skins different folder names.

Folder workflow: dropping art straight into the project still works. Create developer/assets/enemies/moss_bug/ with individual PNG frames. This workflow also accepts single-state names such as idle.png; each skin needs idle.png or idle_01.png. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check, then choose moss_bug from Tiled's enemy_type dropdown. Leave art empty, export, prepare, and build.

Only synced folder IDs appear in Tiled's enemy_type dropdown. With Archetype set to none, a matching developer/config/enemy_types.lua entry supplies behaviour; otherwise the configured default archetype does. An explicit Archetype selects a commented preset without changing the art. The toolkit ships only the default/ fallback skin; sample and training worlds bring their own art. Keep default/ unless you supply another synced default.

After a toolkit update, fully restart Tiled to load the exporter and template changes. Follow the update hand-over for editor-module changes. Run Prepare & Check again before Export Game after changes under developer/config, developer/ui or hooks, because those edits stale the publication.

Make an enemy stay defeated#

  1. In Tiled, select a placed Enemy on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Open On defeat.
  3. Set Respawn to never.
  4. Set Defeat flag to a unique name, such as courtyard_guard_defeated.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  10. Defeat the enemy.
  11. Touch a Checkpoint or rest at a Save Point to save that defeat.
  12. Leave the room and return to check that the enemy stays gone.
  13. Quit and continue the same saved slot to check that it stays gone after loading.

Defeat flag is an ordinary saved flag that gates can read. Keep its name stable after release; leaving it blank uses the baked room/object identity. Without a save, dying restores the last saved flags and can bring the enemy back. Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Enemies and elements ▸ Enemies ▸ Save after a permanent defeat saves immediately after a never defeat when enabled. Click Save and Prepare & Check, then Build, after changing settings.

Enemy respawn in that same section chooses the project default. Respawn type_default uses the archetype's respawn setting, then the project default (room_reload). always returns whenever you enter the room, even when it stayed cached. room_reload returns when the room is rebuilt. save stays gone during exploration and returns after Save Point rest, death or loading: immediately in the current room, otherwise on your next entry. Checkpoints save progress but do not count as rest or revive save enemies. Revived enemies start at full health and their original position. Dormant enemies have no sprite, collision or Dev Menu ▸ Stats state label.

On defeat ▸ species is an optional bestiary identity. Blank keeps the current art-first rule: enemy_type, then archetype, then skin. Set a species to group different art or distinguish enemies sharing the same art. Clear in_bestiary to exclude this placed enemy from recording and totals.

Set elements on enemies and bosses#

  1. Select a placed Enemy in Tiled.
  2. Expand elements.
  3. Choose physical in weak_to to make physical-family hits deal extra damage.
  4. Choose a type in resist_to to reduce that type's damage.
  5. Choose a type in immune_to to prevent its damage.
  6. Choose types in vulnerable_to to make all other types harmless.
  7. Choose damage_type for this enemy's contact, melee and projectile attacks.
  8. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The four affinity fields are multi-select dropdowns: weak_to adds weakness, resist_to adds resistance, immune_to prevents damage, and vulnerable_to makes all unselected types harmless. Blank lists inherit the archetype; none for damage_type inherits its outgoing type. Without an outgoing override, contact and melee are physical and shots are projectile. Physical includes slash, projectile and impact. The empty toolkit's player therefore deals physical-family damage by default. Immune hits show the configured source-text label and play a short flash and sound; switching Damage numbers off hides only the label. Weak hits use a distinct flash and enlarged numbers when enabled.

  1. Select a placed Boss in Tiled.
  2. Expand damage_affinities.
  3. Choose types in weak_to, resist_to, immune_to or vulnerable_to.
  4. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Boss attack patterns set their outgoing damage_type in developer/config/attack_patterns.lua; an omitted type means physical. Preset affinities and exact damage_taken factors live in the commented enemy_types.lua and boss_types.lua files. Multipliers, colours, weak flashes and immune feedback live in damage_types.lua. Prepare & Check refreshes the type dropdowns; reopen the Tiled project after changing that vocabulary.

Place a mounted shooter#

  1. Place an Enemy template in Tiled.
  2. Choose enemy_type for its synced art.
  3. Set Archetype to turret.
  4. Expand movement_and_attacks.
  5. Set aim_mount to floor, ceiling, wall_left, wall_right or free.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  10. Click Export.
  11. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.

type_default inherits the archetype's mount (floor for turret); a preset without a mount uses enemy_defaults.lua's default_aim_mount (free). Floor shooters cover the upper half-circle, ceiling shooters the lower half, and wall names identify the supporting wall. free covers a full circle. Mounts use world directions independently of sprite facing. Keep the scaled projectile origin (18 pixels above the enemy anchor at Size 1) outside the supporting wall.

Tune aim_turn_speed (degrees/second), aim_tolerance (degrees), aim_lead (0 disables prediction), burst_count and burst_interval (seconds) in developer/config/enemy_types.lua; shared fallbacks live in enemy_defaults.lua. shoot_cooldown separates bursts. Terrain blocks shots unless aim_line_of_sight=false. aim_rotates_body defaults off; enabling it also rotates the collision body. Shots request ranged_attack, falling back through the skin's available animation states; turning requests aim.

Boss Add Spawner shares the attack dropdown, so it also offers aimed_shot and passes that selection through the enemy spawn service. Mounted shooters placed in Tiled remain editable gameobjects in the room collection.

Tune enemy movement and attacks in Tiled#

  1. Select the placed enemy in Tiled.
  2. Choose enemy_type, the first custom property, for its synced art.
  3. Choose Archetype for its stats and behaviour, or leave none to inherit the matching preset or the configured default for art-only IDs.
  4. Expand movement_and_attacks for per-placement changes. The stats, rewards, and tuning groups hold less-common overrides; -1 numeric defaults inherit the selected archetype rather than replacing it.
  5. Set the movement or attack field for this placement.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The dropdown uses enemy_types.lua keys. The older enemy_templates.lua spawn templates (such as tank and glass_cannon) are a separate API.

To override this enemy's guard:

  1. Select the placed Enemy on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Expand Defense.
  3. Set Guard to front.
  4. Tick heavy_attack in Guard break.
  5. Set Guard hits to 1.
  6. Set Guard open time to 1 second.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  11. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  12. Click Export.
  13. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  14. Strike from the front with a light attack to check the block.
  15. Strike from the front with a heavy attack to open the guard.
  16. Strike from behind to check that the guard allows the hit.

Guard type_default, blank Guard break / Armor requires lists, and -1 numbers inherit the resolved archetype's defense table, then enemy_defaults.defense. An effective empty Guard break list cannot open the guard. Armor hits sets how many accepted hits remove armor for this life; 0 disables it. Armor requires chooses accepted hit tags; an effective empty list accepts any damaging hit. Rear damage mult scales damage from behind. The tag checkbox lists export as one comma-separated string in enum order. Shots use the player's previous-frame world position for guard direction. The knight's Shield guards only its front while raised.

Prepare & Check reports guard_never_breaks as information when the effective guard has no breaking tags. It warns with armor_tag_never_emitted when Armor requires names a tag absent from configured attacks, action contacts and carryable kinds. Correct the tag or its emitter before testing armor. After changes under developer/config, developer/ui or hooks, run Project ▸ Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

The behavior fields are independent per-instance overrides:

  • movement=type_default keeps the resolved archetype's movement.
  • attack_pattern=type_default and attack_pattern2=type_default keep its attacks. Changing only movement no longer removes a preset attack, and changing only an attack no longer stops preset movement.
  • attack_pattern=disabled deliberately removes the inherited attack in that slot while retaining contact damage. If a preset uses two attacks, set both attack slots to disabled. Use stationary when an enemy should stop moving without losing its configured attack.
  • Older exported values none and inherit remain aliases for type_default. Older movement names such as patrol and chase still load, but the explicit choices below make ground/air behavior clearer.

Common movement choices are:

Movement Result
ground_patrol Walks back and forth around its placement point.
ground_patrol_chase Patrols, chases a detected player on the ground, then returns after losing aggro.
ground_chase Chases a detected player horizontally without leaving the ground.
flying_chase Chases in both horizontal and vertical directions.
vertical_patrol Moves up and down around the placement point.
hover Uses the configured aerial hover/patrol range.
flee Moves away from a detected player.
zigzag Moves in a repeating zigzag.
stationary Does not move; configured attacks still run.
ghost_float Uses the ghost's floating follow and close-range rise behavior.

Ground movers stop at drops, and patrollers turn back there. The ledge settings in developer/config/enemy_defaults.lua are measured in tiles of your project's tile size, so they work at any tile size. stop_at_ledges turns the check on. ledge_margin_tiles sets how close the enemy's centre gets to an edge (a quarter tile). ledge_probe_depth_tiles sets how far below the centre it looks for floor; keep it deeper than your tallest enemy's feet. ledge_step_tolerance_tiles lets enemies walk over small steps down (an eighth of a tile). Per enemy type in developer/config/enemy_types.lua, you can set stop_at_ledges, or ledge_margin in pixels for an enemy that is unusually wide or narrow for your tiles. Ground movers never move up or down to follow the floor, so a downhill slope that drops more than the step tolerance within the margin counts as a drop: the enemy stops at the top of it.

Use detection_range to set the aggro radius and chase_speed for the base chase speed in pixels per second. A value of 0 inherits the enemy type's value. A positive chase_speed_mult multiplies the resolved chase speed; 0 inherits the preset multiplier. Keep values proportional to your art scale and test one enemy before applying them to a whole room.

The shipped enemy types are already paired with their intended behavior:

Enemy type Preset movement Preset attack
bat_enemy Ceiling ambush and continuous aerial darts swoop_attack
ghost_enemy ghost_float Contact damage
mummy_enemy Ground chase melee_swipe
tumbleweed_enemy Ground chase dash_attack
cactus_enemy Stationary shoot_circle

After changing enemy properties, export the room again and run Project ▸ Prepare & Check. Validation reports the effective movement and attacks for each placed enemy and warns about conflicting combinations. Treat those reports as the final behavior contract—the .lua export and baked room collection are generated outputs, so do not repair them by hand.

To make a surface crawler:

  1. Place an Enemy beside a floating solid block in Tiled.
  2. Set Archetype to wall_crawler.
  3. Expand Special movement.
  4. Set Cling side to free to attach to the nearest surface.
  5. Set Crawl speed if this placement needs a different speed in pixels per second.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  8. Build the project.
  9. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  10. Play an empty save slot to check each corner, art turn and contact box.

To release an ambusher with a lever:

  1. Place an Enemy above a corridor in Tiled.
  2. Set Archetype to lurker.
  3. Expand Special movement.
  4. Set Ambush style to drop.
  5. Set Ambush trigger id to a unique channel, such as ceiling_release.
  6. Place a Lever outside the enemy's Ambush range.
  7. Set the Lever's Target id to ceiling_release.
  8. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  9. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  10. Build the project.
  11. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  12. Play an empty save slot.
  13. Pull the lever to release the enemy.

Ambush range also releases an ambusher when the player approaches with line of sight. Rise plays an emerge animation; disguise keeps the hidden sprite visible until release. Hidden ambushers cannot attack or take damage. The optional ambush_then pattern in enemy_types.lua defaults to grounded or flying chase. crawl_skin_px in enemy_defaults.lua sets terrain clearance. Crawlers use the ordinary enemy body; test art and collision after corner turns.

Make an enemy a champion#

  1. Select an Enemy on Tiled's top-level templates layer.
  2. Expand Rank.
  3. Tick Champion.
  4. Set Champion name to the name players should see.
  5. Set Champion reward to grant text, such as currency:25,flag:warden_down.
  6. Set Champion music to an imported track, or leave none for existing music.
  7. Set Champion engage range to a distance in world pixels, or leave -1 to inherit.
  8. Set Champion enrage below to 0.5 for an extra attack at half health.
  9. Choose Champion enrage pattern, such as dash_attack.
  10. Expand On defeat.
  11. Set Defeat flag to a stable name, such as warden_defeated, if gates should use it.
  12. Leave Respawn at type_default to keep this champion defeated permanently.
  13. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  14. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  15. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  16. Select Enemies and elements ▸ Champions.
  17. Set Health multiplier to tune champion health across the project.
  18. Choose Save and Prepare & Check.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  20. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  21. Click Export.
  22. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  23. Approach the champion to check the named bar, selected music and room seal.
  24. Defeat it to check its reward and opened doors.
  25. Leave and return to check that it stays defeated.

Champions also offers Damage multiplier for contact damage and Engage range (world px). Its range default is 0, meaning anywhere in the current room once the player clears the doorway; a positive Tiled range overrides it. Health and damage factors stack once with Elite and difficulty. Size also uses enemy_defaults.champion.scale_mult. Hover over settings names or fields for help. Baseline scale, enrage sound and other defaults are commented in developer/config/enemy_defaults.lua.

Champion bar and Champion seal start enabled. Each champion's own Tiled checkboxes decide; these two settings have no project defaults. Remove old champion.seal_room and champion.show_bar fields from enemy_defaults.lua or creator_settings.lua overrides when Prepare & Check warns about them. An unsealed champion heals fully and removes its extra enrage attack when the player leaves. Player death also unlocks the room and resets health without moving the enemy back to its placement. Enrage adds one attack without replacing the original attacks. Boss bars and music take priority when both fights share a room.

The reward is granted directly once and saved with the defeat, even when Save after a permanent defeat is off. An explicit repeatable Respawn choice can bring the fight back and seal again, but never grants its reward again. Blank Champion name uses the translated “Champion” fallback. Prepare & Check notes a blank name, a missing Defeat flag, or a champion sharing a boss room. The generated persist key still keeps a champion with inherited Respawn gone without an authored flag. Analyze Progression treats its defeat flags and reward as requiring a combat playtest.

After config or UI changes, run Project ▸ Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

Swimming, hopping and returning enemies#

To confine an enemy to water:

  1. Place a Water Volume in Tiled.
  2. Place an Enemy point inside the volume.
  3. Set the Enemy's Archetype to swimmer.
  4. Expand Special movement in Properties.
  5. Set Swim speed to the desired pixels per second, or -1 to inherit.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  10. Play an empty save slot.

A swimmer chases only while you share its live Water Volume. It stays inside swim_margin_px from the water's edges. If a lever drains the water away from its position, it idles in place, including when its ledge behaviour is fall. Rising water can cover it again and resume swimming. A dry placement produces a Prepare & Check warning. Water art needs tiles without collision shapes.

To make a hopper:

  1. Place an Enemy on a stepped floor in Tiled.
  2. Set Archetype to hopper.
  3. Expand Special movement.
  4. Set Hop height in pixels, or -1 to inherit.
  5. Set Hop interval in seconds, or -1 to inherit.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  10. Play an empty save slot.

Hoppers wait, prepare, launch toward a detected player, then land. Without a target they hop in their patrol direction. Enemy fall gravity in Game Settings ▸ Enemies and elements ▸ Enemies controls the shared arc. The commented hop_prepare_time and hop_land_time in enemy_defaults.lua set the windup and landing pause.

To give a chaser a post:

  1. Place an Enemy at its home position in Tiled.
  2. Expand Movement and attacks.
  3. Set Movement to ground_chase.
  4. Expand Special movement.
  5. Set Leash distance to a positive radius in pixels.
  6. Set Leash time to the chasing seconds allowed outside that radius.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  11. Play an empty save slot.
  12. Lead the enemy beyond its radius.

It returns to its original post at patrol speed, suppressing attack and hybrid patterns while returning. Within half a tile of home, it waits for leash_cooldown seconds before detecting and attacking again. 0 disables Leash distance; -1 inherits each of the five Special movement numbers from the archetype, then enemy_defaults.lua. Grounded return paths still obey terrain and ledge rules: provide a walkable route home.

Enemies that swarm around a leader#

  1. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold to refresh the enemy dropdowns.
  2. Reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled.
  3. Place three Enemy objects in an open area.
  4. Set each Enemy's Archetype to swarmling.
  5. Expand Stats on each Enemy.
  6. Set each Group id to the same non-empty value.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  11. Play an empty save slot.
  12. Approach the swarm.
  13. Defeat the leader.

The leader chases you while the other members steer toward a ring around it. After the leader falls, any surviving member can lead and the ring forms again. Use a different Group id for each independent swarm. Movement and attacks ▸ Movement can also select swarm on another archetype. A swarm Enemy with no Group id moves alone and produces a swarm_without_group warning during Prepare & Check.

In developer/config/enemy_types.lua, swarm_leader_movement selects the leader's inner movement pattern; the swarmling uses chase_flying. Use a movement pattern, excluding swarm and ambush. The commented swarm_ring_radius, swarm_separation and swarm_cohesion defaults in developer/config/enemy_defaults.lua tune the ring and close-neighbour steering; an archetype can override them. Revival preserves this tuning.

An Encounter Spawn whose Archetype is swarmling automatically shares one swarm with other enemies in that encounter wave. Each wave receives its own Group id, encounter_<encounter id>_w<wave>, so different waves form separate swarms. No additional Group id field is needed on Encounter Spawn.

Enemies with death effects#

  1. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold to refresh the enemy dropdowns.
  2. Reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled.
  3. Place an Enemy.
  4. Expand death_effects.
  5. Set death_behavior to split, burst or hazard.
  6. Set the matching fields described below.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  11. Play an empty save slot.
  12. Defeat the enemy with combat damage.

type_default inherits the archetype's death behaviour, then developer/config/enemy_defaults.lua. none explicitly cancels it. Numeric -1 fields inherit; a count of 0 disables that spawn. The splitter and burster archetypes select split and burst automatically.

For split, choose split_type for synced child art, split_archetype for stats and behaviour, and split_count for attempts. none in either child dropdown inherits its config choice. Children settle onto the floor, use the same room and drop no loot. In enemy_defaults.lua, split_size scales them relative to the parent's body, split_spacing_px spaces them, and split_max_depth limits generations (default 1). A child inherits its parent's non-empty runtime owner: an Encounter wave waits for every child to be defeated. Placed splitters use a separate owner for their children.

For burst, set death_burst_count and death_burst_speed. Shots are evenly spaced around a full circle. These fields are separate from aimed-shot burst_count and burst_interval. Each shot requests its attack clip, then the death clip takes over immediately.

For hazard, choose death_hazard_type and set death_hazard_time to positive seconds. The template defaults to spikes. The zone is centred at the death position and uses the existing hazard damage contact rules. An instant-kill hazard preset still kills on contact. Pause holds its lifetime; after it expires the zone removes itself independently of the corpse. death_hazard_width and death_hazard_height in enemy_defaults.lua or an archetype set its world-pixel dimensions, and the damage area takes exactly that width and height.

Developer kill messages, bottomless-pit deaths and room unloading trigger no death effects. To check an Encounter, select splitter as an Encounter Spawn ▸ Archetype, defeat the parent, then defeat all children before expecting the next wave. The sample-world encounter seals the floor route while running. Split children and shots consume gameobject capacity; children also consume loot-factory capacity. Test dense fights against your project's limits.

Keep Dash, difficulty and first-use hints#

On Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Abilities, Dash unlocks the ground slide-to-run movement. Enable Slide too. Hold the Slide / Air Dash action through the end of a ground slide to continue at Run Speed; release it to return to walking. Air Dash uses that same action while airborne and has its own unlock. The action can be rebound in Settings. Baseline movement tuning lives in developer/config/player_settings.lua, including slide_to_run.

  1. Choose Project ▸ Game Settings… in Defold.
  2. Open the Game tab.
  3. In Difficulty, set Selection mode to new_game to ask players on an empty slot, fixed to use the creator's default, or anytime to also allow changes from the pause menu.
  4. Set Default profile to the initially selected profile.
  5. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  7. Choose Play Game from the title screen.
  8. Choose an empty save slot.
  9. If prompted, choose a difficulty to start the game, or go Back to cancel without creating a save.

Each slot keeps its choice. In anytime mode, the pause menu's Difficulty row cycles profiles and saves only that choice; it does not save items or other progress since the last save. Normal save triggers, including Quit, still save progress. Profile changes affect the next hit immediately, and health for enemies and bosses spawned afterwards. Fixed mode always uses the configured default, including when loading an existing slot.

Profile labels, descriptions, list order and multipliers live in developer/config/difficulty_settings.lua. Multipliers use the 0.1-10 range; normal uses 1.0 throughout. Adding a profile also requires its key in order for player selection. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check to add the profile to the Enemy difficulty dropdown, then reopen the project in Tiled.

  • damage_taken_mult scales every hit the player takes, including hazards and drowning, except instant kills.
  • damage_dealt_mult scales every hit enemies and bosses take.
  • enemy_health_mult scales enemy health when it spawns.
  • boss_health_mult scales boss health when it spawns; if omitted, it uses enemy_health_mult. Boss phase thresholds keep the same health fractions.
  • loot_mult scales currency from defeated enemies and bosses, rounded once per drop; other pickups and item counts stay unchanged.
  • A placed enemy's Difficulty Override stacks underneath the chosen profile: a hard enemy on Hard has 1.25 × 1.25 health. Empty inherits its authored defaults. This per-enemy override is authored in Tiled separately from the game's profile. Health applies when a foe spawns.

Edit developer/config/tutorial_popups.lua for first-use popups. The first_jump, first_dash and opened_map events run on an accepted Jump, Slide / Air Dash or World Map press. Each popup's trigger_flag prevents repeat display and is saved with normal story flags. Use title and body for source wording, or explicit title_key / body_key matching keys in developer/config/ui_text.lua for translations; missing keys fall back to the source text. Mention actions rather than fixed keys so the hint still makes sense after rebinding.

Add a collectible, key, and chest#

  • Place collectible; choose collectible_type and set amount.
  • Place key; choose a key_id such as the key used by your door/gate.
  • Place chest; give it a unique chest_id, set contents such as currency:25, and optionally set locked plus requires_key. Locked chests open only with Interact, never by walking past. In Access, the Use up key checkbox (consume_key) defaults on; untick it to keep the key after opening. Unlocked chests can still open on touch.

Prepare & Check bakes every placed chest into an editable gameobject in the room collection.

Make a loot table#

  1. Open Project ▸ Loot Tables… in Defold with the game stopped.
  2. Enter a unique id in New table id.
  3. Click New table.
  4. Click Add item.
  5. Choose a Type (the label includes its category).
  6. Set Amount to a whole number of at least 1.
  7. Set Chance between 0 (never) and 1 (always).
  8. Repeat Add item for each additional drop.
  9. Click Save.
  10. In Tiled, set a chest's contents or an enemy's On defeat ▸ Loot table to the table id.
  11. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. Test the drops on an empty save slot.

Move up, Move down and Remove arrange items. Empty tables are allowed. Errors disable Save; repeated types warn because bosses keep one drop per type. Save and Prepare & Check saves and prepares in one action. Close asks before discarding unsaved changes. Saves write developer/config/loot_tables_authored.lua; baseline loot_tables.lua stays intact. Editing a baseline creates an override. Select it and click Revert to loot_tables.lua, then Save, to restore the baseline. Delete removes an authored-only table. Advanced drop_limit, collectible_type and other unknown fields are preserved without editor controls.

Show a completion percentage#

  1. Choose Project ▸ Game Settings… in Defold.
  2. Click Progression.
  3. Tick Completion ▸ Enabled.
  4. Tick Show in Journal to display the breakdown in Records.
  5. Tick Show on ending to add the percentage to the ending's first page.
  6. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  8. Choose Play Game.
  9. Start an empty save slot.
  10. Collect an upgrade.
  11. Open Journal ▸ Records to compare found counts with the published world.

The percentage gives equal weight to upgrades, bosses and visited rooms by default. Upgrades include ability, fragment, weapon and charm pickups. Only published rooms count, including rooms the player has yet to load; revealing a room on the map does not count as visiting it. Respawning pickups and per-type opt-outs stay excluded. A category with no placed entries adds no weight.

To tune weights, edit the commented developer/config/completion_tracking.lua. Set a category's weight to zero to keep its Records row without affecting the percentage. Added collectible categories use a creator label; add them to world_census.lua too. Run Prepare & Check and Build after editing. Completion ships off and adds no save data of its own.

The optional Trials category counts cleared Combat Trials in published rooms. Its weight is zero by default, so it appears in the completion breakdown without changing the percentage. Set categories.trials.weight in completion_tracking.lua to a positive value to include it. The separate Records ▸ Trials section shows each trial's best result.

Show relics and lore in the Journal#

  1. In developer/config/collectible_types.lua, choose a type whose category is relic and whose show_in_collection is true (omitting that field also includes it).
  2. In Tiled, place a collectible on the room's top-level templates layer.
  3. Set its collectible_type to the chosen relic type.
  4. Place a Lore Item on the same layer.
  5. Give it a non-blank, unique lore_id so reading it can be saved.
  6. Set its title.
  7. Set its text.
  8. Set its category to group related entries in the Journal.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  10. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check to refresh the world census.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  13. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  14. Place the room in the layout if it is new.
  15. Click Export to publish the layout.
  16. Choose Play Game.
  17. Start an empty save slot.
  18. Collect the relic.
  19. Read the Lore Item.
  20. Open Pause ▸ Journal ▸ Relics to check its name and found count; unfound placements show ???.
  21. Open the Lore tab.
  22. Select a found entry.
  23. Confirm to open its saved text in the reader.
  24. Close the reader.
  25. Open Records to check play time, collection counts and currency.

Only rooms in the exported layout count, including rooms that are not currently loaded. Relics are ordered by the type's collection_number, then name; their type's description appears in the detail panel. Lore collectibles (types with category = "lore") also count, using their placed or type lore_id for saved text. Unplaced lore granted by other routes adds no row or total. Records is shown by default; books.journal.show_records in developer/config/menu_settings.lua controls that tab.

Make a quest#

  1. Open developer/config/quests.lua.
  2. Add a quest under quests with a stable id, source title, description, optional category and giver display text.
  3. Set start.mode to auto, flag, dialogue or trigger; a flag start also needs start.flag.
  4. Add a non-empty steps list with stable step ids, source text and one complete_when objective per step.
  5. Set optional rewards to grant text such as currency:100 or a list of grant strings.
  6. Save the file.
  7. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  8. Select Progression.
  9. Under Quests, tick Quests.
  10. Set Quest notices to choose whether quest changes show notices.
  11. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Objectives support {flag="found_relic"}, {item="potion", count=2}, {event="enemy_defeated", where={species="bat"}, count=3}, {trigger=true}, {quest="another_quest"}, {level=2} or a final {turn_in=true}. Event names and filters use the table below. Event objectives count only while that step is current; temporary gate openings never count. Items are checked, not consumed. Optional step requires_flag delays completion; set_flag records its completion. A final turn-in objective leaves the quest completed until an explicit turn-in pays its rewards once. Completed quests count toward achievement goals before turn-in. Pause ▸ Journal ▸ Quests lists Active and Completed quests, with the current step beside each title. Completed and turned-in quests, and failed quests marked Quest failed, appear under Completed. An unstarted quest appears under Active only when hidden_until_started is false. Details show the description, the giver for a pending turn-in, and the reward. Confirm opens the description in the scrollable reader. Categories sort by their configured order, then category id; titles and quest ids break ties within each category.

Quest notices shows started, updated, completed and failed notices. Newer queued notices for the same quest replace older ones. Journal ▸ Records ▸ Statistics includes Quests completed when Progression ▸ Records ▸ Statistics in Records is on.

To connect an NPC dialogue after configuring the quest:

  1. Open Project ▸ Dialogue Editor… in Defold.
  2. Select the NPC's sequence in Sequence.
  3. Click Edit… beside the accept line.
  4. Set the accept choice's Start quest to your quest with start.mode="dialogue".
  5. Click Use line.
  6. Click Edit… beside the line that finishes the objective.
  7. Set Advance quest to the quest and step id.
  8. Click Use line.
  9. Click Edit… beside the return conversation's reward line.
  10. Set Turn in quest to your quest.
  11. Set Condition to quest state.
  12. Select your quest in Quest.
  13. Select completed in State.
  14. Click Use line.
  15. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The Lua reference remains developer/config/narrative/dialogues.lua: use quest_start="your_quest", quest_advance="your_quest.step_id" and quest_turn_in="your_quest" on lines or choices. Window saves write whole-sequence overrides to developer/config/narrative/dialogues_authored.lua.

condition={quest="your_quest", state="active"} checks one exact state: not_started, active, completed, turned_in or failed; omitted state means active. condition={quest_step="your_quest.step_id"} matches the current named step while active or completed awaiting turn-in. Unknown ids and disabled quests do not match. invert=true reverses either check. Conditions filter lines; choices run their actions only when selected. quest_advance="your_quest" advances the current step without a name check. quest_fail="your_quest" fails a quest. Flag actions run first, followed by start, advance, turn-in and fail; repeated turn-in never pays twice. All quest actions are inert while Quests is off. Use NPC Appear when and Remove when flags to move a quest giver between placed NPCs rather than creating one from a script.

To use a zone after configuring the quest:

  1. Open your room in Tiled.
  2. Select the top-level templates layer.
  3. Place Logic ▸ Quest Trigger.
  4. Resize its rectangle to cover the activation area.
  5. Set Quest id to the quest's stable id from developer/config/quests.lua.
  6. Under Quest, choose Action: start, advance, complete or fail.
  7. Set Step id when Advance must match a specific step.
  8. Under Conditions and outcomes, set Requires quest when another quest must have a chosen state.
  9. Choose Requires quest state; any skips the state check.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  12. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Playback ▸ Auto play fires on entry; untick it to require Interact inside the rectangle. Lifecycle controls one-shot, persistent once flags and consumption. Blocked actions and quests switched off do not consume the zone or set its flag. Conditions and outcomes ▸ Set flag runs only when the quest action changes state. An empty Step id advances the current objective. Complete marks the quest completed; rewards still wait for an explicit turn-in. For a start zone, use start.mode="trigger"; the shipped example intentionally starts from a flag. Unknown quest ids stop Prepare & Check with the room and object id.

The shipped example starts from desert_hermit_quest_given and completes its find objective from desert_hermit_relic_found, matching the existing dialogue. Its shipped dialogue data keeps those flag branches. To test turn-in, add quest_turn_in="desert_hermit_find_relic" to a line in desert_hermit_complete. The example Quest Trigger can advance the find objective without setting the relic flag. For that test, change the first desert_hermit_talk line's condition to {quest="desert_hermit_find_relic", state="completed"} so it reaches the return conversation after the trigger. Keep giver names as display text, never NPC ids. Title, description, giver, step text and category names are collected by Project ▸ Translations….

Track the player's current goal#

  1. Open developer/config/goals.lua.
  2. Add goals under goals, in order, each with a stable id, a source title, optional text and a non-empty done_when.
  3. Save the file.
  4. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  5. Select Progression.
  6. Under Goals, tick Goals.
  7. Select HUD and prompts.
  8. Tick Show on screen ▸ Goal to show the current goal's title on the HUD.
  9. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  11. Play an empty save slot.

The first goal whose done_when is not yet met becomes current. Each new goal shows a New goal notice with its title. Pause ▸ Journal ▸ Goals, the Journal's first tab, lists the current goal under Current and earlier goals under Finished; later goals stay hidden, and details show the goal's text. The tab appears only while Goals is on and the list is not empty. The HUD line sits below the shared HUD column, shortens a long title to one line and hides after the last goal. Progress only moves forward, so spending an item an earlier goal needed never brings it back. Title and text are collected by Project ▸ Translations….

Point the player at the current goal with a Hint Stone#

  1. Open developer/config/goals.lua.
  2. Give the goal a source hint.
  3. Give the goal a target: {objective = "dash_shrine"} or {marker = "old_camp"}.
  4. Save the file.
  5. In Tiled, select the object the player should find.
  6. Set its progression_objective to the target's objective id (for a marker target, use a Map Marker with that marker_id).
  7. Place a Hint Stone template.
  8. Under Goal hints, tick Show current goal.
  9. Tick Reveal goal target to mark the target on the world map.
  10. Set Goal hint cost to a currency price, or leave it at 0 for a free hint.
  11. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  12. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. Play an empty save slot.

The stone shows the current goal's hint in its popup. It shows its own body when Goals is off, when no goal is current, or when the goal has no hint. A price shows on the stone's prompt and is charged once per goal: reading the same goal's hint again is free, and the next goal has its own price. With too little currency, the popup says what the hint costs and nothing is charged. Reveal goal target also needs Game Settings ▸ Progression ▸ Goals ▸ Hints reveal targets (on by default). The world map then marks the target at every zoom, even with item icons off, and the legend calls it Goal; change its shape or colour in developer/config/map_icons.lua under kinds.goal_target. Requires flag, Set flag, the sound and Trigger once keep working. Prepare & Check reports goal_target_missing when no visible object in a placed room carries a goal's target id, and Analyze Progression warns goal_target_blocked when its objective is not reachable in the model.

Time a course with Countdown Zones#

A countdown runs one clock across rooms. Countdown Zones start, finish or cancel it where the player walks. Countdowns themselves live in developer/config/countdowns.lua; the shipped sample_course gives 90 seconds and restarts at its start zone when time runs out.

  1. Open developer/config/countdowns.lua.
  2. Add an entry under countdowns with a stable id, a label, a duration in seconds and an on_timeout of death, restart or return_safe.
  3. Save the file.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  5. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check to add the new id to the Tiled dropdown.
  6. Close and reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled.
  7. Open the room where the run starts.
  8. Select the top-level templates layer.
  9. Place Logic ▸ Countdown Zone on the start line.
  10. Choose your countdown in Countdown id.
  11. Leave Role set to start.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. Open the room that holds the finish line.
  14. Select the top-level templates layer.
  15. Place another Logic ▸ Countdown Zone on the finish line.
  16. Choose the same countdown in Countdown id.
  17. Set Role to finish.
  18. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  20. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  21. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  22. Play an empty save slot.

Entering a start zone starts its countdown; entering it again during the run changes nothing. A finish zone ends the running countdown with its time, its best time in Journal ▸ Records and any once-only reward. A cancel zone ends it with no result. Finish and cancel zones act only on the countdown they name, and only while it runs. One countdown runs at a time. A restart timeout returns the player to the start zone, standing at its bottom centre. A countdown with start_when starts without any zone; give it a finish zone or let it run out. Zones fire every time the player enters, so a repeatable course can be run again. Saves are refused during a run while block_saves is on. While block_travel is on (the default), a run refuses fast travel from stations and save points, hides the pause menu's travel entry, and refuses Warp Statues, which show their Toast blocked text.

To start an escape from a story event instead of a zone:

  1. Choose the flag the event sets, such as a boss defeat flag, a lever's set_flag_on or a dialogue set_flag.
  2. Open developer/config/countdowns.lua.
  3. Set the countdown's start_when to that flag.
  4. Save the file.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  6. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build.

While a countdown runs, the flag countdown_<id>_active is set: use it in appear_when or remove_when to change the world during the run.

Project ▸ Prepare & Check reports countdown mistakes: countdown_zone_blank (a zone names no countdown), countdown_without_finish (a countdown can start but no finish zone names it), countdown_unused (nothing starts it; information only), countdown_reward_invalid, countdown_unknown_music and countdown_return_safe_kills (return_safe while hazard returns are off, which kills instead). A zone that names a countdown missing from countdowns.lua is reported as catalog_value_unknown. Project ▸ Analyze Progression counts a finish zone's finish flag and reward as possible but unproven, because beating the clock needs a playtest.

Add achievements#

  1. Open developer/config/achievements.lua.
  2. Add an achievements entry under a stable id, using the example below.
  3. Save the file.
  4. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Game Settings….
  6. Select Progression.
  7. Under Achievements, tick Achievements.
  8. Keep Unlock notices ticked to show unlock feedback.
  9. Keep Show hidden count ticked to include locked hidden goals in the header.
  10. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Choose Play Game.
  13. Start an empty save slot.
  14. Meet the achievement's trigger, such as defeating three placed enemies.
  15. Open the pause menu.
  16. Choose Journal.
  17. Select Achievements to check its name, description and unlocked status.
hunter = {
    name = "First Victories", description = "Defeat three enemies.",
    order = 10, hidden = false,
    trigger = {type = "event", event = "enemy_defeated", where = {spawned = false}, count = 3},
    reward = "currency:10",
},

Place this entry inside the config's achievements = { ... } table. Optional reward uses the same grant text as other rewards. It goes once to the save slot active at unlock time; other slots inherit the unlock without that reward. Unlocks and event counts belong to the game's player profile, so deleting a slot keeps them. Project ▸ Reset Project to Empty clears the achievement content and schedules profile deletion on the next game launch. Dev Menu test sessions and boss practice do not count or unlock achievements. Turning Achievements off stops tracking and hides the tab; it does not erase the profile.

Use the optional Steam add-on: it ships off. For Steam achievement mapping and setup through Project ▸ Steam Add-on…, follow the Steam add-on guide.

Set categories to a map such as exploration = {name = "Exploration", order = 10} and an entry's category = "exploration" to group it. Categories sort by order then category id; entries sort by order, source name and stable id. Uncategorized entries follow the categories. Names and descriptions are source text collected by Project ▸ Translations…; ids are internal identities and never labels. A missing name shows the missing-name UI text. Keep ids stable to retain unlocks.

The header counts unlocked goals against all configured goals. Show hidden count adds the number still hidden, including zero; locked hidden = true rows show ??? without description or progress. Once unlocked, they show their source name and description. Visible event goals show their saved count and target, such as 2/3; state goals show their description. Completed rows show Achievement unlocked in the list's completed colour. With Unlock notices off, the configured sfx still plays; leave it blank for silence. The list can be empty when there are no configured goals.

Trigger type Required fields What reaches the goal
event event, positive whole count; optional where That many matching events across save slots; each where field is an exact scalar equality filter.
flag flag The current slot has the flag.
item item, positive whole count The current slot holds at least that many of the item.
currency Positive whole amount Carried currency reaches the amount.
level Positive whole level The player reaches the level while leveling is enabled.
quests_completed Positive integer count The active save slot has completed this many quests; turn-in is not required.
ending ending_id; optional finite non-negative max_playtime_s The active slot has completed the configured enabled ending, with RunStats time at most the optional seconds limit. Omit the limit for any time.
all_of Non-empty ids list All named achievements are unlocked; references must exist and must not form cycles.

State goals are checked at session start and after gameplay events; item and currency goals measure current holdings, not lifetime gains. Event counters stop at their target after unlocking. gate_opened ignores temporary = true by default; set where = {temporary = true} to count those openings explicitly. Prepare & Check reports an unknown event or filter field with the achievement id.

Achievement trigger events#

Use the event name in trigger.event. Only the fields below may appear in trigger.where; values must be strings, numbers or booleans. Fields are optional in an emitted payload, so a filter matches only when its field is present with the specified value. These are the declared gameplay events, not Defold messages.

Event Filter fields When it happens
quest_started quest_id After a quest starts and its flag mirror is set.
quest_step quest_id, step_id, step After a step finishes; step is the next one-based index.
quest_completed quest_id After objectives finish; rewards wait for turn-in.
quest_turned_in quest_id After rewards and state commit in one save batch.
player_damaged amount, damage_type, over_time, lethal After accepted positive damage changes health, before statuses and death.
trial_started trial_id, room_id, goal When a trial is armed and sealed.
trial_completed trial_id, room_id, goal, rank, metric, first_clear, new_best After the result and first-clear reward inside the optional save batch.
trial_failed trial_id, room_id, reason On safe failure, or an abort while playing; reason is time, hit, defeated, incomplete or left.
countdown_started countdown_id, source When a countdown starts; source is zone, condition, restart or script. A restart timeout starts it again with source restart.
countdown_finished countdown_id, tenths, new_best, first_clear When a countdown is finished, after its reward, finish flag and best time; tenths is the time taken in tenths of a second.
countdown_failed countdown_id, reason When a countdown ends without finishing; reason is time, death, cancelled or left.
completion_changed percent, previous When the rounded weighted completion percentage changes during play.
quest_failed quest_id After a quest fails and its flag mirror is set.
goal_changed goal_id, previous_id When a new goal becomes current; goal_id is empty after the last goal.
region_entered region_id, room_id, first_visit When the player enters a room in another region, or the first room after loading; first_visit is true the first time in this save.
npc_met npc_id, npc_type, room_id After the first conversation with a placed NPC in this save ends; npc_type is empty for an NPC with no type.
achievement_unlocked achievement_id, category When a goal unlocks and its profile record is saved.
xp_gained amount, source, total On a positive XP gain.
level_up level, previous Once for each level gained.
item_sold item_id, count, price, station_kind After an item is sold.
purchase_completed station_kind, catalog, entry, price After a purchase succeeds.
encounter_cleared encounter_id, room_id, repeatable, clear_count When an Encounter is cleared.
enemy_damaged amount, by_player, boss, defeated, over_time After an enemy or boss takes accepted damage.
player_parried perfect When the player successfully parries.
status_applied status, target, source When a new status starts; refreshing or stacking it does not count.
hazard_return room_id, lethal When a pending hazard return takes effect.
reward_granted source, reason, entry_count, once_key After a reward is granted in full.
enemy_defeated enemy_type, archetype, skin, species, elite, room_id, spawn_id, defeat_key, spawned, spawn_owner, group_id, respawn, x, y, cause, source_attack, damage_type, xp_value, in_bestiary When an enemy is defeated, after its drops.
boss_defeated boss_id, preset, room_id, x, y, xp After a boss defeat is recorded.
collectible_collected collectible_id, collectible_type, category, room_id, respawns After a collectible is collected successfully.
item_added item_id, count, total When an item's stored count increases.
item_removed item_id, count, total After removal; count is the amount actually removed.
currency_changed delta, total When carried currency changes.
ability_unlocked ability_id, source When a new ability is unlocked.
secret_found secret_id, room_id When a secret is found for the first time.
gate_opened gate_id, source, temporary When a gate opens; temporary identifies reversible openings.
chest_opened chest_id, room_id When a chest opens.
player_died reason When the player dies.
room_visited room_id On the first visit to a room.
flag_set flag, value When a flag's value changes; value is included only for strings, numbers or booleans.
ending_reached ending_id After reaching an ending and saving.

For example, enemy_defeated with where = {spawned = false} counts placed enemies, while where = {species = "bat"} counts that authored species. enemy_damaged.amount is accepted damage, not an enemy defeat count. Repeated flag_set events count value changes, and room_visited counts first visits. The price field on purchase and sale events is the currency part, not the item cost; count on item_sold is the number sold. An event that is not listed is not available as a trigger. New event declarations must add their fields to this table in the same change.

Add a bestiary#

  1. Open developer/config/bestiary.lua.
  2. Add an entries row keyed by the species, with a source name and optional description.
  3. In Tiled, select an Enemy on the top-level templates layer.
  4. Expand On defeat.
  5. Set species to that row's key, or leave it blank to use the art-first identity.
  6. Keep in_bestiary ticked to record its defeats.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Game Settings….
  9. Select Progression.
  10. Tick Bestiary to show its Journal tab and first-entry notices.
  11. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  13. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  14. Click Export.
  15. Choose Play Game.
  16. Start an empty save slot.
  17. Defeat the enemy to see its first-entry notice.
  18. Rest at a save point to save its count.
  19. Quit to the title screen.
  20. Choose Continue.
  21. Open the pause menu.
  22. Choose Journal.
  23. Select Bestiary to check the entry's name, idle picture and Defeated count.

The header shows Recorded N of M for the published world's distinct species and included boss presets. Known spawned species join that total when include_spawned is true. Records outside the current world remain listed but do not increase its total; a world without an enemy census shows only recorded entries and no total. Unrecorded species show ???; set an entry's hidden_until_defeated to hide it until its first defeat.

Set categories labels in display order and each entry's category and order to group and sort the list; ties sort by source name. Give bosses rows a name and optional description, keyed by boss preset. Bosses have no picture. Synced enemy skins need an idle animation for a portrait. Names always come from config; an absent name uses the missing-name UI text.

Descriptions appear after the first defeat. details_after sets the defeat count that reveals archetype weaknesses, resistances, immunities and possible drops. These details use the last recorded enemy art and archetype, without per-instance overrides. Drop labels use each collectible type's name from collectible_types.lua (or the items.lua name) and the translated currency label; loot-table IDs are never shown. unknown_text supplies optional source text for unrecorded entries.

Recording continues while Bestiary is off. include_spawned and include_bosses control spawned enemies and boss presets; notify_new_entry controls first-entry feedback. Counts use species IDs and boss presets, while names remain creator source text for translation. A first defeat without a configured name uses the missing-name UI text. The Dev Menu's Saves page lists the bestiary provider. Reset Project to Empty restores empty bestiary content.

Split health and energy upgrades into fragments#

  1. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Select Player.
  3. Under Health and resources, set Health fragments per container (default 4).
  4. Set Energy fragments per container if needed (default 3).
  5. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  6. In Tiled, choose File ▸ Open File or Project... to reopen MVTK.tiled-project.
  7. Place a Collectible on the room's top-level templates layer.
  8. Set its Collectible type to health_fragment or energy_fragment.
  9. Clear Respawns for permanent pieces.
  10. Place enough pieces to complete a container.
  11. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  12. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  15. If the room is new, open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  16. Place the new room in the layout.
  17. Click Export if the layout changed.
  18. Choose Play Game.
  19. Start an empty save slot.
  20. Collect three health pieces to see Health fragment 3/4 with the default count.
  21. Save at a save point.
  22. Load the slot again.
  23. Collect the fourth piece to see Max health +1 and the immediate health increase.
  24. Open Pause ▸ Journal ▸ Records to find Fragments.

Each piece keeps its placed identity across saves. Completed containers never grant again on reload; changing the piece count applies on the next pickup and keeps previous increases. developer/config/fragments.lua holds commented set definitions, upgrade amounts and translatable notice templates. Custom sets can increase stamina or flask charges; the optional flask example ships commented out. Run Prepare & Check before Export Game after configuration edits. Art initially reuses the matching container image; a placed Collectible can use creator artwork.

Add an ability pickup and gate#

To make an Intangible Barrier crossing:

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place an Ability Pickup before the crossing.
  3. Set Ability ID (ability_id) to intangible_dash.
  4. Place another Ability Pickup before the crossing if the player needs Air Dash here.
  5. Set that pickup's Ability ID (ability_id) to air_dash.
  6. Place a Gate at the crossing.
  7. Set Gate ID (gate_id) to a unique id.
  8. Set Gate type to intangible_barrier.
  9. Resize the Gate to cover the passage with its authored art.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. In Layout Editor, click Export after changing rooms or their placement.
  15. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  16. Collect Air Dash first if it is not already owned.
  17. Try dashing into the barrier before collecting Intangible Dash.
  18. Check that its map icon is locked.
  19. Collect Intangible Dash.
  20. Dash through from each side, including from point-blank range.
  21. End a dash inside the barrier and walk out.
  22. Take an enemy projectile during the dash.
  23. Leave until the room unloads.
  24. Return and check that the barrier is solid while idle.
  25. Check that its map icon is accessible.

The barrier stays visible and never unlocks permanently. It permits passage while Intangible Dash is active, then lets an overlapping player walk out. Its map icon means accessible once the required upgrades are owned; it never means permanently open. Keep its width within the scaled Air Dash speed × duration; in eight-direction mode Prepare & Check also checks its height. The shipped distance is about 567 pixels at Player Size 1. Configure during_slide=true in developer/config/player_settings.lua to also permit ground slides with Slide and Intangible Dash. If during_air_dash=false, only enabled slide passage remains; with both modes off the barrier cannot be crossed. The invulnerable switch grants ordinary damage immunity during passage; lethal hits bypass it. Dash Invincibility becomes redundant during air dashes once this upgrade is owned with that switch on. Use Tiled art or the Gate's Use Defold sprite option for its visible appearance.

To make an ordinary ability gate that opens after a requirement is met:

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place the ability_pickup template before the crossing.
  3. Set ability_id to the ability it grants.
  4. Place the gate template at the crossing.
  5. Set gate_id to a unique id.
  6. Set Gate type to ability_requirement for this physical list-only example, or ability_marker for an invisible annotation.
  7. In Requirements, set Required ability to none for this example.
  8. In Required abilities, tick double_jump for this example.
  9. Tick air_dash.
  10. Set Requirement logic to any to accept either ability, or all to require every selected ability.
  11. Leave persist_unlock=true when the gate should remain open after success.
  12. In State and persistence, set Group id (group_id) to a shared text id if several gates should open together.
  13. Set the same Group id on each other Gate in that set, including gates in other rooms.
  14. Leave Persist unlock enabled on those gates to save the shared opening.
  15. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room for each changed room.
  16. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  18. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Analyze Progression.

Required ability and Required abilities form one set; repeated ids count once. A blank or none singular field retains the Gate type's default: dash_barrier, for example, still includes air_dash. Use ability_requirement or ability_marker for a list-only gate, since their singular defaults are empty. An empty combined set needs no ability. Keep the checkbox list empty for a single-ability gate. For any, test fresh save states with neither ability, only Double Jump, and only Air Dash. Existing single-ability gates keep their behavior.

A persistent opening opens every active Gate with the same group_id and remembers the group for rooms loaded later and saved games. Temporary openings do not open the group. Leave group_id blank for independent gates. After publishing a game, keep each group id stable so saves retain that opening. Playtest two members in different rooms: open one, walk to the other, save, load the slot and check both gates and their map icons show open.

An ability_marker draws no sprite, blocks no movement and has no map icon. Keep its Tiled object visible; room geometry enforces the crossing. Analyze Progression models height, hazard and destructible gates, action hit tags, breakable-wall hit requirements and hazard resistances. It still needs a playtest for movement and combat. For item or action gates, choose the matching Required key, Required item or Required action field instead. For a split room, follow the progression annotation steps to declare its areas and the marker's progression_from / progression_to. Give the pickup a progression_area on the entrance side of the crossing.

For a simple physical locked door, use locked_door instead. Set a unique door_id and one of require_ability, require_key, or require_item.

Gate a tall ledge with High Jump#

  1. In Tiled, paint a ledge above the normal jump height and within 1.5 times that height.
  2. Select the top-level templates layer.
  3. Place an Ability Pickup before the ledge.
  4. Set its Ability ID (ability_id) to high_jump.
  5. Place a Gate at the ledge crossing.
  6. Set Gate ID (gate_id) to a unique id.
  7. Set Gate type to ability_marker.
  8. In Requirements, set Required ability to high_jump.
  9. Follow the progression annotation steps to describe the crossing's areas.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  12. In Abilities ▸ Movement, leave High jump unchecked to require the pickup.
  13. In Advanced ▸ High Jump, set High jump height (x normal) to 1.5.
  14. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  15. Build.
  16. In Layout Editor, click Export after placing or changing the room.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Analyze Progression to check the published crossing.
  18. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  19. Compare the jump height before collecting the pickup.
  20. Collect High Jump.
  21. Jump onto the ledge.
  22. Save at a save point.
  23. Load the saved slot to check the upgrade remains unlocked.

The marker annotates the crossing for Analyze Progression; the ledge geometry enforces it in play. High Jump raises ground and coyote jumps immediately, including buffered jumps. Air jumps retain their height by default; wall, ladder, swim and grapple hang jumps retain their normal launch. Player Size scales both normal and upgraded heights. Extended Jump's held-jump bonus stacks on the upgraded launch, so playtest the combined reach when both are available. Tick High jump in Abilities ▸ Movement to start new games with it instead.

Make wind and updrafts#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Navigation ▸ Wind Current.
  3. Resize its rectangle to cover the affected area.
  4. Set current_id to a unique id for switch links.
  5. Set direction to left or right for sideways push.
  6. Expand force.
  7. Set wind_mode to push.
  8. Set push_speed to the desired drift speed in pixels per second.
  9. For an updraft shaft, change wind_mode to lift.
  10. Set direction to up for an updraft or down for a downdraft.
  11. Set lift_gravity_multiple above 1 to lift against gravity.
  12. Set max_rise_speed to the upward speed cap in pixels per second.
  13. Expand timing.
  14. Set on_seconds to the blowing duration for pulsing gusts.
  15. Set off_seconds to the quiet duration, or leave either duration at 0 for continuous wind.
  16. Set start_delay to the seconds before the first gust.
  17. Place a Lever if this current should be switchable.
  18. Set the lever's target_ref to this Wind Current using the object picker.
  19. For separate artwork, set the art object's owner_ref to this current.
  20. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  21. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  22. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  23. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  24. If this room is new to the layout, open Layout Editor on the title screen.
  25. Click Export after placing the room.
  26. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.

Push is separate from player steering and does not play a run animation while standing still. Lift uses effective gravity even with Jump released or Glide; a jump already faster than the cap keeps its speed. Gust timing freezes while paused. A saved switch channel is replayed when the current's room loads, and linked art is visible only while the current blows. Wind affects the player only.

Make a shortcut#

  1. In Tiled, place a Lever template in the top-level templates layer.
  2. Open the lever's conditions_and_flags properties.
  3. Tick Persist state (persist_state).
  4. Place a Timed Door template.
  5. Give the door a unique door_id.
  6. Open the door's behavior_and_timing properties.
  7. Tick Stays open (stays_open) for a shortcut that never closes.
  8. Select the lever.
  9. Set its target_ref object property to the door.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Build.
  14. Choose Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  15. Activate the lever.
  16. Leave the room.
  17. Return to the room to check the shortcut stays open.
  18. Save at a save point.
  19. Load the saved slot to check the lever and door restore.

Use Pressure Plate ▸ response ▸ Persist state for a plate that stays pressed. A Moving Platform can be selected through a switch's target_ref; preparation supplies a blank trigger_id, and that platform waits for the switch. Matching text target_id and trigger_id fields also work. Stored channels let an unloaded room restore the same state. Prepare & Check warns when receivers in several rooms share a saved channel; use unique IDs unless that link is intentional.

Without Persist state, toggle levers retain their legacy Set flag on restore (the flag means "ever switched on"). Momentary levers set that flag when fired but load off. Persist state is ignored on momentary and elevator-call levers. Keep it off for switch-order puzzles, whose partial progress resets on reload. Doors that start open load silently. A persistent signal holds a timed door open without a timer; Stays open makes every opening permanent. A death reload restores switch Flags from the last save. Export the Layout only when adding a new room.

Add a strike switch#

  1. Place interaction/strike_switch.tx on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Set switch_id to a unique ID in this room.
  3. Set target_ref to the door, switch gate, platform or hazard it controls.
  4. Choose behavior ▸ mode: toggle, once, timed or momentary.
  5. Choose one behavior ▸ accepts value for the required hit kind.
  6. Set behavior ▸ timer_seconds for a timed switch.
  7. Enable behavior ▸ solid when the rectangle should support the player.
  8. Enable conditions ▸ persist_state for saved toggle or once switches.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.

any accepts sword, shot, down strike, slam, bomb and movement contacts. Dashing, sliding or slamming through an any switch operates it. movement accepts Dash, Slide, enabled Backdash contact and Super Jump; bomb accepts the player's Bomb. melee also includes Down Strike. Down Strike bounces on a hit switch through the normal weapon hitbox. An unaccepted hit plays rejection feedback without changing state. feedback ▸ sound_reject and tick_sound override the defaults in developer/config/switches.lua; blank uses the config. behavior ▸ cooldown of -1 uses that config's default_cooldown.

Timed switches count down only while gameplay is unpaused. Momentary switches send OFF after the configured pulse, waiting out menu pause. Neither mode saves its state. For a timed door controlled entirely by switch ON/OFF, turn behavior_and_timing ▸ auto_close off on the Timed Door. Floating switches allow shots through gaps and do not block enemy line of sight, but absorb enemy shots; behavior ▸ accept_enemy_hits is off by default. Shot overlap with a floating switch remains unverified on Box2D v3 until tested in the game.

Switch gates#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Gate from tiled/templates/interaction/gate.tx.
  3. Set gate_type to switch_gate.
  4. Set gate_id to a unique, stable channel id, such as shortcut_gate.
  5. In state_and_persistence, enable stays_open for a saved opening, or disable it to follow the switch's on/off state.
  6. Leave persist_unlock enabled for saved openings.
  7. Place a Lever or Pressure Plate.
  8. Set its target_id to the gate's gate_id, or select the gate with target_ref in the same room.
  9. Enable the sender's persist_state for a shortcut that restores when the gate's room loads later.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room for each changed room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. Open Layout Editor from the title screen if rooms or connections changed.
  15. Click Export.
  16. Choose Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  17. Activate the switch while the gate's neighbouring room is unloaded.
  18. Enter that room to check the restored opening.
  19. Turn the switch off to check that stays_open off closes the gate again.

A saved opening writes gate_<gate_id> and propagates to the gate's group_id. A temporary opening never propagates to a group. Prepare & Check warns when no switch targets a switch gate. Intangible Barrier ignores switch messages; staged_gate counts flags instead.

One-sided breakable walls#

  1. Place Breakable Wall on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Give wall_id a unique, stable id.
  3. In durability, set break_from to left, right, above or below.
  4. Set health to the accepted damage needed to break it.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  8. Hit the wall from its chosen side to check damage.
  9. Hit the wall from another side to check blocked feedback.

any keeps ordinary hit behavior. Required ability and bomb-only rules still apply first. Shots, bombs and movement contacts judge the side by the player's position, including Super Jump from below; they use knockback direction only when the source position is unavailable. Separate explosion messages still destroy the wall immediately. Configure blocked feedback with blocked_hit_sound in developer/config/switches.lua. Diagonal position checks use the wall's swap_width and swap_height bounds, so overriding tile-swap extents also affects that classification.

Switch-order gates#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place the gate template across the passage.
  3. Set its gate_id to a unique id such as rune_gate.
  4. Set Gate type to sequence_gate_3step or sequence_gate_5step.
