  5. In Sequence, set sequence_order to the switch ids in order, such as rune_a,rune_b,rune_c.
  6. Leave reset_on_wrong=true to restart the puzzle on a wrong step, or turn it off to ignore wrong steps while keeping progress.
  7. Place a lever or pressure_plate for each step.
  8. Give each switch its matching lever_id or plate_id from the order.
  9. Set each switch's target_ref to the gate, or its target_id to the gate's id.
  10. For momentary levers, set toggle=false.
  11. Give momentary levers a positive reset_delay, such as 0.5 seconds.
  12. Leave lever starts_on=false.
  13. Leave lever set_flag_on blank.
  14. Leave plate stays_pressed=false.
  15. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check to catch misspelled or duplicate switch ids.
  18. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  19. If this is a new room, open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  20. Place the new room in the layout.
  21. Click Export in Layout Editor.
  22. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  23. Activate a wrong step to check the fail sound and lever reset.
  24. Activate the switches in order to open the gate.
  25. Save the game.
  26. Load that slot to check the gate stays open.

Leave Persist state unticked for each switch in an ordered puzzle. Switches must be in the gate's room. A blank order uses the preset switch ids; an authored list replaces the entire preset, so it may have fewer steps. Reloading a partly solved room resets progress and ordinary levers. Only the opened gate is saved. Momentary plates count on press, never on release.

Make objects appear or disappear with story flags#

  1. In Tiled, select the object that should change, such as an NPC.
  2. Open its World state group.
  3. Set remove_when to the flag that should remove it, such as met_in_cave.
  4. Select the object that should arrive, in this room or another one.
  5. In its World state group, set appear_when to the same flag.
  6. Give the flag a writer, such as a lever's set_flag_on or a dialogue line that sets met_in_cave.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room for each changed room.
  8. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  11. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  12. Set the flag in the game.
  13. Leave the room, then enter it again to check that the first object is gone.
  14. Enter the arriving object's room to check that it is there.

The World state group is on NPC, Collectible, Ability Pickup, Key, Chest, Lore Item, Map Fragment, Sign, Hint Stone, Map Marker, Dialogue Trigger, Teleporter, Save Point, Gate, Locked Door, Breakable Wall, Light Source and Torch. Any other object can carry appear_when, remove_when and presence_timing as plain custom properties.

A condition is a comma list that must all hold. Each term is a flag name, !flag (the flag is not set), ability:<id> or item:<id> (add :n for a count). | joins alternatives inside one term: met_hermit, ability:dash|ability:grapple needs met_hermit and either ability. Blank or none means always.

presence_timing enter (the default) shows a change the next time the room is built: a room whose objects should change is rebuilt as soon as the player is not inside it, even when it is a visible neighbour, so a brief pop can show at the screen edge. live removes the object the moment its remove_when holds. An object is never added while the player watches; it arrives on the next entry.

Create Rooms bakes a <object instance id>__presence companion beside each object with a condition, so you can find it in the room collection. Removing a Breakable Wall object does not remove its painted wall tiles. Rebuilding a room also revives enemies whose respawn is room_reload and resets anything an ordinary unload resets. A room held loaded by an elevator refreshes only after the elevator releases it. Prepare & Check reports a condition that does not parse and a flag that nothing writes. NPC despawn_flag keeps working.

Change terrain with story flags#

Paint terrain that should appear or disappear, such as rubble that seals a passage or a bridge that forms, on its own tile layer.

  1. In Tiled, open the room.
  2. In the Layers panel, select a tile layer inside the room's art group, such as environment.
  3. Choose Layer ▸ New ▸ Tile Layer.
  4. Name the new layer, such as rubble. No other tile layer in the room may use that name.
  5. Paint the changing tiles on the new layer. Use tiles with collision shapes for terrain that should block the player.
  6. With the layer still selected, click Add Property (+) in the Properties panel.
  7. Enter appear_when in Property name.
  8. Choose string from the adjacent type list.
  9. Press Enter to add the property.
  10. Set appear_when to a flag, such as escape_done.
  11. Give the flag a writer, such as a lever's set_flag_on set to escape_done.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  17. Check that the passage is open.
  18. Pull the lever: the rubble appears at once and blocks the passage.

Use remove_when instead for terrain that should disappear, or both. The condition text is the same as for objects (see the section above). The layer changes at once, even with the player in the room, and the change survives leaving the room, saving and loading, because it follows the saved flags.

Optional layer properties:

  • state_collision (string): auto, the default, collides wherever the painted tiles have collision shapes. none draws the tiles without collision.
  • progression_from, progression_to (strings) and progression_passable_when_present (bool): describe the layer for Project ▸ Analyze Progression. See World-state tile layers.

Each layer becomes its own editable instance in the room collection, <room>_state_layer_<layer id>_tiles, with the condition on its presence component. Its cells leave the room tilemap and the room's shared tile collision; another layer painted under them, such as the floor, keeps its own collision. Prepare & Check reports a layer that cannot change: the bounds, solid, door, grapple and ice layers, a layer with parallax, a second layer with the same name, and a layer an object reads by name (such as a breakable wall's wall_layer). Such a layer stays in the room tilemap and always shows.

Add checkpoints, save points, and teleporters#

  • checkpoint: set a unique checkpoint_id; touching it changes respawn location and saves once. Re-entering the current checkpoint stays silent.
  • save_point: set a unique save_id; choose whether it heals/restores energy and requires interaction.
  • teleporter: create a pair with unique teleporter_id values and matching pair_id, or set an explicit target_id. Keep IDs exact and case-consistent.

Let a store buy items#

Shops and NPC vendors buy eligible items by default. Their trade list shows a Sell section below the catalog. Each choice sells one copy; only unique items ask for confirmation. Keys, weapons, charms, protected items and items worth zero stay out of the section. See Selling rules.

  1. Select a placed Shop or vendor NPC in Tiled.
  2. Tick Selling ▸ Buys items on the Shop, or Vendor ▸ Buys items on the NPC.
  3. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  7. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  8. Click Export.
  9. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  10. Press Interact beside the store.
  11. Select an owned item under Sell.

Stop a store buying items#

  1. Select a placed Shop or vendor NPC in Tiled.
  2. Untick Selling ▸ Buys items on the Shop, or Vendor ▸ Buys items on the NPC.
  3. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  6. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  7. Click Export.
  8. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  9. Press Interact beside the store to check that Sell is absent.

Set a store's sale share#

A store pays floor(item price × share) for one copy. -1 inherits Game Settings ▸ Game ▸ Shops ▸ Sell price share; 0 hides all sale items; 1 pays the full item base price. Catalog purchase prices are independent: keep discounted purchase prices at least as high as their sale value.

  1. Select a placed Shop or vendor NPC in Tiled.
  2. Set Selling ▸ Sell share on the Shop, or Vendor ▸ Sell share on the NPC, to -1 or a number from 0 to 1.
  3. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  6. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  7. Click Export.
  8. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  9. Press Interact beside the store to check the sale prices.

Add a shop#

A shop sells entries from a purchase catalog. Without Art item or linked Object Art, the shop is invisible. Catalog prices and stock live in developer/config/shops.lua; see Economy.

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Interaction ▸ Shop from tiled/templates/interaction/shop.tx.
  3. Choose Shop id from the catalog dropdown.
  4. Set Presentation ▸ Prompt text to the interaction prompt; blank uses the translated Shop prompt.
  5. Set Presentation ▸ Menu title if this shop needs a title instead of the catalog name.
  6. Set Presentation ▸ Range to the interaction distance in world pixels.
  7. Tick Presentation ▸ Show on map if the shop should appear after room entry.
  8. Set Access ▸ Requires flag if opening this shop needs a saved flag.
  9. Set Access ▸ Locked text if locked access needs a custom explanation.
  10. Set Art item if you use catalog artwork.
  11. Link an Object Art object's Owner ref to the shop if you use separate artwork instead.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  17. Click Export.
  18. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  19. Press Interact beside the shop.

The paused purchase list shows prices, stock and unavailable offers. A purchase updates the list immediately; saved stock is shared by all shops using its catalog.

Add an upgrade station#

An Upgrade Station sells permanent stats from a purchase catalog. Its offers live in developer/config/shops.lua; their stat effects and prerequisite tiers live in developer/config/upgrades.lua. Without Art item or linked Object Art, the station is invisible.

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Interaction ▸ Upgrade Station from tiled/templates/interaction/upgrade_station.tx.
  3. Choose Upgrade catalog; forge is the sample permanent-stat catalog.
  4. Set Presentation ▸ Prompt text; blank uses the translated Upgrade prompt.
  5. Set Presentation ▸ Menu title if this station needs a title instead of the catalog name.
  6. Set Presentation ▸ Range to the interaction distance in world pixels.
  7. Tick Presentation ▸ Show on map to show the shop marker after room entry.
  8. Set Access ▸ Requires flag if opening the station needs a saved flag.
  9. Set Access ▸ Locked text if locked access needs a custom explanation.
  10. Set Art item if you use catalog artwork.
  11. Link an Object Art object's Owner ref to the station if you use separate artwork instead.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  17. Click Export.
  18. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  19. Press Interact beside the station.

The paused menu shows each upgrade's description and stat changes. A missing prerequisite reads Requires Health Boost I, for example; an owned upgrade reads Applied and cannot be bought again. Buying capacity updates the player immediately and saves the permanent bonus. A stamina boost enables stamina when Max stamina starts at 0. Upgrade stations do not buy inventory items.

Add a bank#

A bank moves currency between the carried balance and one shared saved balance. Banked currency stays safe from the death drop. Banks have no purchase catalog. Without Art item or linked Object Art, the bank is invisible.

  1. Open MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled.
  2. Select the top-level templates object layer.
  3. Place Interaction ▸ Bank from tiled/templates/interaction/bank.tx.
  4. Set Bank id to a unique stable name.
  5. Choose Access ▸ Bank mode: both, deposit or withdraw.
  6. Set Presentation ▸ Menu title if the bank needs a custom title.
  7. Clear Presentation ▸ Prompt text to use the translated Bank prompt.
  8. Set Presentation ▸ Range to the interaction distance in world pixels.
  9. Tick Presentation ▸ Show on map to show the shop marker after room entry.
  10. Set Access ▸ Requires flag if access needs a saved flag.
  11. Set Access ▸ Locked text if access needs a custom explanation.
  12. Choose Art item if using catalog artwork.
  13. Link an Object Art object's Owner ref to the bank if using separate artwork.
  14. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  15. Close Defold after changing toolkit modules.
  16. Open Defold.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  18. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  20. Open Layout Editor on the title screen.
  21. Click Export.
  22. Choose Play Game.
  23. Start on an empty save slot.
  24. Press Interact beside the bank.

The paused menu shows carried and banked balances. Each permitted direction lists its configured amounts followed by Deposit all or Withdraw all. A row is disabled when its full amount is unavailable; it never transfers a smaller amount. The default amounts are 10 and 100, set by settings.bank.steps in developer/config/shops.lua. All banks share the balance regardless of Bank id. Blank menu titles use the translated Bank title. Journal ▸ Records shows Banked while that balance is above 0.

Transfers autosave both balances by default (settings.bank.autosave in shops.lua). A disk failure keeps the transfer in memory and shows a save-failure notice. A pending death drop or a sandbox session suspends saving: transfers then stay in memory without a failure notice. Save after recovering the death drop to keep those changes. With the death drop enabled, dying drops only carried currency; it leaves the saved bank balance intact. Withdrawn currency returns to the carried balance shown by the HUD. Quit and load to check persistence.

Add an offering altar#

An offering altar trades once: its currency and optional item cost buy the reward written on the object. It has no catalog. Without Art item or linked Object Art, the altar is invisible.

  1. Select the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Place Interaction ▸ Offering Altar from tiled/templates/interaction/offering_altar.tx.
  3. Set Altar id to a stable unique name for this altar.
  4. Set Offer ▸ Cost currency to a nonnegative whole amount.
  5. Set Offer ▸ Cost item to an item id if the trade also costs items.
  6. Set Offer ▸ Cost item count to the positive whole item count.
  7. Set Payout ▸ Reward to grant text, such as collectible:health_container.
  8. Set Payout ▸ Reward text if the question needs a custom reward label.
  9. Set Presentation ▸ Menu title if the menu needs a custom title.
  10. Clear Presentation ▸ Prompt text to use the translated Offer prompt.
  11. Set Lifecycle ▸ Requires flag if access needs a saved flag.
  12. Set Lifecycle ▸ Locked text if access needs a custom explanation.
  13. Set Lifecycle ▸ Set flag if the reward should also set a flag.
  14. Tick Lifecycle ▸ Remove after use if the used altar should disappear.
  15. Choose Art item if using catalog artwork.
  16. Link an Object Art object's Owner ref to the altar if using separate artwork.
  17. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  18. Close Defold after changing toolkit modules.
  19. Open Defold.
  20. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  21. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  22. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  23. Open Layout Editor on the title screen.
  24. Click Export.
  25. Choose Play Game.
  26. Start on an empty save slot.
  27. Press Interact beside the altar.
  28. Choose Yes in its cost-and-reward question.

A blank reward label describes the grant; a blank menu title uses Offering. Currency and items are charged together only when the reward can be given. A refused or unaffordable trade changes nothing. A used altar refuses further offerings after loading too. Remove after use removes its linked art along with it, both immediately and on later loads. Keep Altar id unchanged after publishing: it keeps the saved used state stable. A blank id uses the baked object id, which changes if the object is recreated.

See Economy for the reward grammar and cost rules.

Add a skill trainer#

A skill trainer sells ability unlocks from developer/config/shops.lua. Training Hall sells Wall Jump and Double Jump. Each entry uses ability:<id>; ability ids and descriptions come from developer/config/abilities.lua. Without Art item or linked Object Art, the trainer is invisible.

  1. Select the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Place Interaction ▸ Skill Trainer from tiled/templates/interaction/skill_trainer.tx.
  3. Choose Trainer catalog.
  4. Set Presentation ▸ Menu title to override the catalog name if needed.
  5. Set Presentation ▸ Prompt text if the button needs different wording.
  6. Set Presentation ▸ Range for the interaction distance.
  7. Tick Presentation ▸ Show on map to show the shop marker after room entry.
  8. Set Access ▸ Requires flag if opening the trainer needs a saved flag.
  9. Set Access ▸ Locked text to explain that requirement.
  10. Tick Lifecycle ▸ Leave when done to remove the trainer after every catalog ability is learned.
  11. Choose Art item for catalog artwork.
  12. Link an Object Art object's Owner ref to the trainer when using separate artwork.
  13. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  14. Close Defold after updating toolkit authoring modules.
  15. Open the project in Defold.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  18. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  19. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  20. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  21. Press Interact beside the trainer.

Purchased abilities work immediately and survive loading even if unticked in Game Settings ▸ Abilities. Start unlocks show Learned too; unticking a start unlock revokes it only when that save never earned or purchased it. A finished trainer with Leave when done disappears with its art, including when loading a save that already owns every catalog ability. Locked and hidden catalog entries still count toward completion. Trainers do not buy items. Project ▸ Analyze Progression treats purchased rewards as uncertain because it does not prove affordability or access conditions.

Return with a Warp Statue#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Interaction ▸ Warp Statue from tiled/templates/interaction/warp_statue.tx.
  3. Resize its rectangle to cover the interaction sensor.
  4. Set Label to the interaction prompt players should see.
  5. Set Range to the interaction distance in world pixels.
  6. Set Access ▸ Cost currency to the price per return; 0 is free.
  7. Set Access ▸ Requires flag if this statue needs a saved unlock.
  8. Set Access ▸ Cooldown to the seconds between accepted returns.
  9. Set Transition ▸ Fade out to the departure fade duration.
  10. Set Transition ▸ Fade in to the arrival fade duration.
  11. Choose Feedback ▸ Sound warp for an accepted return.
  12. Choose Feedback ▸ Sound fail for a refused return.
  13. Set the toasts in Feedback; blank Toast cost uses the game's currency format.
  14. Set Feedback ▸ Set flag if a successful return should record a saved flag.
  15. Set Art item if you use catalog artwork.
  16. Link an Object Art object's Owner ref to the statue if you use separate artwork instead.
  17. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  18. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  20. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  21. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  22. Click Export.
  23. Choose Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  24. Touch a Checkpoint, Save Point or Fast Travel Station to set the return location.
  25. Travel two rooms away.
  26. Press Interact at the statue to return to that location with departure and arrival fades.

The statue keeps current inventory, flags and collected items, apart from its currency cost and optional success flag. It refuses during a locked boss fight (including bosses without a Boss Arena), while riding a moving or handoff elevator trip, when short of currency, or when the checkpoint is missing or no longer in the published layout. An empty moving elevator does not block it. The price is charged once the return request is accepted; a later room-load failure does not refund it. Show toast controls its toast feedback.

Fast travel stations#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Fast Travel Station from tiled/templates/navigation/fast_travel.tx.
  3. Set Station id to a unique, stable text id, or keep the object name nonempty. Prepare & Check requires Station id or an object name; a blank Station id bakes as the room/object instance id.
  4. Set Station name to the name players should see in the destination list.
  5. Resize the rectangle to cover the station's interaction sensor.
  6. Set Art item to your named artwork if you use a catalog image.
  7. Link an Object Art object with Owner ref if you use separate artwork instead.
  8. Set Unlock cost in Unlock to the currency paid on first activation; 0 is free.
  9. Enable Auto unlock only if the destination should register already unlocked, without the activation rest.
  10. Choose Sound id in Presentation; none makes activation silent.
  11. Set Label locked in Presentation if you want an alternative to Activate. A positive cost appears beside it using the currency format.
  12. Set Label unlocked in Presentation if you want an alternative to Travel.
  13. Choose Show on map in Presentation to show the station's bench marker.
  14. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  15. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  18. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  19. Click Export.
  20. Choose Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  21. Approach the station: Activate appears with your current Interact button.
  22. Press Interact to activate it: an affordable cost is charged once, the checkpoint and unlock save together, and health refills only on this first activation. The Healing Flask refills too while refill_at_save_points in developer/config/healing.lua is on (the default).
  23. Press Interact again to open the world map in travel mode; later uses do not refill health or the Healing Flask.
  24. Place a second station for a refusal check.
  25. Set its Unlock cost to 999.
  26. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  27. Repeat steps 15–20.
  28. Press Interact at that station while carrying less than 999 currency: activation is refused with a formatted message.

Death and saves#

Death reloads the last saved world and inventory. With Death drop on, the loss is calculated from the restored currency, never from unsaved pickups. The slot immediately records the reduced currency and one recoverable drop; quitting and choosing Continue keeps that penalty. The checkpoint location and other saved items, flags and abilities keep their saved values.

The drop waits at the last safe grounded position in the death room and returns its currency when collected. If no trustworthy safe ground was recorded there, the game waives that death's loss and keeps any earlier drop. Dying again with a valid safe position replaces the previous drop; its old amount is lost. A zero-loss result clears the previous drop.

In developer/config/game_config.lua, progression.death also offers max_loss (0 means no extra cap), drop_lifetime (0 lasts until collection or replacement), drop_pickup_radius in world pixels and drop_sound. The marker follows room streaming and survives a save/load round trip. Collection needs no immediate save: another death before saving restores the reduced currency and drop together. Place save points when you want recovered currency and current progress committed to the slot.

Fast travel network and rules#

  1. Give every Fast Travel Station a unique Station id across the world.
  2. Set a Save Point's Save id to a unique text id if it joins the network.
  3. Enable that Save Point's Is fast travel service.
  4. Activate a station or travel-enabled save point in game to unlock its destination and fast_travel_<id> flag.
  5. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… in Defold.
  6. Select Map.
  7. Find Fast travel below Player markers.
  8. Set Travel from to anywhere for pause-menu travel (the default), or stations for station and save-point departures only.
  9. Set Travel cost (currency) to the fee for each trip; 0 is free. The fee is charged once when the trip message is accepted; a later room-loading failure does not refund it.
  10. Set Fade time (seconds) to the duration of each fade; 0 makes it instant.
  11. Enable Block in boss fights to refuse trips during a locked boss fight, including one without a Boss Arena, or a sealed fight in the current room.
  12. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. In Tiled, select a Boss.
  15. Set Reward unlock fast travel in Rewards and persistence to a destination's Station id or travel-enabled Save id. A boss can unlock a destination in a room the player has never visited.
  16. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  17. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  18. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check to check duplicate ids and missing rewards.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  20. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  21. Click Export to publish both destination rooms and validate the reward destinations.
  22. Choose Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  23. Interact with an unlocked station or travel-enabled save point to choose a destination on the world map. Show on map controls its marker; hidden markers remain selectable destinations.
  24. Defeat the Boss to check that its distant destination becomes selectable.

Add breakable pots#

Template Purpose
Environment ▸ Breakable Prop Nonblocking pots, crates and searchable scenery with optional pickups and a switch channel.
  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Drag Environment ▸ Breakable Prop from the Templates panel into the room.
  3. Resize it to a tight rectangle around the pot or crate.
  4. Set Prop id to a unique stable name for saved destruction and reward tracking.
  5. For linked art, insert a tile object for the whole pot.
  6. Set that image object's Class to object_art.
  7. Select the prop in that image's owner_ref property.
  8. Set that image's art_state to default.
  9. Optionally link a second image with art_state broken for its remains.
  10. For painted art instead, set Tiles ▸ Painted layer to the decorative layer containing the prop tiles.
  11. Set Durability ▸ Health to the number of ordinary damage points it takes to break.
  12. Choose Durability ▸ Required ability when only hits from that ability should break it.
  13. Tick Durability ▸ Break on interact to also allow searching it with Interact.
  14. Set Rewards ▸ Drop collectible to a pickup type.
  15. Set Rewards ▸ Drop count to the number of chances to drop that pickup.
  16. Set Rewards ▸ Drop chance between 0 and 1 for each pickup.
  17. Select target_ref to open a door or another switch receiver in this room.
  18. For a receiver in another room instead, set Target id to its channel.
  19. Tick Persistence ▸ Stays broken to save both the broken prop and its opened channel.
  20. Choose Feedback ▸ Sound break for the break sound.
  21. Choose Art feedback ▸ Consume effect for the break particle.
  22. Choose File ▸ Export All Maps after painting prop tiles.
  23. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  24. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  25. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Successful drop rolls create collectable pickups at the prop. Currency is grouped into one pickup worth the successful count times that currency type's value. A chance of 0 or a count of 0 drops nothing. With Stays broken off, the prop can reset when its room reloads, but each planned reward slot can pay only once per save: breaking it again skips loot already on the floor or collected. Chance rolls still run on each break; a later success can fill an unused slot. Grouped currency uses one slot, so its first successful batch is its only payout. Uncollected loot stays on the floor when leaving and returning during the same game session, like chest loot. Floor loot is not saved across quitting and reloading; a saved broken prop does not issue it again.

Props never block the player. Painted props require Rotation 0 and a decorative layer; control layers such as solid and tag layers such as ice and grapple cannot be cut. Choose art tiles without collision shapes: baked room collision remains after the tiles hide. Linked broken artwork remains after breaking; without it, default art hides using developer/config/object_art.lua prop_state_fallbacks. Break on interact bypasses the hit requirement. Without Stays broken, the whole prop returns when the room reloads. Prop drops are runtime pickups excluded from the world census and Journal totals; use placed collectibles for relics, lore and unique items.

Elements#

The empty toolkit's player deals physical damage (slash, projectile and impact) until you add an infusion or configure an elemental attack. No infusion ships enabled and no extra button is used.

Tune damage taken and feedback:

  1. In Defold, open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Select Enemies and elements.
  3. Open Elements.
  4. Set a Player damage taken: <type> factor (0 is immune; 1 is unchanged).
  5. Set Weakness multiplier if weak hits should use another factor.
  6. Set Resistance multiplier if resistant hits should use another factor.
  7. Set Colour damage numbers by element.
  8. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Type labels, colours, families and optional rules live in the fully commented developer/config/damage_types.lua. Lethal hits bypass player type factors. ability_taken adds optional ability resistances; it does not replace hazard/gate protection abilities. Per-attack damage_type lives in combat_config.lua for timeline attacks and player_actions.lua for legacy/movement contacts. An omitted type uses the player_attacks category default.

Add an ability infusion:

  1. Open developer/config/damage_types.lua.
  2. Add { ability = "heat_resistance", melee = "fire" }, to player_attack_infusions (the commented example).
  3. Save the file.
  4. In Tiled, place an Ability pickup on the top-level templates layer.
  5. Set its Ability id to heat_resistance, or use your own configured ability id in both places.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Reopen the Tiled project to refresh catalog choices.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  11. In the game's Layout Editor, click Export.
  12. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  13. Collect the pickup.
  14. Strike an enemy to check fire-coloured damage numbers.
  15. Strike a fire-only wall to check that it breaks.

Infusions are ordered: the first unlocked row wins; categories it omits keep their base type. An infusion overrides per-attack types. To see damage numbers, enable Game Settings ▸ Game ▸ Options ▸ Damage numbers. Enemy and Boss affinity fields select weaknesses, resistances, immunities and accepted types; immune feedback still flashes and sounds with numbers hidden. For an annotated wall route, Project ▸ Analyze Progression reports the infusion ability as its requirement (any configured matching infusion can supply it). The analysis uses player_attacks category defaults and infusion abilities; per-attack-only overrides are conservatively unknown unless also represented there. If no base category or infusion emits the required type, the route is unknown with breakable_wall_damage_type_never_emitted. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check again before Export Game after config, UI or hook edits.

Add hazards, secrets, and breakable walls#

For a hazard:

  1. Select the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Place Environment ▸ Hazard.
  3. Set Hazard type to the preset you want.
  4. In Damage and knockback, choose On contact.
  5. In Elements, choose Damage type, or leave none to inherit the preset.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.

Spikes and pits return by default. A Pendulum Hazard's Damage and knockback ▸ Damage type uses slash when set to none. Water Volume damage uses the water_types map in developer/config/damage_types.lua; lava sends fire and acid sends poison to players and enemies. Hazard and water ability protections remain separate from typed damage resistances.

For a breakable wall:

  1. Select the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Place Interaction ▸ Breakable Wall.
  3. Set a unique Wall id.
  4. In Durability, set Health.
  5. Set Required ability if a particular action must hit it.
  6. In Durability, set Required damage type, or leave none for any type.
  7. In Rewards and secrets, set Reveals secret if it should reveal a secret.
  8. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.

Both wall requirements must match. A physical requirement accepts slash, projectile and impact; a fire requirement accepts fire, independently of the attack's action tag. A Secret needs its own unique Secret id and reveal choice. A Timed Door is controlled by a duration or trigger rather than inventory progression.

A wall with Required ability breaks only to hits that ability makes; a Dash or Air Dash wall breaks to an air dash.

Rewards and secrets ▸ Drop collectible releases one collectable pickup when the wall breaks. Walls stay broken across room reloads and saves and never issue the drop twice. Uncollected loot survives leaving and returning in the same game session, like chest loot, but is not saved across quitting and reloading.

Make a saw run along a rail:

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Environment ▸ Hazard over the saw's starting hitbox.
  3. Set Hazard type to saw_blade.
  4. Give it a unique Hazard id.
  5. In Path motion, set Motion type to line.
  6. Set Motion distance x to the rail's length in pixels.
  7. Set Motion speed to its travel speed in pixels per second.
  8. Set Motion wait to the pause at each end.
  9. Set Motion loop to pingpong to reverse at the ends.
  10. Paint the saw on the art layer named by Motion ▸ Retract visual layer.
  11. Tick Motion ▸ Retract hides tiles on the Hazard.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.

For a drawn path:

  1. Add an object layer named paths outside templates.
  2. Select paths.
  3. Draw the route with Tiled's Insert Polyline tool.
  4. Select the Hazard.
  5. In Path motion, set Motion type to path.
  6. Pick the polyline with Motion path ref.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The saw starts at the first point and travels at constant speed; loop closes the path, pingpong reverses, and once stops at the far end. Motion distance y is positive down. Motion offset is a timing offset in seconds into the cycle at room load.

Make a fire bar:

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Environment ▸ Hazard over the bar's hitbox.
  3. Set Hazard type to flame_jet.
  4. Give it a unique Hazard id.
  5. In Path motion, set Motion type to orbit.
  6. Set Motion radius to the circle radius in pixels.
  7. Set Motion speed to degrees per second, using a negative value for clockwise travel.
  8. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  9. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The default pivot is one radius left of the drawn centre. To choose a pivot, place a point on an object layer outside templates and select it with Motion pivot ref. The drawn centre sets the starting angle and radius.

Make a crusher:

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Environment ▸ Hazard over the raised crusher's hitbox.
  3. Set Hazard type to crusher.
  4. Give it a unique Hazard id.
  5. In Path motion, set Motion type to slam.
  6. Set Motion distance y to the downward drop in pixels.
  7. Set Motion wait to the delay at the top.
  8. Set Slam drop speed to the falling speed in pixels per second.
  9. Set Slam rise speed to the return speed in pixels per second.
  10. Leave Slam warn at -1 to inherit the type's warning duration.
  11. Leave Slam rest at -1 to inherit its bottom hold duration.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The crusher shakes during its warning, drops, holds, then rises. Its commented crusher entry in developer/config/hazard_types.lua sets warning/impact sounds and the impact effect. Moving saws and crushers can carry painted tiles from Motion ▸ Retract visual layer with Retract hides tiles ticked, even without Retractable. The starting room copy clears once; retraction hides only the hazard's carried art. Use art without collision shapes and paint the floor on a separate layer: carried art has the hazard trigger, not a moving solid body. Linked object art remains available. To start with a lever, untick Activation and feedback ▸ Enabled and select the Hazard in the lever's Target ref. Deactivation freezes movement in place; activation continues it, including saved switch replay. Pause freezes motion. Safe returns avoid the whole sweep; place a Hazard Return Zone on safe ground in tight rooms. New rooms still need placement and Export in Layout Editor before Play Game.

To author a bomb wall and bomb secret:

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Abilities ▸ Ability Pickup before the blocked passage.
  3. Set its Ability id to bomb.
  4. Place Interaction ▸ Breakable Wall across the passage.
  5. Give it a unique Wall id.
  6. Set Wall type to bomb_only.
  7. Leave Required ability set to none to use the Bomb requirement.
  8. Place Environment ▸ Secret over the hidden area.
  9. Give it a unique Secret id.
  10. Set Secret type to bomb_wall.
  11. In Discovery, set Reveals on to attack.
  12. Leave Required ability set to none to inherit Bomb from this preset.
  13. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  14. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  17. If this is a new room, open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  18. Place the new room in the layout.
  19. Click Export in Layout Editor.
  20. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  21. Collect the Bomb pickup.
  22. Hold Down and press Ranged Attack while grounded to place a bomb.
  23. Open the pause menu before the fuse ends to check it holds.
  24. Resume play to check the explosion breaks the wall and reveals the secret.

Down + Ranged Attack places a bomb once Bomb is owned. An explicit wall Required ability overrides the wall type's default. Bomb-only walls with the default requirement ignore sword, dash and slide hits. Set the starting unlock under Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Abilities ▸ Utility if a pickup is not needed. Tune fuse, radius, damage, active limit and feedback in the commented bomb block in developer/config/player_actions.lua.

Template rectangles define active areas. Resize them in Tiled to cover the intended region.

To author a passive Reveal Lens secret:

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Abilities ▸ Ability Pickup before the hidden area.
  3. Set its Ability id to reveal_lens.
  4. Place Environment ▸ Secret over the hidden area.
  5. Give it a unique Secret id.
  6. Set Secret type to illusory_wall.
  7. In Discovery, set Reveals on to ability.
  8. Set Required ability to none or reveal_lens.
  9. Resize the Secret rectangle to cover the intended area.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. If this is a new room, open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  15. Place the new room in the layout.
  16. Click Export in Layout Editor.
  17. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  18. Walk near the secret before collecting the lens to check it stays hidden.
  19. Collect the Reveal Lens pickup.
  20. Walk near the secret to check its toast and map marker.
  21. Save the game.
  22. Reload the save to check the secret stays revealed.

The lens is passive: no button press or stamina is needed. The starting unlock is Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Abilities ▸ Utility ▸ Reveal lens. Tune the fully commented reveal_lens block in developer/config/secret_settings.lua: radius_tiles measures from the rectangle's nearest edge in tile widths, and check_interval controls seconds between checks. Only secrets with Reveals on ability and an empty or matching lens requirement participate. A preset such as bomb_wall supplies its own requirement, so use illusory_wall for the lens. Attack secrets and other requirements keep their existing rules. Analyze Progression does not model secrets.

Hazard returns cost health before a fade to a safe landing. A health cost that empties health causes normal death instead. damage hurts and knocks back; safe_return returns; kill kills through protection. type_default uses developer/config/hazard_types.lua. Return damage of -1 inherits the type's optional return_damage, then Game Settings; 0 returns without a cost. The type's on_contact and return_damage are fully commented in that file.

To author Hazard returns:

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… in Defold.
  2. Select Game.
  3. Set Hazard returns in the section after Cutscenes.
  4. Set Return damage.
  5. Set Return fade (seconds).
  6. Set Protection after return (seconds).
  7. Click Save.
  8. Place an Environment ▸ Hazard on Tiled's top-level templates layer.
  9. Choose its Hazard type.
  10. Set Damage and knockback ▸ On contact, or leave type_default.
  11. Set Return damage, or leave -1 to inherit.
  12. Place Navigation ▸ Hazard Return Zone on safe ground at the challenge start.
  13. Resize the zone so its bottom centre marks the landing point.
  14. Set Placement ▸ Return facing to 0 to keep facing, 1 for right, or -1 for left.
  15. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  16. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  18. Correct any return_zone_inside_hazard warning by moving the zone.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  20. Start a fresh save.
  21. Enter the Hazard Return Zone.
  22. Touch the spikes to check the return.
  23. Fall into the pit to check the return.

A zone wins over the last dry, stable ground, which may be in the room just left. Only a lethal pit can fall back to the last checkpoint. A zone with Forget return point clears the challenge start. Leaving its room, dying, respawning, or another teleport clears the active zone. Water is allowed at an authored return point; dry returns end swimming through the water exit trigger.

Dash, slide, parry and invincibility ignore return spikes. Spikes touched in the protection after a hit still return the player, without damage, so an enemy cannot hold them there. A protected pit still returns without damage; a kill contact still kills. Ability or item hazard immunity prevents all three contact modes. Returns stay inside a locked fight, including a Boss with Arena lock and no Boss Arena. A lethal pit with no safe target eventually kills after protection ends.

With Hazard returns off, spikes hurt and knock back while pits kill. A lethal type never becomes ordinary damage, even if On contact says damage. Falling below all rooms also acts as a pit unless progression.hazard_return.world_bottom_acts_as_pit is false. Ridden elevators cancel and reset home for a lethal return; ordinary hazards retain knockback. The optional Death drop still uses its own last-safe-ground record.

To protect the player from a Hazard with an ability or owned item:

  1. Open developer/config/hazard_types.lua.
  2. Set the type's immunity_ability to an id declared in developer/config/abilities.lua, or leave it nil for no ability protection.
  3. Set the type's immunity_item to an inventory id from developer/config/items.lua, a collectible's item_id, or a key id, or leave it nil for no item protection.
  4. Select the Hazard in Tiled.
  5. Set Required resistance to an ability for this placement, or leave none to inherit the type's ability and item protection.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Correct any undefined_immunity_ability or undefined_immunity_item warnings in developer/config/hazard_types.lua.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build after Prepare & Check passes.
  11. Unlock the chosen ability or collect the chosen item during play.
  12. Touch the Hazard to check that it deals no damage.

With Required resistance set, only that ability grants protection; the type's ability and item defaults are skipped. With none, either matching type protection prevents damage. These immunity settings apply to Hazards and matching lava, acid and cold Water Volumes; enemies still take their configured water damage. Deep Water's swim setting grants no useful damage test because that hazard type already deals zero direct damage.

To make a lava area require Heat Resistance:

  1. Place an Environment ▸ Hazard on Tiled's top-level templates layer.
  2. Set Hazard type to lava.
  3. Resize the Hazard rectangle to cover the lava area.
  4. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. In Defold, open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  6. Select Abilities.
  7. Tick Heat Resistance under Defensive to give it to the player in a new game.
  8. Click Save.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Start a fresh save slot.
  13. Touch the lava Hazard to check that it deals no damage.

For an earned unlock, an Ability Pickup with ability_id set to heat_resistance grants it during play while the starting toggle remains off. Acid Resistance protects against acid and poison gas; Cold Resistance protects against freezing water. Lava, acid and cold Water Volumes use the same type protection. A Gate with the lava_floor, acid_pool or cold_zone preset needs the matching resistance.

Add a swinging blade#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Environment ▸ Pendulum Hazard.
  3. Drag its top edge to the pivot.
  4. Drag its bottom edge to the blade; the rectangle height is the rod length.
  5. Set pendulum_id to a unique switch channel.
  6. Expand swing.
  7. Set Arc degrees to the total swing angle.
  8. Set Swing seconds to the time for one side-to-side pass.
  9. Set Start delay for a timing offset between blades.
  10. Expand damage_and_knockback (Damage and knockback).
  11. Choose On contact: type_default means damage, safe_return returns to safe ground, and kill kills even during hit flashing.
  12. Set Return damage, or leave -1 to inherit Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Game ▸ Hazard returns.
  13. Set the blade image's owner_ref to the pendulum using the object picker.
  14. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  15. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Set Blade width to 0 to use the rectangle width. Blade height sets the trigger height at the rod's bottom. Linked artwork rotates around the pivot with the blade, preserving its authored offset and angle. A lever's target_ref can select this pendulum. Its saved switch channel replays when the room loads. With Hazard returns off, safe_return becomes ordinary damage. Returns never select ground inside an enabled pendulum's entire swing area.

Add a zip line#

  1. Add an object layer named paths in Tiled.
  2. Draw an unrotated polyline from the cable's start to its end on paths.
  3. Select the visible top-level templates layer.
  4. Place Navigation ▸ Zip Line from tiled/templates/navigation/zip_line.tx at the start.
  5. Pick the polyline in path_ref with Tiled's object selector.
  6. Set ride ▸ Speed in world pixels per second.
  7. Tick ride ▸ Auto start if touching the start should begin the ride without Interact.
  8. Paint cable art along the polyline on an art layer.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  10. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  13. Adjust Advanced ▸ Zip line ▸ Hang offset.
  14. Click Save.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. Check that the hands meet the cable in play.

The player hangs below the cable and keeps terrain collision. Jump lets go when Jump lets go is enabled. Damage, water, death and teleports end a ride; Grapple and attacks are ignored while riding. Leave space for the hanging body, including at bends and streamed room seams. ride ▸ Exit boost sets the speed along the final segment on release; its default is zero. Keep the path on a visible layer, with at least two distinct points and no rotation. Prepare & Check reports a broken selection as an error and cable/body overlap with solid tiles as a warning. The baked room object exposes its text path_points offsets.

Add simple traversal#

  • one_way_platform: a passthrough platform area.
  • moving_platform: choose motion type, speed, distance, wait time, and loop mode.
  • elevator + elevator_node: build a persistent vertical cabin that can cross streamed rooms. Follow the dedicated workflow below; this is not a tall single-room moving platform.
  • conveyor: choose direction and speed.
  • ice_floor: place Navigation ▸ Ice Floor over floor tiles for custom slippery-ground multipliers.
  • crumble_platform: tune crumble and respawn timing.
  • bounce_pad: tune the launch speed and angle.
  • ladder: resize the rectangle over the climbable art. If the climb animation looks off-center for your rig, tune climb_offset_x instead of moving the rectangle; it only applies when snap_to_center=true and uses unscaled world pixels. A ladder may end level with the floor it serves or up to one tile above it. At the top, Left or Right steps onto the floor beside it, even one a little below the feet, including a one-way platform the ladder climbs up through. Holding Up climbs until the player's feet are level with that floor, or stops at the top when there is no reachable floor beside the ladder. Jump lets go when Can jump off is enabled. Choose Ladder type hang_bar for horizontal hanging or climb_mesh for movement in eight directions. Both ignore Snap to center. A hang bar also ignores Pass through top; Can jump off means Jump lets go without an upward launch. A player holding one kind never switches to another kind they touch, such as a ladder at a bar's end: they let go first, then grab it. Grab extend up and Grab extend down let a climb start that far beyond the ladder's ends, so two ladders that meet end to end, in one room or across a room's edge, hand the climber on.
  • wall_jump_surface: resize over a wall to tune or restrict unlocked wall movement.
  • water_volume: resize the region and tune swim, current, damage, drag, and oxygen properties. buoyancy is a 0–1 factor that becomes an upward cap of 0–200 px/s, current_strength is px/s, and water_drag is per-second drift retention (0–1). Swimming requires the swim ability unless game_config.player.swim.require_ability = false.

Launch the player at an angle#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Navigation ▸ Bounce Pad on supported ground.
  3. Set Bounce speed to the desired speed in pixels per second.
  4. Set launch ▸ Launch angle to 45 for up-right or 135 for up-left.
  5. Set launch ▸ Control lock time to the seconds during which horizontal steering is blocked.
  6. Untick launch ▸ Keep momentum if normal air braking should resume after the lock.
  7. Check the whole player fits through the flight arc and onto safe landing ground.
  8. Add a Map Properties string progression_links such as entry>landing for a one-way route between declared areas.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  10. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  13. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  14. Click Export after placing the room.
  15. Choose Play Game with a fresh save slot.

Angles are measured anticlockwise from right. 90 keeps the existing upward bounce, including its Jump-held behavior; its launch lock and momentum fields do not change that bounce. Angled launches use normal gravity without a jump-release cut while rising. Opposing horizontal input brakes carried momentum after the lock. Water refuses an angled launch. Progression links describe the route explicitly; pad placement does not infer a connection.

Change movement in an area#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Environment ▸ Movement Zone over the area.
  3. Resize the rectangle to cover the intended movement space.
  4. Set Preset to low_gravity, heavy, sticky or sinking.
  5. Set an overrides multiplier only if the preset needs a local change (-1 inherits).
  6. Set overrides ▸ Sink speed to a downward speed in world pixels per second if needed (-1 inherits).
  7. Put a floor or Environment ▸ Hazard below a sinking pool.
  8. Check that the entire player and jump arc clear ceilings, other objects and moving hazard sweeps.
  9. Set Movement zone id to a unique text id if a switch will control it.
  10. Set the controlling Lever's target_id to that id if switching is needed.
  11. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  12. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  15. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  16. Click Export after placing the room.
  17. Choose Play Game with a fresh save slot.

Overlapping zones keep the strongest value per stat, so sticky and low gravity combine without multiplying two slows. Other temporary speed effects, such as Compact Form and healing, still multiply. Jump velocity scales after High Jump; height scales by jump_mult squared divided by gravity_mult, so floaty zones need extra headroom. Wall jumps, ladder jump-off and grapple launches remain unscaled. Climbing and ledge hangs keep their own movement. The existing gravity stat read also applies while swimming; swim and glide fall caps keep their settings. Sinking presets permit repeated Jump presses without a floor. Pause freezes lease refresh and sinking posts; leaving removes the zone source, with lease expiry as an unload fallback. activation ▸ Enabled sets the initial state; saved switch channels replay on room load.

Add a launch cannon#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Navigation ▸ Launch Cannon on clear ground.
  3. Set aim ▸ Aim mode to fixed or sweep.
  4. Set aim ▸ Angle in degrees anticlockwise from right (90 points up).
  5. Set aim ▸ Sweep range for the total arc around Angle when using sweep.
  6. Set aim ▸ Sweep speed in degrees per second.
  7. Set fire ▸ Fire on to jump or auto.
  8. Set fire ▸ Auto delay above zero for automatic firing.
  9. Set fire ▸ Launch speed in world pixels per second.
  10. Set fire ▸ Control lock time for the horizontal steering lock after firing.
  11. Untick fire ▸ Keep momentum if ordinary air braking should resume after the lock.
  12. Set fire ▸ Cooldown for the time between firing and the next entry.
  13. Select an optional fire ▸ Breaks action matching an Action Barrier's requirements ▸ Required action.
  14. Check that the whole loaded player and flight arc clear other objects, moving hazards and solid ceilings.
  15. Provide safe landing ground.
  16. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  17. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  18. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  19. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  20. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  21. Click Export after placing the room.
  22. Choose Play Game with a fresh save slot.

Touching loads the visible player at the cannon centre. Jump fires only in jump mode; auto waits its delay. Linked art rotates with the barrel. The commented player_settings.launch keys set the loaded pose, immunity (lethal contacts still bypass it) and button-token prompt. Breaks action creates one contact for the lock time, opens only a matching barrier and deals no enemy damage. Describe one-way routes explicitly with the map's progression_links.

Make a hang bar or climb mesh#

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place Navigation ▸ Ladder over the climbable art.
  3. Resize it wide and short for a bar, or over the mesh art for a mesh.
  4. Set Ladder type to hang_bar or climb_mesh.
  5. Set movement ▸ Hang speed for a bar or movement ▸ Climb speed for a mesh.
  6. Untick movement ▸ Drop on down if Down must not let go of a bar.
  7. Untick movement ▸ Can jump off if Jump must not let go.
  8. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  9. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  11. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  12. Click Export after placing the room.
  13. Choose Play Game with a fresh save slot.

Jump into a bar to grab it while airborne, then move left or right to its ends. On a mesh, move in eight directions, Jump away, climb onto support at the top, or descend onto the floor. Check the hands meet the bar with your chosen sprite or Spine animations. If the player hangs too low or too high, set Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Advanced ▸ Hang bar ▸ Hang offset: the distance from the bar's centre line to the player's origin, in base px. 0 hangs at the ladder hand height, which suits art that raises its hands. Animation names are in player_settings.lua's ladder_climb table.

Build a cart ride#

  1. In Tiled, select the paths object layer.
  2. Draw a polyline through open space entirely inside the room.
  3. Set the polyline's Name to cart_track.
  4. Paint the cart on art/player or art/environment with tiles that have no collision.
  5. Select the top-level templates object layer.
  6. Place Navigation ▸ Moving Platform around the painted cart.
  7. Set Platform type to path.
  8. Set path ▸ Path name to cart_track.
  9. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle control to throttle.
  10. Leave collision_and_rider ▸ Player parent ticked.
  11. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  12. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  15. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  16. Click Export after placing any new room.
  17. Choose Play Game.
  18. Board the cart.
  19. Hold Right along a rightward track to accelerate.
  20. Hold Left to brake.
  21. Press Jump to leave.

The stick projects onto the current track direction, including Up/Down on vertical segments. Opposing input brakes to rest before reversing. Vehicle rider walks defaults off. Vehicle max speed defaults to 400 px/s, Vehicle acceleration to 600 px/s², Vehicle brake to 1200 px/s² and Vehicle drag to 0 px/s² (coasting). Vehicle end stop holds the endpoint; fall_away falls for one second, hides and reappears after Vehicle return delay (default 2 seconds). Tracks do not collide with walls: keep the whole cart and rider clear of terrain, ceilings and sharp corners. Vehicle motion and its timers freeze while paused. Vehicle positions reset when their room reloads; they are not saved.

Add a track switch#

  1. Draw a second polyline whose first point lies on the first track, within 2 pixels.
  2. Set its Name to cart_branch.
  3. Select the cart.
  4. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle alt path to cart_branch.
  5. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle switch id to cart_junction.
  6. Place Interaction ▸ Lever before the junction.
  7. Set the lever's Target id to the text cart_junction.
  8. Leave the lever's Target ref empty.
  9. Tick conditions_and_flags ▸ Persist state if the route choice should survive saves.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. Flip the lever before driving past the junction.

An on channel selects the second track when crossing the junction forward; changing the switch after passing it does not teleport the cart. Driving backwards past the second track's start rejoins the first. A Strike Switch can target the same text channel. A Target object reference selects the platform's activation channel, not its track-switch channel.

Build a gondola that returns#

  1. Build a painted Moving Platform on a named path as in the cart recipe.
  2. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle control to auto.
  3. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle end to return.
  4. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle return delay to the empty wait in seconds.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Board the gondola.
  10. Jump off before the endpoint.
  11. Watch it return to its starting point after the empty delay.

Auto starts when a player boards; enemy passengers do not drive it. The gondola returns through its current branch to the main track, even when left mid-track. Progression ▸ Progression from / Progression to may name areas in the same room; clear Progression one way to model a return connector. These fields affect Analyze Progression only.

Float a raft on water#

  1. Build a painted Moving Platform on a named path as in the cart recipe.
  2. Place Environment ▸ Water Volume over the basin.
  3. Set swimming_and_current ▸ Current direction to right or left along the track.
  4. Set swimming_and_current ▸ Current strength to the drift speed in pixels per second.
  5. Select the Moving Platform.
  6. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle control to throttle.
  7. Tick vehicle ▸ Vehicle float.
  8. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle float offset to the pixels its top should sit above the surface.
  9. Set vehicle ▸ Vehicle end to stop.
  10. Draw the named path at the raft's bottom-left position on the drained basin floor.
  11. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  12. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  15. Board the raft.
  16. Release directional input to test the current.
  17. Hold the opposite direction to steer back.

Use the water-level switch recipe to drain and raise the basin. The raft follows the surface until its bottom reaches the track, then rests there. Its top offset defaults to 16 pixels; its height is included automatically. Drift follows the track direction matching the current and cannot exceed Vehicle max speed; the raft eases into it at Vehicle acceleration, braking first if it was moving the other way. An auto raft runs with its rider and drifts only when empty. Keep the whole raft and rider clear of ceilings and hazards at every water level. Prepare & Check warns raft_without_water when neither track crosses water.

Paint ice#

  1. In Tiled's Layers panel, select a top-level layer.
  2. If the room has no ice layer, choose Layer ▸ New ▸ Tile Layer.
  3. If you created a layer, name it ice.
  4. Select the ice layer.
  5. Paint tags over the floor cells that should be slippery.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export All Maps.
  7. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Ice tags are hidden and add no terrain collision; keep a floor of shaped tiles on a layer that does not scroll underneath. Leave Parallax X / Y at 1. Paint on walls has no floor effect: floor ice changes grounded movement only, while Wall Jump Surface's ice preset controls wall movement separately.

For per-zone tuning, place Navigation ▸ Ice Floor on the top-level templates layer over a floor. Under surface, leave Accel mult, Decel mult and Turn brake mult at -1 to inherit the preset (default ice), or enter custom values from 0 to 1. Smaller values build speed more slowly, coast farther and skid longer. The default preset lives in player_settings.lua under surfaces.ice; Game Settings ▸ Advanced ▸ Ice provides its three unscaled multipliers. Save and Build after changing them. tiled_object_config.lua ▸ surface_layers.ice.probe_margin_px grows each strip or rectangle upward (default 8 world pixels) so standing feet overlap it.

Make a Compact Form tunnel#

  1. In Tiled, paint a low tunnel with shaped tiles on a layer that does not scroll, with enough room for the compact body.
  2. Select the top-level templates layer.
  3. Place Abilities ▸ Ability Pickup before the tunnel.
  4. Set its Ability id to compact_form.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  10. Place any new room in the layout.
  11. Snap matching painted doors.
  12. Click Export.
  13. Choose Play Game.
  14. Choose an empty save slot.
  15. Collect the Compact Form pickup.
  16. Hold Down and press Slide on the ground to enter Compact Form.
  17. Crawl through the tunnel.
  18. Press Up or Jump under its ceiling to check that standing is refused.
  19. Press Up or Jump at the exit to stand.

For a starting unlock, enable Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Abilities ▸ Movement ▸ Compact Form. Players can also bind Compact Form in Settings ▸ Controls. Tune the commented compact_form block in developer/config/player_settings.lua; run Prepare & Check after changing height_reduction_ratio so the physical body matches the raycast body. Art stays anchored at the same feet position. Compact Form needs the sprite player's generated compact collider; Spine bone physics is unsupported. Ranged Attack places a bomb while compact once Bomb is owned. Down + Slide during a ground slide enters Compact Form; a plain second Slide press keeps Blink's gesture. Check floor and ceiling hazards and camera and ambience zones for body-swap trigger flicker in game.

Tune wall movement by surface#

  1. Place Navigation ▸ Wall Jump Surface on the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Resize its rectangle to cover the wall face.
  3. Set Surface type to stone for unchanged player movement, ice for faster sliding without climbing, wood for slightly slower sliding, or metal for slightly faster sliding.
  4. Turn abilities ▸ Allow climb off to prevent cling, climbing and ledge hangs on that wall.
  5. Turn abilities ▸ Allow jump off to prevent wall jumps, including late jumps just after leaving it.
  6. Turn abilities ▸ Allow slide off when the wall should allow no attachment or fall-speed cap; Allow jump still controls jumping from contact.
  7. Set abilities ▸ Require ability to an additional unlock, or leave none to inherit the player's unlocked mechanics.
  8. Set movement ▸ Slide speed to a multiplier of the player's slide cap, with 0 holding still while held toward the wall or -1 inheriting the material preset.
  9. Set movement ▸ Jump force x to a horizontal wall/ledge jump speed in base player pixels per second, or leave -1 to inherit player settings; 0 is valid.
  10. Set movement ▸ Jump force y to a vertical wall/ledge jump speed in base player pixels per second, or leave -1 to inherit player settings; Player Size scales both overrides when Scale movement is on.
  11. Set movement ▸ Facing to 0 for either side, 1 for a right-facing wall or -1 for a left-facing wall.
  12. Edit the commented developer/config/wall_surface_types.lua presets if your game needs different materials; preset permissions can restrict object permissions further.
  13. In Tiled choose File ▸ Export All Maps and Tiled World to opt existing rooms into these fields; Prepare & Check names older surfaces, which inherit all player settings until re-exported.
  14. In Defold choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  17. In the Layout Editor click Export.
  18. Choose Play Game.
  19. Choose an empty save slot.
  20. Test the walls with the relevant abilities unlocked.

Unmarked walls keep normal player movement. Surface permissions only restrict unlocked mechanics; placing a marker never grants an ability. Every surface remains an editable gameobject baked into its room collection.

Place a grapple point or pushable block#

Place Navigation ▸ Grapple Point on the top-level templates object layer for a small hook target. It bakes as an editable static grapple collider, with no script overrides. Pair it with visible anchor art. See Grappling for surfaces, exact shapes and hook-target tuning.

Place Physics ▸ Pushable Block, resize its rectangle and paint its art. Walk against it to push. Hold Interact beside it to grab, then move toward or away to push or pull; release Interact to let go. Set mass and gravity_scale for the body, and move_embedded_tiles to move captured art with it. Use a stable block_id when you need identity; an empty value is generated from the placed object. To hold a pressure plate down, set the plate's allow_world to true so the block's world body can activate it. Both objects become gameobjects in the room collection.

Raise or drain water with a lever#

  1. Place Environment ▸ Water Volume on the templates object layer.
  2. Resize its rectangle to the starting water area.
  3. Set Water id to a unique channel name.
  4. Expand Level.
  5. Set Level mode to switch.
  6. Set Level offset to the height change in tiles (negative drains; positive raises).
  7. Set Level speed in pixels per second (0 changes instantly).
  8. Paint water on a decorative tile layer, including the highest intended level.
  9. Set Level art layer to that layer's exact name.
  10. Place Interaction ▸ Lever on templates.
  11. Choose the Water Volume in the lever's Target ref field.
  12. Tick Persist state on the lever to keep its level after room reloads and saves.
  13. Use File ▸ Export Current Room.
  14. Run Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  15. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  16. Build the game.

Use whole-tile offsets for painted water. Keep floors and other water volumes on separate tile layers: changing a cell affects matching tilemap components on its gameobject. Linked object artwork follows the volume centre; Level art layer is the painted-row option. A Pressure Plate's Target ref can select the same volume; with a positive offset it raises the water while pressed. Choose rise for a flood that resets on safe return or death.

Add an air pocket#

  1. Place Environment ▸ Air Pocket on the top-level templates object layer in Tiled.
  2. Resize its rectangle inside a Water Volume.
  3. Set Air pocket id to an optional unique name.
  4. Expand Air.
  5. Set Refill rate in oxygen per second (-1 uses swim_settings.lua's oxygen_refill_rate).
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Swim into the pocket while underwater to check oxygen refills.

The rectangle is an invisible sensor; add artwork separately if needed. Overlapping pockets use the fastest refill rate. A rate of 0 stops draining without refilling. Prepare & Check warns when a pocket overlaps no Water Volume.

Float a crate on water#

  1. Place Physics ▸ Pushable Block on the top-level templates object layer in Tiled.
  2. Resize its rectangle above a Water Volume.
  3. Expand Motion.
  4. Tick Floats.
  5. Set Float depth to the share below the surface (0 to 1; 0.6 puts 60% underwater).
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Stand on the crate to check it supports the player.
  11. Drain the water with its lever to check the crate follows the surface.

Paint the crate's art inside its rectangle for captured artwork, as for other Pushable Blocks. With Floats off, the block keeps its usual gravity. Outside a water volume's horizontal span or below its bottom, a floating crate also falls normally. Leave room above the full water-level range so it cannot trap the player under a ceiling.

Make lava rise behind the player#

  1. Place Hazard on the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Resize its rectangle to the lava's starting level.
  3. Set Hazard type to lava.
  4. Set On contact to safe_return or kill in Damage and knockback.
  5. Choose rise for Level mode in Level.
  6. Choose room_enter for Rise start to start on the player's first frame in that room.
  7. Set Rise delay in seconds.
  8. Set Rise speed in pixels per second.
  9. Set Rise limit in tiles above the drawn top.
  10. Clear Retract hides tiles in Motion.
  11. Paint lava rows through the full rise area on a decorative tile layer, separate from solid floors.
  12. Set Level art layer to that layer's name.
  13. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Build.

For a switch start, choose signal for Rise start and select the Hazard with the sender's Target ref. The channel is Hazard id. With a level mode set, activate and deactivate control the level; they no longer switch the Hazard off. Deactivate, a safe return, death or a room reload restores the drawn level and stops the rise. A room-enter rise starts only once per room load; another activation can start it again. Rise never replays Persist state. Heat resistance still protects against lava in every contact mode.

To drain lava with a switch, choose switch, set a negative Level offset, and target the Hazard. Level speed is pixels per second (0 is instant); the sender's Persist state restores switched levels. Water Volume uses the same Level fields for rising floods. Whole-tile offsets keep painted rows aligned.

Water & swimming troubleshooting#

Painted water stays put: set Level art layer to the exact decorative layer name and paint cells up to the highest intended level. Rows hide above the surface and return when it rises; this does not generate new painted water.

On water entry, use the console output to identify the active path: Trigger water gate only (without Water entry swim gate or [WATER] Entity entered water) means a trigger armed no swim state; both gate lines with has_swim=false mean the swim ability is locked and the intended sink-and-lock gate is active; both lines with has_swim=true mean swimming is armed and any remaining issue is movement tuning. Current builds also print [SWIM] armed ... for every source, including bare water triggers; paste that line into a bug report.

For a first game, prefer horizontal/vertical moving platforms. path_name requires a correctly named Tiled polyline path and is an advanced option.

Build an elevator across streamed rooms#

Use one elevator rectangle for the cabin and one elevator_node point for every landing or room-seam waypoint. The cabin is world-owned while moving; adjacent room collections can unload normally once the trip ends.

  1. Paint the cabin on the lower room's environment tile layer with its edges on the tile grid.
  2. Place the elevator rectangle around those tiles.
  3. Set the rectangle's elevator_id to a globally unique ID such as mine_lift_a.
  4. Set its start_node_id to the ID you will give the starting floor node.
  5. Place an elevator_node point at the cabin floor, centered horizontally on the shaft.
  6. Set that node's elevator_id to the rectangle's ID.
  7. Set its node_id to the rectangle's start_node_id.
  8. Set its stop to bottom.
  9. Place an elevator_node point at the upper room's floor, centered on the same shaft.
  10. Set that node's elevator_id to the rectangle's ID.
  11. Set its node_id to another unique floor ID.
  12. Set its stop to top.
  13. Paint reciprocal vertical openings on the door layer between each connected room pair.
  14. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room for each edited room.
  15. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  18. Open Layout Editor from the game's title screen.
  19. Rescan the rooms.
  20. Connect the shaft rooms through their matching painted doors.
  21. Click Export.
  22. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  23. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Both endpoint nodes use node_kind=landing. Each door opening must be at least as wide as the cabin plus shaft_clearance. For several openings between the same room pair, validation selects the opening closest to the shaft. A room with no floor still needs a centered elevator_node with node_kind=pass_through and stop=none. Keep all nodes strictly bottom-to-top; a route cannot leave and later re-enter the same room.

Add a middle floor#
  1. Place an elevator_node point at the new floor, centered on the shaft.
  2. Set its elevator_id to the existing cabin's ID.
  3. Set its node_id to a unique floor ID such as mine_mezzanine.
  4. Set its node_kind to landing.
  5. Set its stop to middle.
  6. Set landing_label to an optional floor name.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. Follow the preparation and layout steps above.

Repeat for each additional floor. Middle floors sit strictly between the bottom and top and may share a room with either endpoint. Only room boundaries need painted doors; a floor inside a room needs no extra opening. The cabin's start_node_id may name any landing in its own room. Floor labels pass through the same display-text service as other player text; saved identities keep the source node_id.

Build a tram between side-by-side rooms#
  1. Place an Elevator rectangle around tile-aligned cabin art in Tiled.
  2. Set elevator_id to a unique route ID.
  3. Set route_axis to horizontal.
  4. Set start_node_id to the station where the cabin begins.
  5. Set collision_and_rider ▸ floor_offset to the cabin's walking surface height.
  6. Place an Elevator Node point at that surface, centered on the cabin.
  7. Set its elevator_id to the route ID.
  8. Set its node_id to the cabin's start_node_id.
  9. Set its stop to left or right, matching that route endpoint.
  10. Place the other endpoint's Elevator Node in the adjacent room.
  11. Set its elevator_id to the route ID.
  12. Set its node_id to a unique station ID.
  13. Set its stop to the opposite endpoint.
  14. Place any intermediate Elevator Node points on the same horizontal line.
  15. Set each intermediate point's elevator_id to the route ID.
