  16. Set each intermediate point's node_id to a unique station ID.
  17. Set each intermediate point's stop to middle.
  18. Paint matching left and right openings on each room's door layer.
  19. Set activation ▸ destination_mode on the cabin to direction.
  20. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room for each edited room.
  21. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  22. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  23. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  24. Open Layout Editor from the game's title screen.
  25. Rescan the rooms.
  26. Snap the rooms together through their matching side doors.
  27. Click Export.
  28. Choose Play Game on a fresh save slot.

Hold Left or Right aboard an idle tram to visit the adjacent station. The next and choose modes work as for vertical elevators. All station points share one world Y within shaft_tolerance; room placement establishes their world X order. left and right are aliases of bottom and top, so existing calls and saved endpoint keys still work. Each side opening must contain the whole cabin plus shaft_clearance above and below. Leave enough additional space for the rider; validation checks the cabin, not the rider's headroom. The existing camera ▸ camera_lock_x option locks the lane Y for a horizontal route. The cabin stays world-owned while room collections keep editable markers.

Call the elevator to a floor#
  1. Place a Lever at the floor.
  2. Set its target_id to the cabin's elevator_id or optional trigger_id.
  3. Expand messages.
  4. Set message_on to elevator_call.
  5. Set call_landing to that floor's node_id.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Follow the preparation steps above.

A Strike Switch has the same messages ▸ call_landing field and calls that floor when struck. Calls track the cabin's arrival and do not save a separate switch state. Existing elevator_call_bottom and elevator_call_top messages still call the shaft ends.

Choose how riders travel#
  1. Select the cabin's Elevator rectangle.
  2. Expand activation.
  3. Set destination_mode to next or direction.
  4. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. Follow the preparation steps above.

next boards toward the next floor in the travel direction, reversing at the ends. direction waits for the rider to hold Up or Down while the cabin is idle at a floor. The held direction selects the adjacent floor; it cannot travel past the shaft ends.

Let the rider choose a floor#
  1. Select the cabin's Elevator rectangle in Tiled.
  2. Expand activation.
  3. Set destination_mode to choose.
  4. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  8. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  9. Click Export.

Boarding waits in this mode. Press Interact aboard the idle cabin to open Choose a floor. The current floor is marked You are here and cannot be selected. Select another floor to close the paused list and ride there. Floor names come from each node's landing_label, falling back to node_id. Choose mode supplies its own interaction even when require_interact is off. External floor calls still work.

Prepare & Check validates the route identities and cabin art together, cuts the cabin tiles out of the source room tilemap, and creates a persistent tilemap, collision child, collection, factory, and generated route index. Prepare & Check then resolves that index against the exported layout and rejects non-adjacent rooms, one-way or horizontal links, misaligned shafts, and openings that are too narrow. At runtime the elevator pins the ordered route while travelling, commits room ownership exactly at each door seam, and gives the camera a temporary shaft-scoped focus. It releases both streaming and camera leases on arrival, cancel, player death, reset, or a dead cabin owner.

If the player leaves the cabin during a ride, the camera returns to the player at once and room streaming follows the player again. The cabin continues to its destination and keeps its route loaded until arrival. A hop that stays over the cabin keeps the ride camera; getting back on during the same trip uses the normal player-follow camera.

Hidden elevator and elevator_node objects are excluded, including markers inside a hidden parent group. This is useful for parking an unfinished draft route, but never hide a node that a visible controller names as its start_node_id; Prepare & Check reports that reference as an error. Generated cabin assets live only in reserved _mvtk_elevators/ subdirectories below the generated, collections, and factories folders. Prepare & Check reconciles those folders against the current manifest, so deleting or renaming an elevator does not leave an old cabin factory behind.

Choose activation_mode=on_board for the usual Metroidvania behavior; require_interact=true changes that mode into a button press while the player is aboard. automatic continuously shuttles through the landings (and, when require_rider=true, only schedules trips while at least one player collider is aboard). trigger_only moves only when its elevator_id or optional trigger_id receives an elevator call. The route is ping-pong. Direction mode always waits for an aboard rider to hold Up or Down, including with automatic activation. Choose mode waits for a floor choice, including with automatic activation. With the persistence defaults enabled, only completed floor keys are saved and restored; an interrupted mid-shaft position is never written to the save.

Keep floor_offset equal to the rectangle height when the walking surface is the cabin's top edge (the 32-pixel template defaults already match). If you resize the cabin vertically, update this value too; use a smaller value only when the walkable floor is intentionally inside the captured art. The co_y_offset and sensor_y_offset fields are fine collision-tuning controls, not substitutes for floor_offset.

The baked landing marker receives elevator_doors_closing, elevator_departed, elevator_incoming, elevator_arrived, elevator_doors_opening, and elevator_trip_completed messages while its room is loaded. Those are extension hooks for a custom animated shaft-door prefab; the elevator templates themselves use the painted shaft opening and do not create or permanently unlock an inventory/ability door.

If generation stops, fix the first reported route error in Tiled. Common causes are an off-grid cabin rectangle, no painted tiles under it, duplicate IDs, missing bottom/top stops, a node that is not at the cabin centerline, a start node in another room, non-reciprocal up/down room links, or a shaft opening narrower than the cabin. Do not hand-edit elevators_generated.lua or any generated cabin resource, including files below _mvtk_elevators/.

Tune climb_offset_x in small 8-16 px steps and keep its absolute value within roughly half the ladder rectangle's width. The player's collision body moves with the offset, so a large value can push it into nearby walls or outside the trigger box; after jumping off, the player must re-enter that box to grab the ladder again. If the rig is off-center on every ladder, fix the art or an animation_overrides setting and reserve this property for individual spots.

Phase-gated arena hazards#

  1. Place environment/hazard.tx inside your Boss Arena.
  2. Set arena_ref to the Boss Arena object.
  3. Set arena.active_phases to 2 for a phase-two hazard.
  4. Tick arena.hide_when_inactive.
  5. Set arena.warn_time to 0.6 for a harmless warning blink.
  6. Export the room with File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.

Boss phase 1 is the first fight phase. Empty arena.active_phases means the whole active fight. arena.arena_id and arena.boss_id are string alternatives to the object links. Set boss_ref to follow one named boss and disappear at that boss's defeat; an arena-only link lasts until the arena unlocks after its cleanup delay. Arena links ignore Level mode hazards; clear those links for a rising or switch-controlled liquid. Switch and pattern toggles keep their existing art and collision. Retracted hazards stay retracted when shown.

The gate hides the collision, sprite, linked presentation and moving art. Painted tiles on motion.retract_visual_layer hide too when motion.retract_hides_tiles is ticked. Use a decorative layer; keep solid floors separate. Check that no painted tiles show before the fight, the warning holds while paused, and Analyze Progression lists no barrier for the linked hazard.

Build a compact boss encounter#

Build the encounter with the boss templates:

  1. Place a boss_arena rectangle over the fight space. Give it an arena_id, such as guardian_arena, and choose camera/locking behavior.
  2. Place one boss point inside. Give it a boss_id, such as guardian, and set its arena_id to guardian_arena.
  3. Place boss_phase point objects for authored boss phases. Give each the same boss_id/arena_id, set phase, health range, attacks, and movement profile.
  4. Optionally place boss_phase_trigger rectangles when entering an area should force a boss phase.
  5. Optionally place boss_weak_point rectangles and link them with the same boss_id.
  6. Optionally place arena_boundary rectangles to seal an arena or change it by boss phase.
  7. Optionally place boss_add_spawner points for enemies called in during selected boss phases.

Use consistent IDs across every object. Start with one arena, one boss, and one boss phase. Add triggers/weak points only after the basic encounter validates.

Bar name (arena_and_ui.bar_name): the name on the boss's health bar; blank uses the object Name unless it is blank, "Boss" or "Boss Spawn", then the preset's name when set, then the boss id. Translate it through Project ▸ Translations…. A shared bar keeps its Boss Arena group_name; with that blank, it uses the lead boss's resolved name.

Put two or three bosses in one arena#

  1. Place a Boss Arena rectangle over the fight space in Tiled.
  2. Select the Boss Arena.
  3. Set arena_id in Custom Properties to a unique identity, such as duo_arena.
  4. Place a Boss point inside the rectangle.
  5. Set its boss_id to a unique identity.
  6. Set its arena_ref to the Boss Arena object, or set arena_id to duo_arena.
  7. Expand arena_and_ui.
  8. Leave arena_lock ticked.
  9. Place a second Boss inside the same rectangle.
  10. Give the second Boss a different boss_id.
  11. Link the second Boss to the same Boss Arena with arena_ref or arena_id.
  12. Expand the second Boss's arena_and_ui.
  13. Leave its arena_lock ticked.
  14. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  15. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  17. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  18. In the Layout Editor, click Export.
  19. Play on an empty save slot.
  20. Enter the Boss Arena to start both bosses.
  21. Defeat one boss and check that only its bar fades while the doors stay shut and music continues.
  22. Defeat the other boss and check that the arena clears and one defeat scene plays.

A third Boss uses the same arena link and its own boss_id. With separate health, each boss has its own health and stagger gauge. The bars share the existing HUD band; slot_gap and max_bars in developer/config/boss_ui.lua control their spacing and count (up to three). Prepare & Check warns when a group exceeds that count. Boss bars take priority over champion bars.

A group saves every member's rewards and defeat flags together when the last boss falls. Dying after one member falls brings back the whole group. Bosses with arena_lock off stay independent. The lead boss (first by placed id) plays its intro scene and supplies the music; use the same music for all members, or leave their music blank and set the Boss Arena's battle_music. At the end, one defeat scene plays: the last defeated member that has a scene supplies it. Boss phase labels update separately; arena pieces follow the highest living boss phase, and the arena's own boss phase only advances.

To add a stagger punish window:

  1. Reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled with File ▸ Open File or Project... to refresh the template fields.
  2. Select the Boss object.
  3. Expand combat in its custom properties.
  4. Set stagger_threshold to a positive value, such as 30.
  5. Set stagger_time to the punish window's length in seconds, or leave -1 to inherit the preset or project default.
  6. Set stagger_damage_mult to the incoming damage multiplier during that window, or leave -1 to inherit.
  7. Set parry_stagger to the pressure from a successful parry, or leave -1 to inherit.
  8. Set stagger_decay_per_s to the pressure lost per second after the configured delay, or leave -1 to inherit.
  9. Select a Boss Phase object that should resist stagger.
  10. Expand attack_sequence.
  11. Tick stagger_immune for that boss phase.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. In the Layout Editor, click Export.
  17. Play on an empty save slot.
  18. Hit the boss to fill the gauge below its health bar.
  19. Check that the immune boss phase never staggers.
  20. Check the gauge in both Wide screens framings.

The five Boss combat number fields use -1 to inherit. A stagger_threshold of 0 turns stagger off; all shipped boss presets keep it off. The threshold scales with boss health difficulty. A boss phase without stagger_immune clears the previous boss phase's immunity. Parries add pressure without reducing boss health; damage over time adds no pressure and bypasses absorb shields. Put absorb_shield or counter_stance in a Boss Phase's attacks list to enable those attack patterns.

Gauge appearance is configured in developer/config/boss_ui.lua through show_stagger_gauge, color_stagger, stagger_bar_height and stagger_offset_y. Turning off the gauge leaves stagger combat enabled. The alternate boss_hp.gui scene ignores update_boss_stagger and shows health only; use boss_health_bar.gui for a stagger gauge in each boss bar.

Changes under developer/config, developer/ui or hooks make the publication stale. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

Boss presentation fields live under the Boss template's visuals group:

Field What to choose
boss_visual_type Sprite, or Spine with the add-on enabled.
visual_component The registered component for this boss's art.
Intro animation (intro_animation) none starts idle immediately. Choose roar, taunt, or another boss animation key for a one-shot entrance, then idle.

Animation keys resolve through developer/config/boss_animations.lua and the selected sprite atlas's aliases. A missing sprite intro clip starts idle. Prepare & Check warns about unknown Intro animation values. Use the standard export, preparation and playtest steps after changing a boss's presentation.

boss_arena supplies the arena rectangle and entry-lock behavior.

Boss Manager and Boss Arena share one door-lock family. Other fights can hold their own locks; the doors open after every family releases the room. Death clears every family and fight seal. Fast travel (when Block in boss fights is enabled), Warp Statues and hazard returns respect fight confinement. Teleporters, elevators and Save Points keep their usual behavior during a sealed fight. Save Points can still save and rest; departing through their fast-travel service uses the fast-travel blocking setting.

To seal an arena or change it by boss phase:

  1. Place an Arena Boundary rectangle inside the Boss Arena and resize it to the wall's collision area.
  2. Give it a unique boundary_id.
  3. Set arena_ref to the Boss Arena object, or expand links and type its arena_id.
  4. Expand activation and leave enabled_when_locked on to block only while the fight is locked.
  5. Leave active_phases empty for the whole fight, or enter boss phase numbers such as 1,2 to block only in those boss phases.
  6. For a wall that follows a live boss even without an arena lock, turn enabled_when_locked off.
  7. For an unlinked wall, set start_enabled to choose whether it blocks.
  8. In Tiled, choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  9. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. In the Layout Editor, click Export.
  13. Play on a fresh save slot and check the wall during each boss phase and after victory.

Boundaries are invisible static collision objects baked into the room collection. Their boss phase follows the live boss even if the arena's phases count is 1. After victory, linked boundaries open.

Call in enemies during a fight

  1. Place a Boss Add Spawner point where adds should appear.
  2. Give it a unique spawner_id.
  3. Set the boss link with boss_ref, or with links.boss_id.
  4. For an arena-only link, set arena_ref to the Boss Arena instead, or enter its arena_id in links.
  5. Choose enemy_type from the Enemy dropdown.
  6. Expand spawning.
  7. Leave active_phases empty for the whole fight, or enter boss phase numbers such as 2,3.
  8. Set spawn_interval to the seconds between batches.
  9. Set spawn_count to the number of enemies per batch.
  10. Set spawn_radius to the spread around the point; 0 uses the point exactly.
  11. Set max_alive to the living-add cap; 0 removes this cap.
  12. Set max_total to the fight's total-add limit; 0 removes this limit.
  13. Turn despawn_on_defeat off only if surviving adds should remain after the fight.
  14. Expand movement_and_attacks.
  15. Choose movement, or leave type_default to use the enemy's movement.
  16. Choose attack_pattern for the first attack slot, or leave type_default.
  17. Choose attack_pattern2 for the second attack slot, or leave type_default.
  18. In Tiled, choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  19. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  20. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  21. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  22. In the Layout Editor, click Export.
  23. Play on a fresh save slot.
  24. Check that adds appear only during the listed boss phases and never exceed max_alive.
  25. Check that adds use the chosen movement and attacks.
  26. Defeat the boss.
  27. Check that remaining adds disappear unless despawn_on_defeat is off.

start_delay delays the first batch; 0 waits one spawn interval. start_active controls whether the spawner runs. The stats group multiplies enemy health, damage and speed; the selected difficulty profile applies once afterward. An arena-only link follows the live boss's phase, even when the arena's phases count is 1. Surviving adds still follow room-exit, room-unload and player-death cleanup. Prepare & Check reports missing boss or arena links.

Boss summon attacks create real enemies. Summons share a limit of four living enemies per boss and drop nothing by default. Tune summon_max_active and summon_drops in developer/config/boss_defaults.lua, or set max_active and drops on an individual summon attack in developer/config/attack_patterns.lua. Enemies created during play disappear when you leave their room, die, or end their boss fight. developer/config/enemy_settings.lua can keep them on room exit; room unload and fight cleanup still remove them. Placed Tiled enemies remain baked, editable gameobjects in their room collections.

Boss movement and charge difficulty is authored directly on those placed objects:

  • On boss, expand attack_tuning to set the base chase_speed, charge_speed, charge_distance, charge_telegraph_time, charge_duration, charge_cooldown, and charge_recovery.
  • On boss_phase, use attack_sequence for the boss phase attack list, attack interval/range, and optional charge overrides. Use movement for the boss phase chase_speed and speed_multiplier. A boss phase override wins over the boss value; the speed multiplier is then applied to movement and charge speed.
  • On boss_arena, expand encounter and set charge_edge_padding to keep a charge endpoint away from the rectangle's left and right edges.

The optional tuning fields default to -1, meaning inherit. Leave them at -1 when that object should use its boss, preset, or attack-pattern value. After changing any of these properties, save the TMX, run File ▸ Export Current Room, and run Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.

Leave the map's room-wide room_zone at exploration or platforming. Do not select the old boss_arena room preset: it can lock the camera from the room's entry position. The placed boss_arena rectangle is the supported owner of the fight's lock and camera behavior.

Share one health bar#

  1. Select the Boss Arena in Tiled.
  2. Expand health_bar in Custom Properties.
  3. Tick shared_health (Shared health).
  4. Set group_name (Group name) to the name shown above the shared bar, or leave it blank to use the lead boss name.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. In the Layout Editor, click Export.
  10. Play on an empty save slot.
  11. Hit either boss and check that the same health bar drains.
  12. Empty the health bar and check that both bosses fall together.

Each boss keeps its own defenses, hit invulnerability and stagger pressure. The shared bar shows no stagger gauge. Two quick hits against different bosses can both drain the pool.

Make the survivor enrage#

  1. Leave shared_health clear on the Boss Arena.
  2. Place a Boss Phase point in Tiled.
  3. Set boss_ref to the boss that should survive.
  4. Set arena_ref to its Boss Arena.
  5. Set phase to a boss phase above its starting boss phase.
  6. Expand health_thresholds.
  7. Tick partner_defeated (Partner defeated).
  8. Expand transition.
  9. Set transition_type to rage.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  13. In the Layout Editor, click Export.
  14. Play on an empty save slot.
  15. Defeat the other boss and check that the survivor enters the authored boss phase.
  16. Damage the survivor and check that losing health keeps that boss phase active.

Health never starts a partner-defeated boss phase by itself. With shared health, all members fall together, so a partner-defeated boss phase does not start.

Cutscene triggers#

  1. Place Cutscene Trigger from tiled/templates/interaction/cutscene.tx on the room's top-level templates layer.
  2. Resize its rectangle to cover the entry area.
  3. For dialogue, set Content ▸ Cutscene dialogue id to a key from developer/config/narrative/dialogues.lua.
  4. For video, set Content ▸ Video id to a key from developer/config/narrative/videos.lua. Content also controls video audio, timeout and fades. Preparation generates fade to black, wait, fade back, video and dialogue, in that order; empty content steps are omitted.
  5. Set Cutscene id only when you need a fixed sequence name. With blank content it selects a sequence in developer/config/narrative/cutscenes.lua; with content it names the generated sequence. Blank gives each object an independent tiled_cutscene_<room>_<object id> entry. cutscenes_generated.lua is maintained by preparation.
  6. Set Lifecycle ▸ Trigger once for the repetition you want. It defaults to on; off allows repeated firing while the zone remains enabled.
  7. Set Lifecycle ▸ Consume on fire for the lifetime you want. It defaults to on; off keeps the zone enabled after firing. Replay requires both Trigger once and Consume on fire off.
  8. Set Lifecycle ▸ Persist once for the save behavior you want. It defaults to on and remembers the once state after room reloads and loading a save. Blank Trigger id gives each placed object its own once key; Once flag optionally supplies an explicit key.
  9. Set Conditions and outcomes ▸ Requires flag if the scene needs a prerequisite.
  10. Set Conditions and outcomes ▸ Skip if flag if another flag should suppress the scene.
  11. Set Conditions and outcomes ▸ Set flag if firing should record an outcome.
  12. Export the room with File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  13. Run Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  14. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check to rebuild scenes and check their cutscene, dialogue and video ids.
  15. Select Project ▸ Build.
  16. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  17. Play on a fresh save slot. Scenes pause gameplay until their steps finish.
  18. Enter each trigger to check its own scene.
  19. Re-enter each trigger to check the repetition settings.
  20. Load the save slot to check the saved once state.

Opening and boss scenes, skipping and video clips#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… in Defold.
  2. Select the Game tab.
  3. Set Cutscenes ▸ Opening cutscene to a scene id from developer/config/narrative/cutscenes.lua or a generated Cutscene Trigger. Blank disables the opening; it plays once per fresh save after startup is ready. Movement test previews omit it.
  4. Set Allow skipping for the whole game.
  5. Set Skip hold time (seconds) to the continuous hold you want, from 0.2 to 5 seconds.
  6. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  7. Select the boss in Tiled.
  8. Set Presentation ▸ Defeat cutscene to its scene id. The scene starts after cleanup unlocks the arena and plays once per saved boss identity.
  9. Select a Cutscene Trigger whose generated scene must play in full.
  10. Clear Playback ▸ Skippable for that scene. For a manual sequence, use { skippable = false, steps = { ... } } in developer/config/narrative/cutscenes.lua; ordinary step arrays remain valid. Dialogue that sets flags or offers choices always prevents skipping, and Prepare & Check lists those scenes.
  11. Place video clips in developer/assets/cutscenes/common/. Keep README.txt at the parent folder root, outside the bundle.
  12. Add each clip to developer/config/narrative/videos.lua with editor_uri including common/ and uri relative to the bundle root. Bundling copies the contents of common/ to that root.
  13. To offer translated or subtitled clips, add a variants table inside the clip: language-code keys such as es, subtitled, or es_subtitled. Each variant supplies editor_uri and uri like the base clip. Optional timeout and play_sound override the base clip; omitted values use its settings. Variants are optional and increase the exported game size.
  14. Set Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility ▸ Captions for the starting Captions choice (default off); saved player choices win. With Captions on, playback tries the current language's subtitled variant, its language variant, the generic subtitled variant, then the base clip. With Captions off, it tries the language variant then base. Explicit cutscene-step uri/editor_uri values bypass variants. Editor runs prefer editor_uri, then uri; exported games use uri.
  15. Burn subtitles into those variant files. Captions are never drawn over a playing video, because the video player covers the game window.
  16. Set fallback_title, fallback_caption and optionally fallback_seconds in the clip's videos.lua entry. These are your translatable source title and description on a dim card when video is unavailable or fails, including a failure after playback starts. Duration defaults to 3 seconds. When both texts are blank (the default), the scene simply continues. A normal video finish or failsafe timeout continues without the card.
  17. Set skip_action in developer/config/narrative/cutscene_settings.lua if you want a different existing action from default_bindings.lua. Avoid Pause and Settings; the hint follows the action's remapped button. Native video hides the hint.
  18. Export the boss room with File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  19. Run Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  20. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  21. Select Project ▸ Build.
  22. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  23. Start a fresh save slot to check the opening.
  24. Tap Jump during scene dialogue to advance a line.
  25. Hold Jump to skip an eligible scene. Releasing it must leave the player still.
  26. Choose Resume after pausing a scene to check that gameplay stays paused until the scene ends.
  27. Defeat the boss to check its scene.
  28. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check again before exporting a game copy.
  29. Select Project ▸ Export Game. Its EXPORT_MANIFEST.txt lists the clip under common/.
  30. Open the exported game.project in Defold.
  31. Select Project ▸ Fetch Libraries.
  32. Select Project ▸ Bundle ▸ Windows Application….
  33. Play the bundled game to check the clip.

Captions#

Add caption steps in developer/config/narrative/cutscenes.lua, alongside fades, waits and dialogue. Both ordinary step arrays and { steps = { ... } } scenes accept them:

arrival = {
    { type = "caption", text = "Beyond the gate...", speaker = "", t = 3.0, wait = true },
    { type = "caption", text = "We made it.", speaker = "Ada", t = 3.0, wait = true },
},

text and the optional speaker are translatable source text. t is the duration in seconds, defaulting to 3. wait defaults to true and holds the scene until that time passes; wait = false continues immediately while the caption remains for its duration or until the scene ends. A later scene caption replaces it. Skipping or finishing the scene clears it. Caption steps always show, like dialogue, even with Captions off.

The line sits above the skip hint and below dialogue height, on top of fades. Keep each caption short enough for the centred band, including at 150 % Text size. The backdrop fits the measured text and wraps longer lines.

Players use Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility ▸ Captions (On/Off) and Caption background (Off/Dim/Solid), just after Vibration. Captions governs sound captions and subtitled clip selection. Text size scales the caption line. Scene captions take priority over sound captions.

With the game stopped, Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility ▸ Captions, after Assist, sets Captions and Caption background for players without saved choices. Players can change captions shows or hides both player rows; hiding them preserves saved choices. The commented captions block in developer/config/accessibility.lua holds the baseline values.

Sound effects listed under sfx in developer/config/sound_bank.lua can carry an optional caption = "Jump" field. Use short source text describing the sound; it is translated when displayed. Only sound_bank ids are covered, including sounds requested directly with play_sfx. Sounds played by component URL (such as boss, enemy-behaviour, breakable-wall and pickup sounds) have no bank id and are outside this caption system. An enemy hurt/death sound using a bank id can carry a caption.

The creator switch captions.sound in developer/config/accessibility.lua allows sound captions (default true). They show only when the player's Captions choice is On. sound_seconds sets the line duration (default 2.5 seconds); sound_repeat_gap sets the minimum gap for the same id (default 4 seconds). There is one live sound line and no queue: other sounds during it are discarded. Scene captions take priority. Add a caption to an entry used in play (for example jump), run Project ▸ Prepare & Check, then Build and Play Game. Check it with Captions On and Off. Project ▸ Translations… ▸ Export Text for Translation also collects these sound captions.

Captions never draw over a playing video. Subtitled clips are separate video variants with subtitles burned into the picture; see the video steps above. After changing a scene or creator defaults, click Save and Prepare & Check in Game Settings, then Build and test the opening on an empty save slot. Project ▸ Translations… ▸ Export Text for Translation collects caption text and speakers from the authored scenes.

Add NPCs, signs, hints, and dialogue#

  • sign: use inline title/text for the simplest readable sign.
  • hint_stone: use for short contextual guidance. Its Goal hints group shows the current goal's hint instead (see "Point the player at the current goal with a Hint Stone").
  • npc: set a stable npc_id, display name, and inline dialogue or a dialogue_id.
  • npc: Appearance ▸ Facing -1 flips its art to face left; 1 keeps the art as drawn. Draw NPC art facing right, or flip a left-drawn image with Tiled's horizontal flip.
  • dialogue_trigger: resize the trigger area and set the dialogue_id; tune one-shot/flag behavior if needed.
  • lore_item: use for collectible story text.

Create reusable conversations, readable text and story flags in the dialogue window:

  1. In Defold, open Project ▸ Dialogue Editor….
  2. Enter a stable id in New sequence id.
  3. Click New sequence.
  4. Click Add line.
  5. Enter the speaker in Speaker.
  6. Enter the dialogue in Text.
  7. Choose Condition if the line needs a flag, ability or quest check.
  8. Click Add choice if the player should choose a response.
  9. Enter the choice's Text.
  10. Choose Goto if the choice should open another sequence.
  11. Click Use line.
  12. Click Save.
  13. In Tiled, set the NPC or dialogue trigger's Dialogue id to your sequence id.
  14. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  15. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The commented developer/config/narrative/dialogues.lua remains the Lua reference. Window saves go to developer/config/narrative/dialogues_authored.lua; an entry with the same id replaces the whole reference sequence. Select an override and click Revert to dialogues.lua, then Save, to restore its reference version. Delete removes only sequences created in the window. Used by N objects reports references; deleting a referenced sequence asks for confirmation. Changing dialogue text also changes its translation key: export text again from Project ▸ Translations…. Build to play saved changes; run Prepare & Check before Export Game, since the publication is stale.

Use {input:jump} in sign, hint, lore and dialogue text to name the current button. "Jump with {input:jump}" reads "Jump with Space" after keyboard use and "Jump with A", "Jump with Cross" or "Jump with B" on the selected controller theme. Names are Settings Controls row ids or lower-case action names; {input:dash|both} shows both key and pad. Unknown tokens stay as typed and warn once. Write holds as plain words: "Hold {input:grapple}".

Turn an NPC into a vendor#

NPC vendors share the purchase catalogs, stock and menu used by shops. A vendor's menu title is the NPC's display name. Supply Art item or linked Object Art for its appearance; choosing a preset does not supply baked NPC art.

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Place Interaction ▸ NPC from tiled/templates/interaction/npc.tx.
  3. Set Npc id to a stable, unique id.
  4. Set Npc name to the display name and shop title.
  5. Choose Vendor ▸ Vendor shop from the catalog dropdown.
  6. Set Vendor ▸ Vendor flag to a saved story flag if trading needs an unlock.
  7. Choose Vendor ▸ Vendor greeting: first_visit, every_visit or never.
  8. Enter Dialogue ▸ Dialogue text for a short greeting, or set Dialogue id to a reusable sequence.
  9. Set Art item to your NPC artwork.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  15. Click Export.
  16. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  17. Press Interact beside the NPC.

With a Vendor flag, the NPC talks normally until that flag is true. A set_flag = "met_merchant" action on a dialogue choice or a flag:met_merchant grant from any reward can unlock a vendor whose Vendor flag is met_merchant. A Sign's Conditions and persistence ▸ Set flag can unlock it too. first_visit greets once after unlocking and opens the shop when the dialogue closes normally. Later visits open directly. every_visit greets each time; never, or no dialogue, opens directly. Leaving the room or a rejected dialogue opens no shop and does not use up the first greeting. Greeting state is saved with the NPC id; keep that id stable after release.

The merchant and alchemist choices in Npc type supply their catalog, name and greeting from developer/config/npc_types.lua. Vendor shop = none keeps that preset's catalog; on a preset without a catalog it leaves a normal NPC. NPC names and dialogue remain source text collected by Project ▸ Translations….

Show a marker when an NPC has something new#

An NPC can show ! above its head while it has lines the player has not heard, and ? when those lines start or turn in a quest. Talking clears it. When a story flag, quest or ability changes what the NPC would say, the marker comes back. Give the NPC a dialogue whose lines use conditions, such as the commented living_guide_sample in developer/config/narrative/dialogues.lua.

  1. In Tiled, select an NPC on the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Set Dialogue ▸ Dialogue id to a sequence whose lines have a condition.
  3. Tick Behavior ▸ Show new dialogue marker.
  4. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  8. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  9. Walk to the NPC and check that ! shows above it.
  10. Press Interact and read the conversation to the end.
  11. Check that the ! has gone.

To give every NPC of one type a marker, set show_new_dialogue_marker = true on that type in developer/config/npc_types.lua; a ticked box turns it on for one NPC. Reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled after updating the toolkit so the Behavior group appears.

  • Game Settings ▸ Game ▸ NPCs ▸ New dialogue marker turns every marker off or on.
  • The marker texts are markers.new_text and markers.task_text in developer/config/npc_settings.lua; Project ▸ Translations… collects them.
  • Pressing Back on the first line still counts as heard.
  • An NPC whose shop opens directly, with no greeting, shows no marker.
  • What each NPC last said is saved with the slot. Loading an older save can show a marker again for a conversation held after that save. New Game+ starts with every NPC unheard.
  • Editing a conversation's text shows its marker once more in existing saves.
  • Each marker is a world label. Prepare & Check warns above 12 marker NPCs in one room (npc_living_many) and when a marked NPC has no dialogue (marker_no_dialogue).
  • The npc_met event fires after the first conversation with each NPC in a save; achievements can count it.

Make a town feel alive#

An NPC can say short ambient lines when the player walks near, turn toward the player and pace a short stretch of floor. Everything holds while the game is paused, in a conversation or in a menu.

  1. In Tiled, select an NPC on the top-level templates object layer.
  2. Tick Behavior ▸ Face player.
  3. Enter Behavior ▸ Barks as short lines separated by |, such as Mind the gate.|Lovely day..
  4. Set Behavior ▸ Wander width to the pixels of floor to pace, such as 128.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  10. Walk toward the NPC and check that a line appears above it.
  11. Walk away and check that the line hides.
  12. Stand next to the NPC and check that it stops and turns to you.
  • Bark range (default 96 px) is how near the player must be for a line; Bark cooldown (default 8 s) is the wait after a line hides. Lines never repeat back to back.
  • Leave Barks blank to use the type's barks = { "...", "..." } list in developer/config/npc_types.lua. Project ▸ Translations… collects every line.
  • An NPC showing the new-dialogue marker does not bark until you have talked to it.
  • Wander width paces half the width either side of the placed spot at Wander speed (default 30 px a second), pausing at each end for wander.pause_min to wander.pause_max seconds from developer/config/npc_settings.lua. It stops while the player stands within the NPC's interaction range.
  • Wandering has no physics: keep the stretch flat and clear, or the NPC floats over gaps and slopes. Prepare & Check warns when the line leaves the room (wander_outside_room).
  • Turning and pacing assume the NPC's art is drawn facing right; flip a left-drawn image with Tiled's horizontal flip. The art keeps its animation while walking.
  • Game Settings ▸ Game ▸ NPCs ▸ Ambient lines turns every line off or on, and Line time sets how long each stays up.
  • Each barking NPC carries a world label: Prepare & Check counts it with marker NPCs for the npc_living_many warning, and warns about a changed Bark range with no lines (barks_no_text).

Keep a record of people#

Journal ▸ People lists the characters the player has talked to, with a short bio and the last thing each one said. A person is an NPC type in developer/config/npc_types.lua, so every NPC of that type, in any room, is the same person.

  1. Open developer/config/npc_types.lua.
  2. Find the type your NPC uses, such as desert_hermit.
  3. Add journal = { title = "The Hermit", bio = "Lives alone in the dunes.", order = 10 }, to that type.
  4. Save the file.
  5. In Tiled, select an NPC on the top-level templates object layer.
  6. Set its npc_type to that type.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Game Settings….
  9. Select Progression.
  10. Tick People in the People section.
  11. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  13. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.
  14. Open the pause menu.
  15. Choose Journal.
  16. Select People and check that the person shows ???.
  17. Close the menus.
  18. Talk to the NPC and read the conversation to the end.
  19. Open Journal ▸ People again.
  20. Select the person to read the bio and Last said: line.
  • title is the shown name; leave it out to use the type's name. order sorts the list, lowest first; ties sort by title.
  • Untick Show people not met to hide people until the first talk.
  • The header counts people met out of everyone with a journal block.
  • Project ▸ Translations… collects each title and bio. The last line is the dialogue's own text, so it translates with the dialogue.
  • An NPC with no npc_type, or a type without a journal block, is never listed.
  • People is saved with the slot and starts empty on a new save and on New Game+.
  • Prepare & Check warns (people_unplaced) when no exported room places an NPC of a type with a journal block, because that person can never be met.
  • The tab stays hidden while People is off or no type has a journal block.

Add switches and stations#

For a gate that counts switches in any order:

  1. Place Interaction ▸ Gate on the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Set gate_type to staged_gate.
  3. Enter the comma-separated flag names in stages ▸ stage_flags, for example seal_a,seal_b,seal_c.
  4. Set stages ▸ stages_required to the number needed (0 means all listed; -1 uses the preset, which defaults to all).
  5. Place a Lever for each flag, in this room or another room.
  6. Set each lever's conditions_and_flags ▸ set_flag_on to its corresponding flag name.
  7. Leave each lever's target_id blank.
  8. Set a lever's switching ▸ toggle to false if it should be momentary.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room for each changed room.
  10. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.

A Pressure Plate's response ▸ set_flag can supply a stage flag too. Flags count once set; switching off never clears them. Partial staged progress survives room reloads and saves, unlike an ordered sequence gate's partial progress, which resets on room reload. The gate opens on the last required flag and saves its opening with state_and_persistence ▸ persist_unlock on. Its proximity hint shows n/N. Prepare & Check warns about an empty list or unwritten flag and reports an error when the count exceeds the list length.

  • Link pressure_plate and lever to supported targets with matching IDs.
  • Use health_station and energy_station for refill interactions.
  • Use map_station to reveal/update map information.

Start with unique lowercase snake_case IDs. Copy-pasted objects must not keep an ID that is supposed to be unique.

Add a positional sound#

  1. Place Audio/Visual ▸ Sound Source on the room's top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Resize its rectangle to cover the sound's full-volume area.
  3. Set Ambience id to a looping sound registered in developer/config/ambient_sounds.lua and configured on developer/game/audio/audio_controller.go.
  4. Set Positional audio ▸ Falloff end to the fade distance in pixels beyond the rectangle edge.
  5. Set Mixing ▸ Gain to the maximum volume for this source.
  6. Choose an id that no area-mode Ambience Zone or room/world ambient sound uses; positional sources may share an id and use the loudest source's gain.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. In Defold choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.

An existing Ambience Zone can use Positional audio ▸ Positional for the same distance mixing. Leave it off for overlap playback and music ducking. Positional mode ignores music ducking, Stop on exit and Trigger once. developer/config/positional_audio.lua sets the camera/player listener, fade speed and stereo strength. Pan distance 0 follows half the visible world width, including wider windows. Two quiet frames stop a loop below the configured gain threshold; unloading its last source releases it.

Add ambience, particles, camera, and screen effects#

  • Set room-wide music with the map music file property.
  • music_trigger changes registered tracks inside a rectangle.
  • ambience_zone blends a registered ambient sound inside a rectangle.
  • particle_emitter plays a registered effect at a point/area.
  • camera_zone changes camera follow, locks, focus, zoom, or fitting within its rectangle.
  • screen_effect_zone applies the supported screen treatment while the player is inside.
  • map_marker adds a point of interest to the map.

Music files go under developer/assets/music/ after sync. Sound effects and ambient files are registered through the project's audio/config resources. Use Prepare & Check after changing music; music synchronization is included.

Combat music#

Combat music ships off. It adds a fight track above room music; boss, champion and encounter music take precedence. A music trigger ends it until the next room. Pausing freezes combat timing and combat fades. Audio is unverified until heard in the running game.

By default, only living enemies the player can see within range in the current room count toward engagement. Walls and platforms, including one-way platforms, block sight. Only enemies the player can see in this section is on by default; untick it to count enemies through walls. Accepted player hits still start combat music even when the enemy is hidden.

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Select Game.
  3. Tick Combat music in the Combat music section after Cutscenes.
  4. Enter a registered track id in Combat track.
  5. Set Quiet delay (seconds) to the desired delay before room music returns.
  6. Click Save.
  7. Optionally add a map property named combat_music of type music_tracks in Tiled's Map ▸ Map Properties.
  8. Choose a registered track, inherit for the project default, or none to disable combat music in that room.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled for any changed room.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Start Play Game on an empty save slot.

New rooms made with File ▸ new_room_blank already have combat_music. Reopen the Tiled project to load the updated room command. Older rooms need steps 7–9 only when they need an override. For additional tuning, the commented developer/config/combat_music.lua controls proximity radius, enemy count, minimum engagement time, player hits, spawned enemies and fade times. Both fade times must be at least 0.5 seconds. Only living enemies in the current room that the player can see count by default. Changing configuration or Tiled extensions stales the publication: run Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

Light a dark room#

  1. Place Abilities ▸ Ability Pickup on the top-level templates object layer in Tiled.
  2. Set its Ability id to lantern.
  3. Set the Tiled map's darkness float from 0 (clear) to 1 (fully dark in Light mask mode, with only lights revealing the art). Glow only stops at 0.9. Rooms without the property use visual.default_room_darkness in developer/config/game_config.lua.
  4. Add a Bool map property named requires_light set to true to every room the player cannot cross without light, including every darkness-1 room without an always-lit route.
  5. Place Environment ▸ Light Source or Interaction ▸ Torch for fixed lights on the top-level templates object layer. Light Source has radius, intensity, enabled and track_position; Torch has starts_lit, require_interact, allow_extinguish, light radius/intensity and optional labels, sound IDs and set_flag. Visible torch art comes from Tiled. Empty IDs use the placed object; each object bakes into an editable gameobject in its room collection.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Choose Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  11. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Display ▸ Lighting to tune the look.
  12. Choose Light mask or Glow only in Dark rooms. Shadow red/green/blue set the unlit colour; Light red/green/blue tint revealed art; Falloff below 1 spreads light and above 1 tightens its edge.
  13. Open Advanced ▸ Lantern to tune Radius, Intensity, Flicker and Minimum darkness. The radius stays in world pixels regardless of Player Size; bright rooms show no Lantern light.
  14. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Analyze Progression after publishing the layout.
  17. Add requires_light to any room named by dark_room_unmarked if the player needs light to cross it. A room whose fixed lights already show a safe route may ignore the warning.

The mask affects the world before Glow-material art and picture effects; the HUD, menus, room masks and bars stay visible. developer/config/lighting.lua keeps max_darkness config-only: 1 permits full black, and 0.9 keeps a faint view. Lantern needs no button; Abilities ▸ Utility ▸ Lantern starts fresh games with it. Its pickup and starting unlock use the same saved ability. A dark Screen Effect Zone inside a room needs a progression_links annotation such as entry>deep=lantern; the room gate is never inferred from darkness alone.

Change the UI theme without framework code#

Begin with the commented creator configs:

  • developer/config/ui_theme.lua for colors, fonts, and spacing.
  • developer/config/hud_layout.lua for HUD positions/sizes.
  • developer/config/menu_typography.lua for runtime menu text sizes and fitted text bands.
  • developer/config/settings_defaults.lua for default user settings.

Authored menu text lives in the editable GUI scenes under developer/ui/menus/. Start with list_menu.gui for Inventory and other shared lists: its rows are 18 px and its prompts and page counters are 16 px at 1920×1080. Check prompts, page counters and long translated rows at your target resolution after changing fonts or spacing. Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility ▸ Text size scales reading text; menu rows and prompts keep their authored size.

Pause buttons are styled in developer/ui/menus/pause_menu.gui. Resume, Inventory, Settings and Quit are always available. World Map follows world_map_settings.lua's enabled switch; Fast Travel also requires Map ▸ Fast travel ▸ Travel from to be anywhere (the default) and no blocking boss fight. Journal appears by default through its Records tab. Shown rows pack into the authored row positions with no gaps; the hint and controller icons follow the last shown row. With Journal shown and Difficulty hidden in new_game mode, the footer sits one slot (50 pixels) higher than with all eight rows visible. Feature modules supply the optional line under the title, such as an area name or reconnect message. Run Prepare & Check before Build after editing the scene.

Feature displays use editable nodes in developer/ui/game/feature_hud.gui. Their commented entries in hud_layout.lua and Show on screen toggles arrive with each feature; none are shown by the empty host. The top-right column packs shown blocks above the minimap, with notices below it. With all blocks hidden the minimap stays in its original position. column_gap sets the gap; minimap size and fine offsets remain in minimap_settings.lua. Moving the health bar into this corner also requires moving the matching feature offsets to keep them clear. Check custom layouts in game at each HUD scale.

Restyle toasts and prompts#

Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ HUD and prompts ▸ Toasts and prompts for toast position, vertical offset and text size, and prompt position, both offsets, opacity and text size. Message backing sets the plate's red, green, blue and opacity for toasts, Interact, gate hints and the developer menu's Saving off banner. Save, then Build to see your changes.

For finer control, edit the commented toast and interact_hint blocks in developer/config/hud_layout.lua: text colour, plate colour overrides, padding, width and height limits, toast horizontal offset and fade times. Game Settings values saved for a field take precedence over that field's baseline. The defaults keep toasts centred at y=120 and place prompts centred at y=360, above the countdown. The centered preset's health bar sits at y=34; its smaller bars sit at y=4. These bands stay clear at HUD scales 0.5 to 2; check your own offsets and font in game. Long messages wrap and then shorten to their configured height cap. A font too tall for even one line is capped to fit the band. Tutorials keep their full text and move above the prompt if reading-size changes would cross it. Prompts hide during dialogue and cutscenes, including captions.

A toast that must shorten its text ends in a read-more marker, such as ▶ E or ▶ RB. Press Interact to scroll the line to its end. That press reads the toast without using the nearby object; Interact works normally once the marker disappears. The timer holds while scrolling, then starts its normal duration again. Pause, menus, dialogue and the startup cover freeze both scrolling and the timer. The fully commented toast.read_more block in developer/config/hud_layout.lua sets enabled and speed_px_per_second; these controls are not in Game Settings. If a very narrow band cannot hold the complete marker and one text glyph, the cut line caps its glyph scale to fit. Widen toast.max_width to retain larger type.

In Defold, open developer/ui/game/toast.gui (plate, label) and developer/ui/shared/mode_hud.gui (interact_hint_plate, interact_hint_text). Give the authored box your texture and slice-9, or change the text node's font. Keep each plate before its text in draw order, use manual box sizing, and name a texture image as atlas/image. The script fits the plate around the text; configuration supplies the tint. To use a plate's editor colour, set its config entry's plate_color = "authored" (or message_plate = "authored" for all plates), clearing any Game Settings colour overrides first. Notices falling back to toasts use the same toast style. A touched gate's hint wins over another nearby prompt; leaving restores the nearby prompt immediately.

In toast.gui, keep read_more_text under the invisible stencil box read_more_clip. The fixed read_more_marker names Interact. The extra text nodes follow label's font, leading and tracking; the script sizes the clipping box and scrolls its child within the toast band.

Set Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ HUD and prompts ▸ Show on screen ▸ Currency format to {amount} Gems to show 120 Gems in the HUD and Inventory header. Write exactly one {amount} (or legacy %d); %% displays a percent sign. Formats are limited to 40 characters, and invalid formats cannot be saved. The commented currency.format in developer/config/hud_layout.lua is the baseline; invalid runtime formats fall back to $ %d. Set currency.thousands_separator = "," there for 1,200 Gems, or leave it empty for ungrouped digits. Save, Prepare & Check and Build after changing currency.

Build after edits. Avoid editing GUI scripts or mvtk/ until you intentionally leave the no-code creator workflow.

Show a play timer and run statistics#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Select HUD and prompts.
  3. Tick Show on screen ▸ Play timer.
  4. Select Progression.
  5. Tick Records ▸ Statistics in Records.
  6. Tick Records ▸ Time on ending.
  7. Click Save.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Open Pause ▸ Journal ▸ Records in the game.

Play time shows in-game time as h:mm:ss. Statistics adds deaths, enemy and boss defeats, non-respawning collectibles, secrets and visited rooms. Counts are recorded even with Statistics in Records off. Finishing an ending also marks it seen in the shared player profile. Journal ▸ Records shows how many enabled endings have been seen and each seen ending's title and best in-game time. These records survive deleting a slot; Dev Menu test sessions and boss practice do not write them. See ending records. Play timer starts off and shows the same in-game clock in the HUD's top-right column when enabled. Time on ending starts off and changes the ending line to h:mm:ss when enabled; with it off, the existing minutes line remains. These switches are independent.

Finishing an ending keeps the fastest whole-second time for that ending in the player profile, even when the timer displays are off. Best times survive switching or deleting save slots. Dev Menu test sessions and boss practice never update these records.

Pause menus, Settings, the Journal, a pausing Inventory, dialogue, cutscenes, the death screen and loading stop run time. Streamed room transitions count. Hitstop and slow motion slow the timer too. Compare a fresh slot with a stopwatch that excludes those pauses. After two death restarts, check Deaths 2 and that Play time did not jump back. Save normally to keep those statistics; quitting from the death menu before restarting retains the last saved statistics. The save-slot screen uses separate wall-clock play time and keeps the session time played before each death restart.

10. Complete Supported Template Catalog#

The live catalog is every .tx template under tiled/templates/. The .tx files are the source of truth. The generated detailed reference at MVTKINLINETOKEN3END lists every property, default, and description; this master table tells beginners what each object is for.

Abilities — 1#

Template Purpose
ability_pickup Grants a configured movement/combat ability when collected.

Audio/visual — 4#

Template Purpose
ambience_zone Plays/blends registered environmental ambience in a rectangle.
music_trigger Changes registered music when the player enters a rectangle.
particle_emitter Plays a configured particle effect, once or looping.
sound_source Plays registered ambience with positional volume falloff.

Bosses — 7#

Template Purpose
boss Places the boss identity, stats, visuals, rewards, and encounter link.
boss_arena Defines arena bounds, entry lock, camera, phases, and battle music.
arena_boundary Blocks an invisible rectangle while locked and in selected boss phases.
boss_add_spawner Calls in capped enemies with chosen movement and attacks during selected boss phases.
boss_phase Authors one boss phase's attacks, movement, health range, and transition.
boss_phase_trigger Forces a linked boss phase when its rectangle is triggered.
boss_weak_point Adds a linked vulnerable/damage-multiplier region.

Collectibles — 5#

Template Purpose
chest Places a persistent chest with inline contents and optional key requirement.
collectible Places currency, health, energy, and other configured pickups.
key Places a key collectible with a specific key ID.
lore_item Places an interactable/collectible story item.
map_fragment Places a collectible that reveals map progress/content.

Author encounter rectangles and wave points#

Entering an Encounter starts its waves once the player is clear of the doorway. An on_signal Encounter starts from a live on signal or an activate message. Its wave enemies settle onto the floor at the authored points; defeats advance to the next wave. The final wave opens the exits, shows Area cleared, plays the selected victory music and drops the configured reward.

  1. In Tiled, select the top-level templates layer.
  2. Place templates/enemies/encounter.tx.
  3. Resize the Encounter rectangle around the intended fight area.
  4. Set encounter_id to a unique text identity, or leave it blank for the baked object identity.
  5. Set start ▸ encounter_start to on_enter or on_signal.
  6. Place templates/enemies/encounter_spawn.tx inside the rectangle.
  7. Set enemy ▸ encounter_wave to the wave number, starting at 1.
  8. Set enemy ▸ enemy_type to the synced art.
  9. Set enemy ▸ archetype to the stats and behaviour preset.
  10. Set enemy ▸ spawn_count to the number scheduled at this point.
  11. Set timing ▸ spawn_delay in seconds.
  12. Set timing ▸ spawn_interval in seconds.
  13. Place a second Encounter Spawn point inside the rectangle.
  14. Set its enemy ▸ encounter_wave to 2 for a second wave.
  15. Set encounter_ref if a point should belong to an explicit Encounter instead of the smallest containing rectangle.
  16. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  17. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  18. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  19. Open the room under developer/rooms/collections/ to inspect the editable sensor and spawn markers.
  20. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  21. In Layout Editor, click Export.
  22. Choose Play Game.
  23. Select an empty save slot.

Prepare & Check warns when an Encounter has no points and rejects an orphan point outside every Encounter. An explicit encounter_ref can link a point outside the rectangle, but must select a visible Encounter in the same room. Overlapping rectangles choose the smallest; equal areas choose the lower Tiled object ID. Signals from levers and pressure plates can use target_ref to select an Encounter. A saved switch channel does not start an Encounter on load.

flow ▸ lock_room describes a seal for the whole room, including routes to checkpoints and trams. rewards ▸ repeatable disables saved clear state; otherwise clear_flag defaults to encounter_cleared_<encounter identity>. Keep an authored encounter_id stable after releasing a game. Clear flags and target shortcut channels are saved together when Autosave on clear is on. Mid-fight state is never saved: death, room unload or leaving an unlocked fight resets its waves without a reward. Each fight releases only its own seal, so an ongoing boss fight still holds the room shut. Fast travel is available again once all fights have released their seals. Teleporters and elevators retain their existing travel behaviour.

To tune project defaults:

  1. Choose Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Select Enemies and elements.
  3. Expand Encounters.
  4. Set Default wave delay (seconds).
  5. Set Default simultaneous cap.
  6. Set Wave messages.
  7. Set Autosave on clear.
  8. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Set an Encounter's target_ref to a Timed Door to open a shortcut on clear. A non-repeatable clear saves the target channel, so the door stays open after reload even with auto_close enabled. Repeatable encounters write no saved clear flag or target channel and can run again after leaving their rectangle or sending another on signal. Rewards get fresh pickup identities on each clear. Reword Wave {wave} of {total} and Area cleared in developer/config/ui_text.lua; default telegraph effects, telegraph timing and toast duration live in the fully commented developer/config/encounter_settings.lua. Boss music takes priority over champion, encounter, combat and room music.

Build a combat trial#

A trial runs one linked repeatable Encounter. Interact opens its start screen; confirm Begin to start the countdown. Losing returns the player safely to the pedestal with their starting health. Items and energy spent stay spent. A reward is granted only on the first clear, and later clears only improve the saved best result. Add pedestal artwork through art_item or a linked Object Art object. Trial timing, rank names and sounds are in the commented developer/config/trial_settings.lua.

The start screen shows Goal, Time limit, Ranks, Best and any Limits. Choose Leave to return without starting. Rank thresholds measure seconds for clear and defeat-count goals, or hits for survive goals. Journal ▸ Records ▸ Trials lists placed trials and their best saved rank and time (hits for survive).

Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Progression ▸ Trials provides Countdown (seconds), Restore health on clear and Save on clear. Click Save to apply them. HUD and prompts ▸ Show on screen ▸ Trial controls the trial goal, countdown, clock, move limits and result beneath the power-up block. Pause holds the countdown and clock; the result remains for result_duration play seconds.

Ability limits block Dash, Bomb, Compact Form, Super Jump, Blink, Grapple, Healing, Charge Attack, Down Strike and Ranged Attack. Other abilities use passive or unlock-only checks; Prepare & Check reports selections it cannot block. Attack limits support light, heavy, charge, down-strike, ranged and parry-counter actions. Movement, Bomb, Throw and Parry tags bypass that executor; use a supported ability limit where available. Limits begin after the countdown and clear on every trial outcome or abandonment. Existing attacks are not cancelled.

  1. Place an Encounter rectangle on the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Set its start ▸ encounter_start to on_signal.
  3. Tick its rewards ▸ repeatable.
  4. Untick its flow ▸ enemy_drops.
  5. Place Encounter Spawn points inside the rectangle.
  6. Set each point's enemy ▸ encounter_wave to its wave number.
  7. Place a Combat Trial pedestal on the templates layer.
  8. Set its encounter_ref to the Encounter rectangle.
  9. Set its trial_id to a permanent unique id.
  10. Set its trial_name to the name players should see.
  11. Choose goal ▸ trial_goal: clear, defeat_count or survive.
  12. Set goal ▸ defeat_target for a defeat-count goal.
  13. Set goal ▸ time_limit in seconds (positive for survive; zero means unlimited for other goals).
  14. Tick goal ▸ no_damage if the first hit should fail the trial.
  15. Set ranks ▸ rank_1 (seconds, or hits for survive; -1 disables the rank).
  16. Set ranks ▸ rank_2.
  17. Set ranks ▸ rank_3.
  18. Set rewards ▸ trial_reward to first-clear grant text, such as currency:25.
  19. Select any abilities to block in limits ▸ disabled_abilities.
  20. Select any attack tags to block in limits ▸ disabled_attacks.
  21. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room in Tiled.
  22. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  23. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  24. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  25. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  26. Choose Play Game.
  27. Select an empty save slot.

Carry and throw objects#

  1. Place Physics ▸ Carryable on the top-level templates object layer in Tiled.
  2. Set Carry id to a unique text id, or leave it blank for the baked instance id.
  3. Choose Kind: crate, orb or bomb.
  4. Set Handling ▸ Move speed mult between 0.25 and 1.
  5. Tick Handling ▸ Blocks jump for a heavy object.
  6. Set Handling ▸ Throw speed in world pixels per second.
  7. Set Fragility ▸ Fuse time in seconds for a bomb fruit.
  8. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Click Export in the Layout Editor after connecting rooms.
  13. Start Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  14. Press Interact beside the object to pick it up.
  15. Press Attack to throw it, or Up + Attack for the high arc.
  16. Press Interact while carrying to drop it.

While carrying, combat, dash, slide, Blink, backdash, ground pound and grapple are blocked. Water, climbing, ledge hang and stun drop it. A room change, lost object or break returns it home. The bomb fuse starts at pickup and holds during Pause. Lost objects wait the configured Return delay before reappearing. Edit shared kinds, angles, hold height, delay and optional carry animations in developer/config/carry_settings.lua; slowdown uses the shared speed sources. Custom artwork uses linked object art with owner_ref; otherwise the stock placeholder follows the object. Keep the full throw arc and blast clear of other tests. Crate impacts hit nearby enemies, cracked walls and Strike Switches set to any. A bomb fruit opens bomb walls without the Bomb ability, so use progression_links for fruit-only routes in Analyze Progression.

Carry objects to sockets#

  1. Place Interaction ▸ Socket on the top-level templates object layer in Tiled.
  2. Set Socket id to a unique text id.
  3. Select the receiving door with Target reference.
  4. Choose a kind in Acceptance ▸ Accepts, or leave any selected.
  5. Set Acceptance ▸ Accepts id to a particular Carry id if needed.
  6. Tick Response ▸ Persist state for a socket that consumes its object permanently.
  7. Set Response ▸ Set flag on if a staged gate should count the first fill.
  8. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Click Export in the Layout Editor.
  13. Start Play Game on a fresh save slot.
  14. Drop or throw the matching carryable into the socket.
  15. Choose Take beside a filled socket with Persist state off to carry the object again.

For another room or a shared channel, enter its text Target id instead of selecting Target reference. Message on defaults to activate and Message off to deactivate. A persistent fill saves the socket and target channel before signalling; receivers restore their own channel silently. The object stays gone after saving and reloading. A reversible socket resets with the room; Take sends off only when pickup succeeds. Set flag on means ever filled and taking never clears it. Leave Persist key blank for room plus Tiled object id, or keep a custom key stable after releasing the game. Link empty and filled art states for the socket; default art dims when filled. A socket holds the remaining bomb fuse time; Take resumes it. Use the socket's Socket id in a sequence gate's Sequence order; keep Persist state off for resettable sequence puzzles. For a staged gate, use Set flag on with the gate's Stage flags and leave Target id blank.

For a thrown-object puzzle, choose thrown in Strike Switch ▸ Behavior ▸ Accepts. Crate and orb impact circles emit the throw tag; the bomb fruit emits bomb. A floating switch needs to lie within the impact circle where the thrown object lands; a solid switch can itself stop the object's sweep. Keep the entire throw lane clear.

11. Assets: Where Your Files Go#

What you own/edit Normal location or workflow
Original Tiled rooms and worlds Your authoring folder outside the Defold project.
Original .tsx tilesets Your authoring folder; exporter localizes them to developer/assets/tiled/tilesets/.
Primary tilesheet PNGs Your authoring folder; exporter/import puts project copies under developer/assets/tilesheets/.
Image-layer/image-collection art Localized to developer/assets/tiled/backgrounds/.
Room music Any source folder; Prepare & Check copies it to developer/assets/music/.
Sound effects developer/assets/sfx/ plus the commented sound registries.
Original enemy frame folders Outside the Defold project; link a frame through an enemy's Tiled art file property.
Imported or directly supplied enemy frames developer/assets/enemies/<enemy_id>/; Prepare & Check syncs them.
Optional player source pack developer/assets/source/player_template/.
Generated player frames/atlas developer/assets/sprites/player/ and developer/assets/atlas/player.atlas.
Fonts developer/assets/fonts/.
Particle/visual assets developer/assets/fx/ and the configured particle registries.

Keep stable filenames when replacing a placeholder that is already referenced. If you rename an asset, update the creator-facing atlas/config reference and run the appropriate import/sync command. Do not solve a missing asset by hardcoding its path in framework code.

12. Configuration Without Coding#

Configuration files are Lua tables, but creator-facing files are data: change values beside clear comments and keep punctuation/commas intact. Make one small change, build, and test it before changing many systems at once.

The default shows more world on wider or narrower windows, from about 4:3 to 2.4:1, while keeping the whole 16:9 design area visible. Bars return beyond those limits; the world never stretches. The HUD and menu content stay in the safe 16:9 area. Camera pixel snapping stays on the design grid; fractional window scaling can produce slight sub-pixel shimmer.

Choose screen shapes#

  1. Stop the running game.
  2. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  3. Select Display, immediately after Camera.
  4. Set View on other screen shapes to Expand (show more world) or Letterbox (keep 16:9 with bars).
  5. Set Players can change it on only if players should get a Wide screens row in Settings ▸ Graphics.
  6. Set Narrowest shape to the minimum width divided by height to fill (4:3 is about 1.33).
  7. Set Widest shape to the maximum width divided by height to fill (21:9 is about 2.39).
  8. Set Bar red from 0 to 1.
  9. Set Bar green from 0 to 1.
  10. Set Bar blue from 0 to 1.
  11. Click Save.
  12. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.

With Players can change it off (the default), the creator's mode always wins, including over a player's previously stored choice. With it on, Wide screens starts at the creator's mode and switches live between Show more and Keep 16:9. Reset returns it to following the creator. Baseline values and comments live in developer/config/display_settings.lua; Game Settings saves overrides.

Add screen effects#

  1. Stop the game.
  2. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… in Defold.
  3. Select Display.
  4. Set Brightness, Gamma, Saturation, or the tint fields in Picture tone.
  5. Set Scanlines, Curvature, or Vignette in Retro screen if wanted.
  6. Set Players can change brightness off if players should not have a Brightness slider.
  7. Click Save.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  10. Choose Play Game.
  11. Compare the world picture with the HUD, menus, dialogue, masks and bars.

All effects start neutral or off. Gamma above 1 lifts shadows; saturation 0 makes the picture grey. Tint strength 0 disables the colour filter. Effects cover the world only, including glow; GUI world overlays stay above them. Players adjust Settings ▸ Graphics ▸ Brightness from 50 to 150 percent in 5 percent steps. This multiplies creator Brightness and is saved between visits. The permission starts on. The title screen and Layout Editor stay unfiltered.

Whole-pixel art remains crisp with effects unless Curvature bends it. Curvature moves the visible world under the pointer near edges; pointer-to-world positions still use the flat picture. Scanline count (60–1080, default 270) is config-only in developer/config/display_settings.lua. Active effects add a full-picture copy each frame; all-neutral settings bypass it. Unsupported devices fall back to the original picture and log once. Check the built game and export on each target platform; Vulkan and Metal shader compatibility remains unverified.

Pixel scaling#

  1. Stop the running game.
  2. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  3. Select Display.
  4. Set Pixel scaling in the Pixel scaling section to Integer for whole art pixels, or Fit for the default framing.
  5. Click Save.
  6. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Integer uses the largest whole-number art scale that fits at Camera ▸ Zoom. Windows too small for one screen pixel per art pixel fall back to Fit and log once. Camera snapping follows whole screen pixels in Integer mode; camera zones with another zoom_level are not guaranteed whole-pixel. HUD and menus keep their fitted size, and the band around the picture uses the bar colour below the HUD. Fit keeps the existing picture and design-grid camera snapping.

Frame wider pictures#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Select Display.
  3. Set Mask outside room in Framing to cover space outside smaller clamped or locked rooms with the bar colour.
  4. Set HUD frame to Safe (16:9, the default) or Picture (picture edges).
  5. Click Save.
  6. Select a background image layer or image object in Tiled.
  7. Set its Class to background_visual.
  8. Tick Stretch to fill (stretch_to_fill) to widen it with the picture.
  9. Use File ▸ Export Current Room.
  10. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.

If you use parallax, you may prefer to leave it off.

Stretch to fill defaults off and returns to the authored size in Letterbox mode. Prepare & Check warns when a parallax image does not cover the widest picture; extend the artwork by the reported pixels or enable its image-layer repeat. Named zones in developer/config/camera_zones.lua can set mask_outside_room = false to show space outside a room. Omit it to inherit Mask outside room.

Frame a custom GUI#

  1. Keep content nodes at Adjust mode ▸ Fit without edge anchors, or under a centred Fit 1920 × 1080 root with Parent adjust reference.
  2. Set a top-level childless full-screen backdrop's Adjust mode to Stretch only in scenes where you want that backdrop to fill the window.
  3. Choose a render layer for your GUI scene from mvtk/ui/render_layers.lua (for example, hud, popups or menus).
  4. Require mvtk.ui.render_layers as RenderLayers in the GUI script.
  5. Set the order in init with gui.set_render_order(RenderLayers.menus).
  6. Test the scene at 21:9 and 4:3.

Stretch is opt-in per scene and may distort backdrop artwork. Content under a root must keep Fit; a stretched root would also stretch its children. Scripted full-picture dimmers and fades use the shared Viewport.cover helper instead.

Beginner-safe task-to-file map#

Task File
Player movement/stats developer/config/player_settings.lua
Abilities unlocked at a new game developer/config/ability_defaults.lua
Baseline combat tuning developer/config/combat_overrides.lua
Enemy presets developer/config/enemy_types.lua
Collectible definitions developer/config/collectible_types.lua
Gate types developer/config/gate_types.lua
Hazard types developer/config/hazard_types.lua
Reusable dialogue developer/config/narrative/dialogues.lua
UI theme/HUD developer/config/ui_theme.lua, developer/config/hud_layout.lua
Screen shape limits, player choice and bar colour developer/config/display_settings.lua
Default settings developer/config/settings_defaults.lua
Fog of war and exploration reveal developer/config/map_discovery_settings.lua
Permanent game/save identity developer/config/persistence_settings.lua
Debug switches developer/config/debug_config.lua
Boss presets developer/config/boss_types.lua and the other commented boss_*.lua configs
