Understand precedence#

Maps begin hidden and uncover the area shown by the gameplay camera, including an entire room when it fits on screen. Scrolling and zoom changes reveal newly visible cells even while the player stands still. A visible neighboring room can appear on the map before the player enters it; preloading alone does not reveal terrain, and hidden-secret rules still apply. Map stations reveal their configured areas, and discovery is saved independently in each slot. The default mode = "nearby" also reveals an optional radius around the player; mode = "room" reveals the whole current room on entry, even beyond the screen. The commented map_discovery_settings.lua controls that extra radius, cell size, and edge_softness: the world-pixel radius that rounds and feathers the fog on both maps. Increase it for softer edges, or set it to 0 for hard edges; it does not change saved exploration or marker visibility. Tiled markers explicitly marked visible_from_start can still point toward unknown areas.

Set a unique, permanent game_id in persistence_settings.lua before releasing your game. When left empty, the title in game.project determines the save directory, so changing that title changes the save location. Old shared toolkit saves remain untouched; start with a fresh slot after upgrading. See the configuration handbook for preserving a verified older save.

There is no single override order for every subsystem. Use these rules:

Concern Higher priority → lower priority
Player, starting abilities, combat, game settings Published creator_settings.lua overrides → baseline creator config → framework fallback. Machine-local authoring values are not imported.
Player's saved preferences Player settings menu → Game Settings defaults → settings_defaults.lua.
Placed enemy, boss, gate, collectible, and similar objects Tiled instance property → selected config preset/type → framework fallback.
Tile size Imported tilesource/Tiled art → generated tile_settings.lua mirror. Do not hand-tune the mirror.
Playable room resources Current Tiled export → Prepare & Check generated collection/factory.
Runtime world placement Latest successful Layout Editor Export → older published layout fallback.

If a baseline config edit appears to do nothing, check the published override in Game Settings. Use the row’s x to return that field to its default, then Save. Keep tuning overlapping player, ability, combat and game values through Project ▸ Game Settings….

Tune an attack#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… with the game stopped.
  2. Choose Attacks.
  3. Select Light combo 2 in the attack picker.
  4. Change Window 1 hitbox 1 damage.
  5. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  7. Hit an enemy with the second light combo hit to check its damage number.

The picker lists every attack defined in developer/config/combat_config.lua. Each section exposes cooldown, energy cost, startup, recovery, every active window's start and duration, every hitbox's damage, width, height and knockback, and damage type. Ranged shot exposes its projectile damage, speed, lifetime and stun time and muzzle position. Optional numeric projectile direction and knockback fields appear when authored in the config; leaving them absent preserves automatic aiming. Window starts must follow startup and must not overlap earlier active windows. Invalid fields show an error and prevent Save, including on hidden attacks.

Hover over a name or field for help. Blue names differ from defaults; each row's x resets it. Save publishes developer/config/creator_settings.lua overrides and preserves the baseline files. Per-attack values take precedence over the compatible Combat quick fields. The light quick fields affect the first light combo hit only; their hover help names the exact affected attacks. Combat ▸ Invincibility, after Parry, sets after-hit, Slide, Dash and after-parry times in seconds; Slide and Dash require their Invincibility unlocks, and 0 covers their whole movement. Entering a per-attack value equal to its default still overrides a quick field; its x removes that override even though the name is not blue. Sounds, sprite offsets, Spine bones, reaction types and parryable flags remain in developer/config/combat_config.lua. A newly added attack appears when the model next loads; quit and reopen Defold after changing that config.

Generated or internal: do not hand-edit#

  • developer/config/tile_settings.lua
  • developer/config/rooms_config.lua
  • developer/config/elevators_generated.lua
  • developer/config/runtime_settings.lua
  • developer/config/*_generated.lua
  • developer/config/tiled_enemy_overrides.lua
  • developer/config/enemy_skins_generated.lua
  • developer/rooms/generated/
  • developer/rooms/collections/
  • developer/rooms/factories/
  • developer/runtime/layout/world_layout.local.lua
  • everything under mvtk/ for normal creator work

Regenerate these through Prepare & Check or Layout Editor Export.

Healing#

By default one button heals: a hurt player presses G or D-pad Right to drink from a flask with a few charges. A hit, jump, dash or attack cancels a drink without spending it. Potions found in the world work the same way.

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… and choose Player ▸ Healing.
  2. Set Healing to flask (a refillable built-in heal) or items (potions only).
  3. Set Flask charges and Heal per use. Zero charges removes the flask.
  4. Optional: to make the flask a reward, untick Abilities ▸ Defensive ▸ Healing, then place an Ability Pickup with ability_id healing. Potions never need the ability.
  5. Choose Save and Prepare & Check.
  6. In Tiled, place a Collectible with collectible_type heal_charge_upgrade for each extra flask charge.
  7. Place a Collectible with collectible_type potion_small or potion_large for each potion.
  8. Give every new pickup art: place it as a tile object, or give its collectible type an item image tile. A rectangle pickup without either is invisible.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  10. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check, then Project ▸ Build.
  11. On the title screen, open Layout Editor and click Export.
  12. Start an empty save slot and test G or D-pad Right while hurt.

Resting at a save point refills the flask only while that save point's Heal on save is on. Respawning and loading a checkpoint refill it too; a health pickup never does. Players choose a potion as their quick item in Inventory ▸ Items, or by holding the button in flask/items mode. HUD and prompts ▸ Show on screen ▸ Quick item turns the HUD slot on or off.

To heal by holding Quick Use and spending energy:

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Choose Player ▸ Healing.
  3. Set Healing to channel.
  4. Set Heal per channel to the health restored by each completed heal.
  5. Set Energy per heal to the energy spent by each completed heal.
  6. Set Channel time (seconds) to the time per heal after the hold starts.
  7. Choose Player ▸ Health and resources.
  8. Set Max energy above zero and at least as high as Energy per heal.
  9. Choose Combat ▸ Resource gain.
  10. Tick Gain energy from combat to refill energy by fighting.
  11. Set Energy per hit.
  12. Set Energy per parry for regular parries.
  13. Set Energy per perfect parry for perfect parries (replaces the regular gain).
  14. Set Energy per defeat for the extra gain on a lethal player hit.
  15. Choose Save and Prepare & Check.
  16. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  17. Start an empty save slot.
  18. While hurt, hold G or D-pad Right to heal while energy lasts.
  19. Release Quick Use to stop healing.

The Healing ability gates both the flask and the channel heal. Potions never need it. In channel mode, choose a quick item in Inventory ▸ Items; a tap uses that item and holding heals. With no quick item, tapping shows a hold hint. The first completed channel takes about 1.15 seconds from pressing the button with default settings (0.35-second hold threshold plus 0.8-second channel). Damage and cancel actions stop the current heal without spending energy. The energy bar is the channel meter; there are no flask pips in this mode.

Energy starts full and is not saved. After spending energy, four default enemy hits restore 1 energy with Gain energy from combat on. With it off, pickups, energy stations and save points supply energy. Max energy at 0 prevents channel healing, including a free heal; Prepare & Check reports an error. The Dev Menu's Infinite energy waives healing and attack costs while the energy pool is enabled. For an optional attack cost, set that attack's energy_cost in the commented developer/config/combat_config.lua; a cost above the maximum prevents the attack, while Max energy 0 waives attack costs.

For advanced tuning use the commented developer/config/healing.lua: drink and channel duration, channel held repeat and tutorial event, movement, air/water rules, refills, sounds and FX, quick-list order and the 0.45-second step repeat (unused for channel healing). The initial hold threshold is default_bindings.lua's hold_seconds.QUICK_USE (0.35 seconds).

Weapons#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Equipment ▸ Weapons.
  2. Tick Weapons.
  3. Choose Starting weapon (or Unarmed).
  4. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  5. Reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled.
  6. Place a Collectible on the top-level templates layer.
  7. Set collectible_type to weapon_heavy_maul.
  8. Give the pickup art using the Collectible's art fields.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Export the changed layout with Layout Editor ▸ Export.
  13. Play an empty save slot.
  14. Pick up the weapon.
  15. Press C or Left Stick click (L3) to cycle owned weapons.

Quick switch enables the Next Weapon action. Players can also equip owned weapons in Inventory ▸ Weapons; the equipped badge and HUD identify the selection. An unmet ability requirement disables the weapon's row. Gear never appears in a store's Sell section while Weapons is on. The virtual starting weapon needs no pickup; a duplicate pays the configured currency. Weapons ships off, leaving its pickups in place and combat unchanged. Tune the fully commented developer/config/weapons.lua list for names, descriptions, attack sets, scaling, ranged overrides, requirements and pickup icons. HUD and prompts ▸ Show on screen ▸ Weapon controls the HUD block.

Crafting#

  1. Open developer/config/weapons.lua.
  2. Define the optional tiers list; its first entry is tier 2, with scales multiplying the base weapon's scales.
  3. Open developer/config/crafting.lua.
  4. Add an ordered recipe with id, name, description, costs and gives.
  5. Set costs to comma-separated currency:<amount> and item:<id>:<count> entries.
  6. Set gives to a configured weapon_tier:<weapon>:<tier> or item:charm_<id>.
  7. Add an optional requires_flag to hide a recipe until that flag is set.
  8. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Equipment ▸ Crafting.
  9. Tick Crafting.
  10. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Open Inventory ▸ Crafting while playing.
  13. Select a recipe to read costs and have/need counts.
  14. Confirm the craft.

Crafting ships off. Enable Weapons to use tiers and Charms to equip crafted charms. A tier requires that weapon and its preceding tier. Completed recipes cannot charge again; missing costs spend nothing. Crafting saves once, and the next attack uses the tier scaling. To upgrade a charm, include its base item in the costs: consuming the last copy unequips it; equip the new charm in Inventory ▸ Charms. Crafting accepts only weapon tiers and charm outputs; station catalogs retain the full grant grammar.

See Upgrade materials for the shared material and how to split the economies. Sample Heavy Maul tier 2 costs two materials and 100 currency, tier 3 costs three and 200, and Ember Charm costs two materials.

Random equipment drops#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Equipment.
  2. Tick Weapons if weapons should be eligible.
  3. Tick Charms if charms should be eligible.
  4. In Random drops, tick Random drops.
  5. Set Chance per defeat between 0 and 1.
  6. Choose the Weapons, Charms and Permanent upgrades families in that section.
  7. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Start an empty save slot.
  10. Defeat an enemy.
  11. Collect its extra pickup when a roll succeeds.

The feature ships off. A successful roll adds at most one pickup alongside the enemy's normal drops. Each eligible item is equally likely. Owned items and unmet prerequisites stay out of the pool; when nothing is left, no extra pickup appears. Bosses, summoned minions and abilities are excluded. Pickups left behind are not saved.

Protect a gate or story reward with random_drop = false on its entry in developer/config/weapons.lua, charms.lua or upgrades.lua. Random weapons can open typed barriers early; Analyze Progression never assumes a random reward. An enemy type's optional random_drop_chance overrides the project chance; 0 means never. Edit these commented configs with the game stopped, then run Prepare & Check before Build or Export Game.

Loot tables can name weapon_<id>, charm_<id>, upgrade_<id> and ability_<id>. Project ▸ Loot Tables… derives its Type choices from the same collectible catalog as Tiled's Collectible type dropdown, including generated types. Close and reopen MVTK.tiled-project after Prepare & Check refreshes the catalogs. A named or placed upgrade stays until its prerequisites are met. If the upgrade is already applied at collection, it pays random_drops.lua's duplicate_currency. Placed upgrades count toward Completion by pickup identity; random drops add no world total. Set an upgrade's count_in_census = false to exclude its placed pickups.

Charms#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Equipment ▸ Charms.
  2. Tick Charms.
  3. Set Base slots.
  4. Set Maximum slots.
  5. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  6. Reopen MVTK.tiled-project in Tiled.
  7. Place a Collectible on the top-level templates layer.
  8. Set collectible_type to charm_vitality.
  9. Give the pickup art using the Collectible's art fields.
  10. Place another Collectible on the top-level templates layer.
  11. Set its collectible_type to charm_slot.
  12. Give the slot pickup art using the Collectible's art fields.
  13. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check in Defold.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. Export the changed layout with Layout Editor ▸ Export.
  17. Play an empty save slot.
  18. Collect the pickups.
  19. Open Inventory ▸ Charms.
  20. Confirm a charm to equip it.
  21. Confirm the equipped charm to unequip it.

Charms ship off; disabled pickups stay in place. The header shows used and total slots. Each owned charm shows its slot cost, description and equipped state. A charm that does not fit is disabled with Not enough free slots; equipped charms can still be removed. Players can change charms anywhere in the Inventory. Capacity is Base slots plus collected Charm Slots, capped by Maximum slots. Charms and Charm Slots stay out of Items and store Sell sections while Charms is on. With Charms off, Charm Slot remains visible under Items and cannot be sold. The equipped set is kept at the next normal save and restored by Continue.

Tune the fully commented developer/config/charms.lua list for stable ids, source names and descriptions, whole slot costs, pickup icons and effects. Generated pickup types are charm_<id>; grants use item:charm_<id>. Duplicate owned charms pay the configured currency. Keep ids stable in saved games.

Effect key Result
max_health Adds health capacity without refilling current health; unequipping clamps health to the lower maximum.
max_energy Adds energy capacity.
max_stamina Adds stamina capacity.
speed_mult Multiplies walking and running speed.
damage_mult Multiplies outgoing attack damage.
damage_taken_mult Multiplies incoming damage; values below 1 reduce it.
damage_taken_mult:fire Multiplies fire damage only; use a configured damage type after the colon for other resistances.
xp_mult Multiplies XP gains; does nothing while Leveling is off.
currency_mult Multiplies currency pickups, including dropped coins, with nearest-integer rounding; never boosts duplicate-gear payouts.

Additive effects sum and multiplier effects multiply on the equipment layer. invulnerable is forbidden for permanent charms. A health charm does not heal. If a loaded equipped set exceeds capacity, the last-equipped charms are removed.

Controls and input#

Action Keyboard Controller
Quick Use (Heal / Item) G D-pad Right
Next Weapon C Left Stick click (L3)
Blink (once owned) V again during a dash or slide Right Trigger again
Super Jump (once owned) Hold Down while standing still, then Jump Hold stick Down while standing still, then Jump

Blink is unavailable while swimming or climbing.

Tap to use the selected quick item. In flask/items mode, hold to switch when carrying two or more quick items; with one item, holding uses it. In channel mode, holding heals and items are selected in Inventory. Players can remap Heal / Item on Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Controls, page 2 (eight rows per page).

Players can remap keyboard keys and controller buttons for Jump, Dash, Attack, Special, Ranged Attack, Interact, Minimap, Inventory, Parry, Grapple, World Map, Map Marker, Heal / Item, Ground Pound and Pause in that one menu. Backdash, Compact Form and Next Weapon appear when their features are available. Potion and Settings stay hidden. Choose a row, then press a key or controller button; the value shows Key / Button, with - for an empty slot. A taken key or button swaps the two actions' slots and shows Swapped with <action>. Keyboard changes preserve controller buttons; controller changes preserve keyboard keys. Reset restores both devices to the project's defaults.

Escape cancels capture and always remains a Pause fallback. The camera stick and menu navigation stay fixed; creators configure navigation in developer/config/menu_settings.lua. Movement keys (WASD and arrows), R and the F1–F7 debug keys cannot be captured. Letter and digit keys are available.

Defold's editable input map is developer/input/game.input_binding. Open it in the Defold editor to add or change keyboard, mouse, touch, or gamepad triggers. The current physical-action allowlist and starter high-level mappings live in the inputs and actions tables in developer/config/default_bindings.lua. Physical IDs are device-separated: keyboard inputs use key_*, and standard controller inputs use gamepad_*.

By default, pressing M or R3 (the right thumbstick click) cycles the gameplay map display through no map → minimap panel → full-screen map → no map. The physical key_m and gamepad_rstick_click actions are mapped to MINIMAP_TOGGLE; the separate interactive world-map action remains independently rebindable.

On the open world map, players pan with the left stick, the D-pad, WASD or the arrow keys, or by dragging with the left mouse button, and zoom with the right stick, the triggers, the mouse wheel (at the pointer) or Q and E. R3 or C centres the view on the player again. I or the top face button shows or hides the legend, which lists the controls and the kinds of icon on screen. While choosing a fast-travel destination, up and down still pick the stop, and LB and RB jump to the previous or next one. These are fixed map inputs, not remappable actions; set the deepest zoom, the pan speed and whether the legend starts shown in Game Settings ▸ Map ▸ World map.

The world map opens zoomed to the player's current room. In developer/config/world_map_settings.lua, open_zoom = "room" picks the deepest available zoom where the room fits within open_room_fraction of both screen width and height (default 0.5, or half). Set open_zoom = "world" to use the whole-world fit times scale_multiplier, raised to min_scale, instead. Missing room outlines or bounds use that same fallback. Each opening checks the current room again. Fast travel uses the same zoom and centres on the selected destination, keeping it visible.

A cursor points at the map: the screen centre with a controller or the keyboard, and the mouse pointer once the mouse moves. It snaps to the nearest icon, and a panel at the top left names the region, the room's room_title and the icon under it. A room is named only where the player has seen it, and a room hidden as a secret is never named. Region names from developer/config/biomes.lua appear over the map once the player has visited or revealed one of their rooms, and fade out as the map zooms in. In fast travel the cursor snaps only to unlocked stops, and A travels to the stop it points at. With a controller, pointing at a screenshot marker selects it, and B deletes it. The snap distance, the panel and the region names are in developer/config/world_map_settings.lua (cursor and labels).

Players can leave their own stamps on the world map, such as a star, ! or ?, to mark places they cannot reach yet: X opens the palette at the cursor, left and right choose, A places and B cancels, and X on a stamp removes it. Turn stamps off or set their limit in Game Settings ▸ Map ▸ Player markers, and edit the palette in stamps in developer/config/world_map_settings.lua.

Both maps mark items, chests, lore, secrets, barriers, bosses, trials and stations from the prepared world census, so icons also show for rooms that are not loaded. Once seen, a station's icon (shop, upgrade station, bank, trainer, altar, save point, fast travel, health, energy and map station, teleporter, vendor) stays across room changes and saves until the station is gone for good. The world map shows item icons when zoomed in; the minimap shows the icons of the current room and the rooms next to it, as small coloured shapes. A secret stays unmarked until found, and a Map Station reveals layout but not items unless the rule is mapped. Shapes, colours and rules per kind are in developer/config/map_icons.lua; its minimap block turns minimap icons off or sets their limit.

To change one object's icon in Tiled:

  1. Select the object.
  2. Add a custom string property named map_icon.
  3. Type an icon id from icons in developer/config/map_icons.lua, or hide.
  4. Export the room with File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.

An object the census does not list gets an icon only when map_icon names one. auto keeps the default icon.

To remove one object's icon from both maps:

  1. Select the object.
  2. Add a custom bool property named hide_on_map.
  3. Tick hide_on_map.
  4. Export the room with File ▸ Export Current Room.
  5. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.

To change when item icons appear for the whole game:

  1. Stop the game.
  2. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  3. Open Map ▸ Item tracking.
  4. Choose a rule in Show items when.
  5. Click Save and Prepare & Check.

This toolkit targets Defold 1.14.1's built-in SDL controller database. It tracks controller connect/disconnect events and uses the first mapped controller for the single player; unmapped devices remain visible in diagnostics but cannot claim gameplay, and unplugging the active pad clears its held input and selects the next mapped controller. Left- and right-stick direction actions preserve analog strength. game.project owns the one hardware deadzone, so do not add another deadzone in player settings or a project .gamepads compatibility file.

Runtime remaps use a versioned current-only save. When the binding schema changes, incompatible older remap data is ignored and current defaults are used; there is no legacy alias migration layer. Each capturable keyboard key and digital controller button belongs to one action. Saved remaps take precedence over defaults added by a toolkit update.

Keep action IDs synchronized across both files and any creator-facing config or GUI that listens for them. For example, renaming gamepad_lstick_left in only the input binding does not rename the action expected by the player controller. Prefer changing the key/button attached to an existing action. Add a new action ID only when the corresponding feature already knows how to consume it.

Set creator defaults in the Controls tab, directly after HUD and prompts. Movement, Combat, Items and interaction, and Map and menus hold the key and button fields. Button timing sets the initial Quick Use hold (default 0.35 seconds) and step repeat (default 0.45 seconds). Menu navigation stays in developer/config/menu_settings.lua.

  1. Choose Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Open Controls.
  3. Choose an action's default key or button, or None to clear that slot.
  4. Resolve any Also used by errors by choosing a free key or button.
  5. Set Heal / item hold time (seconds) if needed.
  6. Set Quick item step repeat (seconds) if needed.
  7. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Conflicts mark both fields and disable Save; an empty player slot warns. Escape stays a fixed Pause fallback. WASD, arrows, R, F1–F7 and Guide are unavailable. The x beside a changed field removes its creator override. Saving keeps default_bindings.lua intact. Each remappable row still owns its label, player visibility and capture slots; physical lists stay in actions. Player preferences in Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Controls stay above creator defaults. Reset returns to the creator defaults; players with older full binding saves must press it once to receive changed creator defaults.

To introduce a physical input that is not listed:

  1. Add its trigger to developer/input/game.input_binding in the Defold editor.
  2. Add its action ID to inputs in developer/config/default_bindings.lua.
  3. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Prepare & Check also runs before Export Game after a binding or config change.

13. Troubleshooting By Stage#

Installation or first Defold build fails#

  • Confirm Defold is 1.14.1 or a later 1.14.x hotfix and the toolkit was fully extracted/installed.
  • Keep the project in a writable folder; avoid protected system directories.
  • Wait for Defold dependencies to finish loading.
  • Read the first red Console error, not only the last one.
  • Run Project ▸ Set Up This Toolkit again, especially after moving or copying the toolkit folder.

A property type error appears after updating the toolkit#

After updating, run Reload Editor Scripts → Prepare & Check → Build. Prepare & Check rebuilds the room collections with the current property types.

A Console error The property '<name>' must be of type 'string' means a room or prefab still stores the old hash type. Run Prepare & Check. If the message names one of your own prefabs, re-enter that value in the Defold Properties panel, save the prefab, and Build again.

Tiled toolkit menus or templates are missing#

  • Open MVTK.tiled-project with File ▸ Open File or Project..., not only a .tmx.
  • Click Enable Extensions in the yellow bar at the top right. If you dismissed it, close the project and open it again.
  • Check Tiled's View ▸ Views and Toolbars ▸ Console for an error that names a file in tiled/extensions.
  • Confirm File ▸ new_room_blank, File ▸ Export Current Room, File ▸ Export All Maps and Tiled World and File ▸ Start a New World... appear.

Export says "Required exporters not found"#

Tiled's defold or lua plugin is off; the message lists which one (false). Choose Edit ▸ Preferences..., click the Plugins tab, tick defold and lua (defold.dll and lua.dll on Windows, libdefold.so and liblua.so on Linux), close the window and export again.

Tiled cannot detect the project root, or exports go to another toolkit#

Exports go to the toolkit whose MVTK.tiled-project is open. Open the MVTK.tiled-project in the toolkit folder you want (the folder that holds game.project), click Enable Extensions if Tiled asks, and export again. WHERE_THINGS_ARE.txt in each toolkit folder lists that toolkit's paths.

Export says a tileset is unsaved or missing#

  • Save the room and every external .tsx.
  • Confirm the .tsx still points to the original tilesheet image.
  • For external authoring, keep editing the original, not the localized project copy.
  • Re-export after fixing the source path.

Tiles are invisible or use the wrong art#

  • Confirm the room uses one primary tilesheet for painted tile layers.
  • Verify Tile Width/Height match the actual image grid.
  • Re-export, then run Prepare & Check.
  • Check developer/assets/tilesheets/ for the localized primary image.
  • Read Prepare & Check warnings about tilesource resolution.

The player falls through floors or hits invisible walls#

  • Open the .tsx and confirm solid tiles have Tile Collision Editor shapes.
  • Save the .tsx, not only the room.
  • Paint tiles with Tile Collision Editor shapes on any layer that does not scroll, such as environment. The solid layer is optional for hidden collision.
  • Scrolling (parallax) layers never collide; use tiles without collision shapes for scrolling art and decoration.
  • Remove accidental collision shapes from decorative tiles.
  • Alternatively use solid_box/solid_slope, then export and rebuild.

Prepare & Check fails#

  • Stop the running game first.
  • Retry Reload Editor Scripts → Prepare & Check → Build in order.
  • Fix the first error in Defold Console.
  • Open debug_output.txt when Prepare & Check points there.
  • Do not patch generated collections to hide the source error.

Prepare & Check reports catalog_value_unknown#

A placed object, or a room's Map Properties, uses a value that its dropdown no longer lists. Behaviour values, such as a collectible type, are errors and block Build; presentation values, such as a sound or a biome, are warnings.

  1. Note the room, object ID and property that the diagnostic names.
  2. In Tiled, choose File ▸ Open File or Project... and reopen MVTK.tiled-project to refresh the dropdowns.
  3. Open that room.
  4. Find the object ID in View ▸ Views and Toolbars ▸ Objects. For a map property such as biome, choose Map ▸ Map Properties... instead.
  5. Choose a listed value for that property. To keep the old value instead, add it back to the config file that the diagnostic names.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.

The same diagnostics appear at Build start, in Game Settings, in Analyze Progression and during Layout Editor Export. Each check reads the config files fresh, including ones that other config files load, so a config edit needs no editor restart.

Prepare & Check reports an economy problem#

An economy_ message identifies the file or room, object ID and field to fix. Errors block preparation; warnings and information leave design choices to you. The economy diagnostics table explains all messages, including invalid rewards and costs, trainer start unlocks, catalog kinds, currency sources and sale prices.

  1. Note the location and field named in the diagnostic.
  2. Open the named config file, or the named room in Tiled.
  3. Select the object ID in View ▸ Views and Toolbars ▸ Objects when the message names a Tiled object.
  4. Correct the named field using the diagnostic's fix.
  5. Save the edited file.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room if you changed a Tiled object.
  7. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.

For economy_sell_share_range, Selling ▸ Sell share on a Shop and Vendor ▸ Sell share on an NPC accept -1 to inherit or 0 to 1. The inherited value comes from Game Settings ▸ Game ▸ Shops ▸ Sell price share over shops.lua's settings.sell_share. An altar's economy_bad_grant or economy_altar_without_reward points to Payout ▸ Reward. A misspelled catalog uses catalog_value_unknown, with the dropdown steps above. Build is not needed to repeat these checks.

Prepare & Check reports a combat trial problem#

Each combat_trial_ diagnostic names the room, trial and object ID. Errors block preparation; warnings and information identify designs that need a combat playtest.

Diagnostic Meaning and correction
combat_trial_encounter_not_signal Set the linked Encounter's start ▸ encounter_start to on_signal.
combat_trial_encounter_not_repeatable Tick the linked Encounter's rewards ▸ repeatable.
combat_trial_encounter_reward Clear the Encounter's rewards ▸ reward_collectible to avoid paying it on every clear; use the trial's first-clear rewards ▸ trial_reward.
combat_trial_enemy_drops Untick the linked Encounter's flow ▸ enemy_drops to prevent farming loot on retries.
combat_trial_target_unreachable Lower goal ▸ defeat_target to the scheduled linked spawn total, or add Encounter Spawn counts.
combat_trial_survive_no_limit Set goal ▸ time_limit above zero for a survive goal.
combat_trial_ranks_order Put enabled ranks ▸ rank_1, rank_2 and rank_3 thresholds in increasing order; -1 disables a rank. Lower seconds or hit counts are better.
combat_trial_reward_invalid Correct rewards ▸ trial_reward using the grant error's named kind, id or count.
combat_trial_duplicate_id Give each pedestal a permanent unique trial_id across all rooms.
combat_trial_in_boss_room A boss, boss arena or champion shares the room. Prefer a separate trial room, or playtest the interaction without entering the boss arena during a trial.
combat_trial_near_door Move the pedestal away from painted doorways. This proximity warning uses the player collider; runtime checks the player's actual position before starting.
combat_trial_room_exit A teleporter or elevator can abandon the trial by carrying the player out. Move it or playtest this exit.

A missing link uses combat_trial_no_encounter: set encounter_ref to an Encounter in the same room, or put the pedestal inside its rectangle. The sample world's stations room intentionally shares its trial with the duo boss arena, so combat_trial_in_boss_room is expected there.

  1. Note the room and object ID in the diagnostic.
  2. Open that room in Tiled.
  3. Select the named object in View ▸ Views and Toolbars ▸ Objects.
  4. Correct the field named in the diagnostic's fix.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.

Analyze Progression says a trial reward needs a playtest#

Project ▸ Analyze Progression treats a trial's clear flag and grants as possible rewards. Its reason is Clearing this trial requires a combat playtest. An ability, key or flag behind that reward can make a route unknown; the analysis cannot prove the player can win the trial. Currency and other combat economy still require playtesting.

  1. Choose Project ▸ Analyze Progression.
  2. Find the trial's room and object ID in the report.
  3. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  4. Choose Play Game.
  5. Start an empty save slot.
  6. Clear the trial to check its first-clear reward and the route it unlocks.

A room is missing from Layout Editor#

  • Confirm both <room>.lua and <room>.tilemap exist.
  • Run Prepare & Check.
  • Confirm developer/rooms/collections/<room>.collection exists after generation.
  • Open Layout Editor and click Rescan Rooms.
  • Use a simple unique room filename.

Rooms do not snap or transitions fail#

  • Paint at least one tile on each intended door_* layer.
  • Connect right ↔ left or top ↔ bottom.
  • Leave a physical opening in art/collision at the marker.
  • Export both rooms, rebuild, Rescan Rooms, and place them again.
  • Click Layout Editor Export after snapping.

The wrong room or position loads#

  • Keep exactly one intended player_start for a simple project.
  • Avoid multiple maps marked is_starting_room=true.
  • Export/rebuild after changing the start.
  • Use an empty save slot; an existing checkpoint may override the new start.
  • Export the layout before Play Game.

A Tiled object does not appear#

  • Put it on the visible top-level templates layer.
  • Use a shipped .tx instance rather than drawing an untyped rectangle.
  • Keep mvtk_template_version unchanged.
  • Fill required IDs/dropdowns and avoid duplicate unique IDs.
  • Export, Prepare & Check, and inspect the generated collection in Defold.

Ground enemies at ledges#

  1. In Tiled, place an Enemy on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Select the placed Enemy.
  3. Expand movement_and_attacks.
  4. Set movement to ground_patrol_chase.
  5. Expand stats.
  6. Set ledge_behavior to fall.
  7. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  8. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  11. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  12. Click Export.
  13. Choose Play Game.
  14. Start on an empty save slot.
  15. Lure the enemy off a ledge onto a lower floor.

stop turns at ledges. fall walks off and lands below. fall_when_chasing stays on its platform during patrol and drops only while chasing you. type_default inherits the resolved archetype's ledge_behavior, then the project default. Ground movers follow slopes and small steps; bottomless pits defeat falling enemies without loot, using their usual respawn policy. Falling jump attackers arc and land; stop attackers keep their jump animation at floor height.

To change the project defaults:

  1. With the game stopped, choose Project ▸ Game Settings….
  2. Select Enemies and elements.
  3. In Enemies, set Default ledge behaviour to fall.
  4. Set Enemy fall gravity (tiles/s²) to the downward acceleration.
  5. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  7. Choose Play Game.
  8. Start on an empty save slot.
  9. Lure an art-only ground enemy with type_default off a ledge.

The default is stop. An instance's explicit choice overrides its archetype, and an archetype's explicit choice overrides Game Settings. Other fall tuning is commented in developer/config/enemy_defaults.lua. Reopen the Tiled project after Prepare & Check refreshes the dropdowns. Check patrol versus chase, platform landings, slopes, steps, jump arcs and pit defeats in game. Run Prepare & Check again before Export Game after settings changes.

Enemy nests#

A nest is an Enemy with archetype set to nest. It stays put, has no contact damage or push, and charges before summoning up to three living helpers. Defeating it removes its helpers by default.

  1. In Tiled, place an Enemy template on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Set archetype to nest.
  3. Choose the nest's art in enemy_type.
  4. Expand summon.
  5. Choose the helpers' stats in summon_archetype.
  6. Choose their art in summon_enemy_type.
  7. Expand stats.
  8. Set required_action to ground_pound to accept only that attack, or none for any hit.
  9. Expand on_defeat.
  10. Set respawn to never.
  11. Enter a stable defeat_flag, such as nest_cleared.
  12. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  13. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  15. Reopen the Tiled project to refresh its archetype dropdown.
  16. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Build.
  17. On the title screen, choose Layout Editor.
  18. Click Export.
  19. Choose Play Game.
  20. Start on an empty save slot.

required_action is available on every Enemy. Its dropdown lists none, dash, slide, ground_pound, wall_climb, grapple, light_attack, heavy_attack and backdash. Choose an action that actually deals damage in your game; the gate does not turn movement into an attack. bomb, charge_attack and super_jump are not listed. A required action blocks all other or untagged hits, including damage-over-time ticks, with the usual block feedback. elements ▸ vulnerable_to separately restricts accepted damage types; both restrictions must pass.

The nest archetype already defaults to never. Its defeat_flag uses the existing saved Flags rule described in Save compatibility. Save after clearing it, then reload to check it stays gone. With Save after a permanent defeat off, dying before saving restores the last saved state. Check the ground-pound nest with another attack first, then with Ground Pound. Tune helper limits as described below. After config changes, run Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

An enemy that summons helpers#

  1. In Tiled, place an Enemy template on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Set archetype to summoner.
  3. Expand summon.
  4. Choose the helpers' art in summon_enemy_type.
  5. Choose their stats and behaviour in summon_archetype.
  6. Set summon_count to the number attempted after each charge.
  7. Set summon_interval to positive cooldown seconds.
  8. Set summon_max_alive to the living helper limit for this summoner.
  9. Set summon_max_total to the successful helper limit per life, or 0 for unlimited.
  10. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  11. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  13. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  14. On the title screen, choose Layout Editor.
  15. Click Export.
  16. Choose Play Game.
  17. Start on an empty save slot.

The six summon fields inherit with -1 for numbers or none for dropdowns. none art uses the configured summon art, then the minion archetype's skin or default. For another enemy archetype, select summon_minion in movement_and_attacks ▸ attack_pattern.

The archetype's commented summon table in developer/config/enemy_types.lua also controls radius, telegraph_time, despawn_with_summoner, drops and the optional effect id from fx_types.lua. Fallbacks are in enemy_defaults.lua. Helpers settle onto the floor and use their own art and body at Size 1. They drop no loot unless drops is true. A rejected spawn still waits for the cooldown. Game Settings ▸ Enemies and elements ▸ Enemies ▸ Enemy cap per room limits placed enemies and helpers together.

Approach the enemy and watch its charge before helpers appear. Compare despawn_with_summoner = true and false: helpers disappear on defeat only when true, including when the summoner goes dormant. A living summoner's despawn always clears its helpers, and room cleanup removes any survivors. After config changes, run Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

A timed spike shell or Shield guard#

  1. In Tiled, place an Enemy template on the top-level templates layer.
  2. Choose its art in enemy_type.
  3. Set archetype to spike_shell.
  4. Expand tuning.
  5. Set spike_offset to a nonnegative number of seconds, such as 0.5.
  6. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  7. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  9. In Tiled, reopen MVTK.tiled-project with File ▸ Open File or Project….
  10. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Build.
  11. In the Layout Editor, click Export.
  12. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.

The shell repeats an exposed opening, a warning, and an armed interval. It plays charge art when available and shows a warning tint. Armed contact deals twice the base damage by default. Hits while armed do no enemy damage, knockback, poise, i-frames, hurt sound or hurt animation; they give a block sound and flash, and BLOCK when Damage numbers is on. Your attack's impact shake, hitstop and hit sound still play before the enemy decides to block. Damage over time passes through the guard.

Tune spike_exposed_time, spike_warn_time, spike_armored_time, spike_contact_multiplier and spike_start_phase on the spike_shell entry in developer/config/enemy_types.lua. The starting step is exposed, warn or armored; the placed offset advances from that step. Different offsets let a row of shells open at different times. The movement_and_attacks group's attack_pattern dropdown also offers spike_cycle for other archetypes; enemy_defaults.lua provides their fallback timings and warning colour.

The shield attack raises and lowers the guard. Its default timing is two seconds raised and two seconds lowered; defense.shield_up_time and defense.shield_down_time in developer/config/enemy_defaults.lua set those durations. shield_tell_period sets the white tell interval. An archetype's defense table can override either Shield duration. An enemy with only Shield blocks from every side while raised. The knight archetype now blocks only from the front while its Shield is raised; rear hits deal normal damage.

To try guards and armor:

  1. Select a placed Enemy on Tiled's top-level templates layer.
  2. Set Archetype to shield_bearer or armored.
  3. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  4. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  7. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.

The shield_bearer always guards its facing side. Front melee hits show BLOCK, play a sound and spark, and push the player back; rear hits deal 1.5 times damage. A heavy attack or parry opens its guard for one second. The armored archetype shows ARMOR on light hits; the third heavy attack, Ground Pound or down strike removes its armor and deals damage. Damage over time bypasses guards and armor, but an enemy's Stats ▸ Required action still rejects every mismatched or untagged hit, including damage over time. A parry stun consumes no armor unless its requirements explicitly include parry.

Defense settings live in commented defense tables in developer/config/enemy_types.lua, with fallbacks in enemy_defaults.lua. guard accepts none, front, top or all; guard_break and armor_requires accept attack-tag lists. guard_hits counts breaking hits, guard_open_time sets the opening and stun, armor_hits sets armor durability, and rear_damage_mult scales direct rear hits. Armor and partial guard counts reset when that enemy revives. Guard direction uses the attacker's previous-frame world position, including the player's position for shots and movement contacts. If that position is unavailable, horizontal knockback supplies the side. Ranged attacks, bombs and throws never cause guard recoil. Framework feedback words use the combat.blocked and combat.armored translation keys; Damage numbers controls their labels. The Dev Menu's Stats AI labels include guard state and remaining armor.

After changing developer/config, developer/ui or editor hooks, run Project ▸ Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

Enemy art or animations are missing#

  • Confirm a linked skin contains idle_01.png; a directly supplied project folder can also use idle.png.
  • Use individual PNG frames, not one strip/sheet.
  • Use recognized state prefixes and consistent frame dimensions.
  • For linked art, confirm art points to an existing frame outside the Defold project, named <state>_<number>.png. Export again after changing the original frames.
  • For project folders, leave art empty and choose the synced folder ID in enemy_type.
  • Run Prepare & Check after Defold finishes importing files, then Build.
  • Restart Tiled after updating the toolkit's exporter or templates.

Music or ambience is silent#

  • Use .ogg or .wav, not MP3.
  • Set map music as a file property for room-wide music.
  • Save/export the room, then run Prepare & Check.
  • Check the Console for an unsupported extension or missing source path.
  • For music_trigger/ambience_zone, choose an ID already registered by sync/config.

Boss arena or phases do not link#

  • Give boss, boss_arena, and boss_phase matching boss_id/arena_id values.
  • Start with a single boss phase and a known boss_type.
  • Keep the boss point inside the arena rectangle.
  • Remove optional boss phase triggers/weak points until the base fight validates.
  • Re-export and Prepare & Check after every boss-object change.

Layout changes appear in editor but not Play Game#

Click Layout Editor Export and wait for completion. Menu and placement autosave do not replace the published runtime layout.

14. Validate, Release, And Report Bugs#

Final creator validation checklist#

  • Every changed .tmx and .tsx is saved and exported.
  • Reload Editor Scripts, Prepare & Check, and Build all succeed.
  • Layout Editor Export succeeds after the final placement.
  • A new empty save starts in the intended room.
  • Every door works in both intended directions.
  • Checkpoints/save points survive a restart.
  • Required abilities, keys, gates, secrets, and boss rewards persist correctly.
  • Music/ambience changes are intentional and not restarting unexpectedly.
  • No red Console error appears during a full playthrough of the test path.

Create a standalone release project#

Project ▸ Export Game is different from Build and from Layout Editor Export. It packages the published room/layout revision into a standalone Defold project under export/. It does not regenerate rooms. Stale or unpublished changes must be prepared, built, and published before exporting.

Before using it:

  1. Export the rooms from Tiled.
  2. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  3. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  5. Export the final Layout Editor layout.
  6. Playtest with a clean save.
  7. Run Project ▸ Export Game.
  8. Open export/game.project as a separate Defold project.
  9. Choose Project ▸ Fetch Libraries.
  10. Build the standalone project.
  11. Playtest the standalone project.
  12. Use Defold's normal bundle command for the target platform only after that standalone project passes.

Do not confuse export/ with your editable source. Regenerate it from the main toolkit project.

The standalone title screen shows your game name and opens save-slot selection; Game Settings and Layout Editor stay in the source project. Export includes published room data, referenced runtime art/resources, configuration, and creator-declared custom/bundle resources. By default, source art, Tiled originals, authoring reports, unused room resources, dependency caches, and build folders are omitted. export/EXPORT_MANIFEST.txt lists each included file and why it was selected. Keep dynamically selected custom data in game.project's custom_resources or bundle_resources so it is included explicitly. Custom stream paths use the runtime's configuration resolver; export reports an error if their Lua configuration cannot be evaluated instead of using other paths.

Export writes and verifies a staging project before replacing export/. A failed write or publication preserves the previous export; retrying recovers an interrupted directory swap. Changes made directly in export/ are replaced by the next export.

File a useful bug report#

Report the problem on the toolkit's issue tracker. Include:

  • Toolkit version/build information from BUILD_INFO.txt when present.
  • Windows version, Defold version, and Tiled version.
  • The numbered guide step and exact menu command that failed.
  • What you expected and what actually happened.
  • The first relevant red Console error and nearby lines.
  • debug_output.txt if Prepare & Check names it.
  • The smallest affected .tmx, .tsx, exported .lua, or config needed to reproduce.
  • Whether the source map is inside or outside the toolkit.
  • Whether a new empty save slot reproduces the issue.

Do not attach secrets, paid asset packs you cannot redistribute, or personal paths without redacting them. For a crash, also state whether the editor, game, Tiled, or an editor command crashed.

15. Testing tools#

  1. Build in Defold and play a fresh save slot. The debug tools are already included and start hidden. In developer/config/debug_config.lua, dev_tools.enabled enables them, start_visible shows stats on boot, and cheats shows the Cheats and Boss practice pages. These settings are inert in release builds; designer previews disable the tools.
  2. Use the debug-build controls while testing: | Key | Action | |---|---| | F1 | Show/hide FPS, position, movement/combat state, health, energy and live stamina | | F2 | Toggle the engine's physics collision boxes | | F3 | Toggle attack hitbox drawing | | F4 | Open the Dev Menu; advance its tool page while open | | F5 | Toggle audio tracing in the console | | F6 | Cycle the console log level | | F7 | Reload combat configuration after Defold's Reload Resources | | Tab | Advance the Dev Menu tool page | | Up / Down | Select a row | | Left / Right | Page through rows | | Enter | Activate the selected row | | Escape | Close the Dev Menu | | Text / Backspace | Filter rooms, bosses, Cheats inputs or Saves flag prefixes / erase the last character | | Dev Menu page | Action | |---|---| | Saves | Inspect a saved slot without loading it or changing the active slot; read metadata, providers, flags, abilities, inventory, death drop and completion | | Boss practice → All abilities | Toggle temporary ability grants for the next practice | | Boss practice → boss row | Replay that placed boss, including every partner sharing its arena; rows show room, preset or placed identity, and a defeated marker | | Boss practice → End practice | Restart from the last save, discarding practice progress | | Stats → AI labels | Toggle movement patterns, attack-slot states, detection and HP above enemies; boss labels include their state and phase index. Up to 24 on-screen actors are shown, refreshed at 10 Hz per actor | | Stats → DPS meter | Toggle accepted enemy/boss damage summed over the last 5 seconds, divided by 5; show the session peak DPS and last 10 hits. Boss weak-point hits count once. Turning the meter off resets its session |
  3. Press F4 during gameplay, type part of a published room id, then select it and press Enter. The world pauses while the menu is open. Landing prefers the room's player start, then save point, then station, then an inset door. A room without a usable landing point uses its centre and shows a notice; check that location before continuing. Warps fade and reset movement; normal saving remains enabled. The menu refuses to open over another menu, a cutscene, completion or game over. Controllers keep their normal bindings; the Dev Menu uses keyboard controls.
  4. On the Dev Menu's Cheats page, use these rows. Any cheat first starts a test session; the persistent Saving off (developer tools) banner stays visible even with F1 stats hidden. Checkpoints and save points work during a test session, but nothing is written to disk. Sandbox deaths charge no saved currency fee. | Row | Action | |---|---| | Start test session (saving off) | Turn saving off before testing, including room warps | | End test session | Restart at the last saved state; turn cheats off and saving back on after arrival | | God mode | Ignore damage; pits return to safe ground even with Hazard returns off, while return spikes leave the player in place | | Infinite energy / Infinite stamina | Waive resource costs, including ability stamina checks | | Unlock all abilities | Grant the configured abilities through the normal reward path | | Refill health, energy and flask | Restore health, energy and healing charges | | Give currency | Type a positive whole amount in the filter, then choose this row | | Give item/key | Filter and select a configured item or canonical key; grant one | | Kill living enemies in current room | Use each enemy's normal death, loot and defeat event | | Reveal map | Reveal every eligible room | | Set flag / Clear flag | Type the exact flag name in the filter, then choose the row | | Game speed | Choose x0.25, x0.5, x1 or x2 for this test session | Clear the filter with Backspace before choosing another item. Left/Right pages through the cheats. Quitting keeps saving off through shutdown; Continue loads the real save with cheats off. A new game in another slot also starts clean. A plain warp outside a test session still saves normally.
  5. On Saves, select a slot and press Enter. Left/Right pages through its stored details; typing filters flags by an exact, case-sensitive prefix. Providers show their stored version and immediate payload key count; retired providers are labelled. Source identifies the primary or backup file. An absent legacy checksum is labelled separately from a valid one. Blocked, corrupt and missing slots show only their reason. Select Back to slots on the first details page to inspect another slot. This page reads the saved snapshot, so unsaved gameplay is not shown; opening, filtering and paging never save, load progress or change slots.
  6. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check to refresh the boss list.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build to run a debug build.
  8. Press F4 during gameplay.
  9. Press Tab until Boss practice appears.
  10. Select All abilities to toggle the optional temporary grant.
  11. Type part of a room, boss preset or placed identity to filter the boss rows.
  12. Select a boss row with Up / Down.
  13. Press Enter to start practice. Practice refuses a locked fight or an active Cheats test session. End the test session first. Saving off (boss practice) stays visible even when F1 stats are hidden. Defeated flags are cleared for every arena partner; rewards, notices and defeat scenes run inside the sandbox, so you can test their real flow. Winning restarts from the last save after defeat cleanup. On death, use the normal Restart action. To leave early, open Boss practice and select End practice. The restart discards rewards, temporary abilities and all unsaved progress since the last save, then enables saving only after arrival. No in-memory snapshot is kept. Re-arming in an already loaded room and entering an arena directly from the warp landing remain UNVERIFIED. If the fight does not start, step out of the arena and back in. Bosses without an arena start on room entry. The page is English-only debug text and Export Game removes it.
  14. Run Prepare & Check before Export Game. The exported project has no debug_overlay instance in developer/game/main.collection, even if you later build that project in debug mode. Release builds also remove the overlay at runtime and ignore these hotkeys.

16. More systems#

Prepare, publish, and finish a game#

After Reload Editor Scripts, Project → Prepare & Check coordinates asset import, room generation, resource synchronization, player settings, translation checks and validation with safe cancellation points. Translation checks run even when rooms are already current or the project is empty. When the world has rooms, Check world budget compares each room's neighbourhood with the engine limits in game.project and writes developer/assets/tiled/world_budget.txt; its findings are warnings unless developer/config/performance_budgets.lua sets block_on_overflow = true. When a limit is too small, Preparation Status offers Apply Recommended Engine Limits, which lists each change and updates game.project only after you confirm; run Prepare & Check again before Build. See World budget. Preparation Status explains changed files, missing source provenance, publication requirements, and rebuilds. Layout Export still explicitly publishes placements and the minimap; Export Game rejects stale publications.

Game Settings ▸ Ending configures the main region, flag, or boss ending, presentation and Continue policy. The ordered endings list in developer/config/game_completion.lua adds complete other endings with optional story, boss, completion and in-game time requirements; the first eligible other ending wins before the main ending. Each slot completes one ending, including after Explore. Other endings are optional objectives in Analyze Progression. Preview uses temporary state. The end_game template is baked as an editable room gameobject. Completion is saved before presenting credits or leaving gameplay. See Preparation and endings for the complete workflow, failure handling, and native acceptance checks.

Designer tools#

Open Project ▸ Game Settings… for player PNG import, camera tuning, HUD and prompt styling, map discovery, save policies and a disposable movement course. These tabs share the window’s draft; Save and Prepare & Check publishes and prepares it. Build afterward. Windows previews build a separate project and keep saves inside that copy. See Designer tools for import naming, controls and troubleshooting.

Accessibility#

Text size offers 100 %, 125 % and 150 % in Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility. It scales reading text: dialogue and its choices (long lines page with the usual advance button), lore popups, toasts, tutorial boxes and notices. HUD labels, list rows, tabs, menus and prompts keep their size. Open text refreshes when the choice changes. In Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility, Text size is first under Player defaults and Options players see; saved player choices win.

When a larger Text size shortens a toast, its marker names the current Interact binding. Press it to scroll the rest of the line; the marker follows keyboard, controller and remapped bindings. A toast that fits has no marker.

Players can change Screen shake (Auto, Full, Reduced or Off), Flashes (Full, Reduced or Off), Freeze frames (Full, Reduced or Off), and controller Vibration (On or Off) in Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility. Auto follows the creator's Game Settings ▸ Game ▸ Options ▸ Camera shake choice.

Choose the player defaults#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility with the game stopped.
  2. Choose Screen shake under Player defaults.
  3. Choose Flashes under Player defaults.
  4. Choose Freeze frames under Player defaults.
  5. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  7. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot with fresh preferences.
  8. Open Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility to check the defaults.

These defaults apply when a player has no saved choice. Player preferences are shared across save slots, so an empty slot alone does not clear them. Saved player choices win over new creator defaults.

Hide an option from players#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility with the game stopped.
  2. Untick the option under Options players see.
  3. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  5. Choose Play Game.
  6. Open Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility to check that the row is hidden.

Hiding an effect row keeps applying an existing saved player choice; it does not reset it. The commented baseline defaults live in developer/config/accessibility.lua. Saving Game Settings publishes overrides in developer/config/creator_settings.lua.

Press instead of hold#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility ▸ Options players see with the game stopped.
  2. Leave Players can choose press instead of hold ticked to offer the choices, or untick it to use the creator defaults.
  3. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  5. Choose Play Game.
  6. Open Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility.
  7. Turn Press instead of hold: Grab On to grab a contacted pushable block with one Interact press and let go with another.
  8. Turn Press instead of hold: Grapple On to stay hooked after releasing Grapple and let go with another press.
  9. Turn Press instead of hold: Heal On to start channel healing with a Quick Use tap when no quick item is selected and stop with another press.
  10. Turn Press instead of hold: Run On to keep running after a slide without holding Slide; press it again after the slide or stop moving to end the run.
  11. Turn Press instead of hold: Glide On to glide with one Jump press while falling after air jumps are spent; land or press Jump again to stop.
  12. Turn Press instead of hold: Aim On to enter the grounded aim stance with one Ranged Attack press and fire and leave it with another.

Only usable choices appear: Grab requires block grab; Grapple requires the ability and appears only when Game Settings ▸ Grapple ▸ Hook ▸ Stay hooked is off; Heal requires the channel healing model; Run requires slide-to-run to be on or unlockable; Glide requires the glide ability; Aim requires the combat aim stance to be on. The rows follow Caption background and precede assist options; use the next page when needed. On applies to keyboard and controller. Off keeps the original hold controls.

Creator defaults live in the commented toggles block of developer/config/accessibility.lua and start Off. Hiding the choices keeps saved preferences but uses these creator defaults instead. Reset accessibility restores the defaults. A menu, dialogue or cutscene ends an active toggle; death, respawn and teleport also clear it. Channel healing stops on a hit, empty energy or full health, and repeats only when channel.repeat_while_held in developer/config/healing.lua is true. Quick Use still uses a selected inventory item first. There is no Hold time slider.

Use assist options#

  1. Open Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility while playing.
  2. Choose Damage taken: 100, 75, 50, 25 or 0 %. At 0 %, ordinary enemy hits do nothing; kill contacts still kill.
  3. Choose Game speed: 100 down to 50 %. Physics, enemies and animations slow together; menus keep their normal speed.
  4. Turn Pit rescue On to return from pits and kill-mode hazards to safe ground, even when the creator's Hazard returns option is off.

Game speed also slows game-time clocks: the play timer, countdowns and trials. A countdown or trial that was slowed at any point still clears and pays its first-clear reward, but records no best time or trial rank. A save slowed at any point records no ending best time and cannot earn an ending achievement with a play-time limit. These time rules apply even with Record assist use off. Assist marks follow retained play time across death reloads, including assist switched off before death. A fresh load starts from saved marks only; quitting without saving discards unsaved marks just as it discards unsaved play time. These choices save with the player's preferences and apply across save slots. Pit rescue is a separate choice; reducing Damage taken does not turn it on.

Set which assist options players can use#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility ▸ Assist with the game stopped.
  2. Leave Players can use assist options ticked to offer all three assist rows, or untick it to hide them and ignore saved assist choices.
  3. Set Pit rescue damage from 0 to 100; difficulty and the player's Damage taken choice also scale this cost.
  4. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  6. Choose Play Game.
  7. Open Pause ▸ Settings ▸ Accessibility to check the offered rows.

Record assist use and choose achievement eligibility#

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… ▸ Accessibility ▸ Assist with the game stopped.
  2. Leave Record assist use ticked to mark saves that use reduced damage, slower game speed or Pit rescue and show Assist used in Journal Records and ending statistics, or untick it to hide and stop writing that record.
  3. Tick No achievements on saves that used assist to refuse unlocks on marked saves; it ships unticked and only works while Record assist use is on.
  4. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Achievement counters keep counting while unlocks are blocked. Achievements are shared across saves and can unlock on a save that never used assist. A creator may instead author an assist-use achievement with {type="flag", flag="assist_used"}; leave the achievement restriction off for that goal. Prepare & Check warns when the restriction is on but recording is off. New Game+ clears these save marks and marks the new cycle again if assist remains on.

Translate your game#

Players start in their supported system language, falling back to the game's default. With several languages, they can choose Settings ▸ Display ▸ Language unless Game Settings ▸ Languages ▸ Show language choice is off. Set the creator's default with Game Settings ▸ Languages ▸ Default language.

  1. With the game stopped, open Project ▸ Translations….
  2. Enter a stable lowercase Code, such as es, in the Add language fields.
  3. Enter the language's Own name, such as Español.
  4. Choose its Plural rule: one other for English or Spanish, none for one form, or one few many for Slavic integer forms.
  5. Click Add language.
  6. Select the new language in Language.
  7. Click Export Text for Translation to write developer/localization/<code>.tsv inside your project.
  8. Open that TSV in a spreadsheet as UTF-8, with tabs separating columns.
  9. Fill the translation cells, keeping id, where, source, every {name} or {input:jump} token and every printf token such as %d unchanged. Controls appear as \t, \n, \r and \\; keep those escapes where needed.
  10. Review any stale rows against their current source wording.
  11. Set each completed row's status to done.
  12. Save the spreadsheet over the same TSV as UTF-8 TSV (tab-separated text).
  13. Click Import Translations in the Translations window.
  14. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check to check every language.
  15. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  16. If you place rooms manually, open Layout Editor.
  17. In a manual layout, click Export to publish the updated game configuration.
  18. Play an empty save slot through Play Game to test the translated lines.

Leave untranslated rows missing; a done row cannot be empty. Import reports encoding errors and names rows with token errors. Correct those in the spreadsheet before importing again. Prepare & Check includes summaries such as Spanish: 12 missing, 0 errors; missing, stale and not-yet-imported TSV work produces warnings, while token mismatches stop preparation.

Import writes checked flat Lua tables and their bundling manifest under developer/config/translations/; creators use the TSV instead of editing those generated files. An untouched Export/Import preserves an existing translation table byte for byte. Re-export after changing game text: exact source matches keep their translations, and changed wording at a known location is marked stale. UI wording keeps its named meaning even when two English captions are equal. If several old sources at the same location have different translations, Export names the ambiguity and preserves the existing TSV. Save a copy, then resolve those competing translation cells before exporting again.

An Import stales the publication. Prepare & Check republishes a Tiled world; Layout Editor ▸ Export republishes manual placement after Build. Before Export Game, run Prepare & Check again after config, UI or editor-hook edits, and publish the manual layout if needed. Reset Project to Empty and the empty toolkit release restore English only and remove creator TSVs and translation tables.

Choose a font for your languages#

The default DejaVu Sans Mono Bold covers accented Latin, Greek and Cyrillic. Chinese, Japanese and Korean need a font that includes their letters.

  1. Copy your licensed .ttf or .otf into developer/assets/fonts/.
  2. Copy its licence into the same folder.
  3. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  4. Select Languages.
  5. Click Browse… beside UI font file to select the font (or enter its project-relative path).
  6. Click Save.
  7. Run Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Run Project ▸ Build.
  9. Play an empty save slot to check menus, HUD, world map and boss bars at your target resolutions.

Build UI font runs after Check translations every time. It generates ui_text.font and ui_label.font, retaining nominal size 14. Languages ▸ Text rendering offers Vector (the default) and Distance field. Vector stays sharp at any size but needs RGBA16F/R32UI textures; it cannot run on OpenGL ES 2 or WebGL 1 devices and has no automatic fallback. Exclude GLES 2.0 when building, or choose Distance field for those devices. Glyph generation offers Built with the game (the default) and On demand. On demand bundles the font file and needs the full text layout system. Prepare & Check updates and selects the toolkit's app manifest, preserving its other settings and platform list. For a different selected manifest, enable Use full text layout system there yourself; preparation refuses unsafe edits. Refresh the native engine after this change. Switching back, including Reset Project, restores static fonts and leaves the selected full-layout engine in place. Export Game preserves the source manifest selection. Keep the disabled generated_text_template in runtime text scenes, and measure text through GeneratedText.metrics in custom GUI scripts: Defold measures Vector text at a fixed 16 px while drawing it at the Font Size 14, and the helper corrects for it. A missing letter stops preparation with its language, code point and sample text. Choose a font that contains it, then run Prepare & Check again. Do not edit the generated Characters fields. After importing translations or changing the font, run Prepare & Check before Export Game.

Refresh a catalog dropdown in Tiled#

  1. Edit the commented catalog entry in its developer/config/ file (for example, npc_types.lua).
  2. In Defold, choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check. Its result and console name changed dropdowns.
  3. In Tiled, choose File ▸ Open File or Project... and reopen MVTK.tiled-project.
  4. Select the object and pick the new value in its dropdown.

Prepare & Check also refreshes these choices before checking exports when there are no rooms. Removing an entry removes its dropdown choice; update placed objects that still use the old value.

Status effects on enemies and bosses#

Enemy Elements offers Status immune, Status on hit and Status chance. Immunities add to the archetype's status_immune list in enemy_types.lua. none inherits the archetype's outgoing status; chance -1 inherits its probability, 0 disables application and 1 always applies it. Contact, melee and projectile hits carry the same status. Only an accepted hit applies it.

To place an enemy that poisons on hit:

  1. Place an Enemy template on the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Expand Elements.
  3. Set Status on hit to poison.
  4. Set Status chance to 1.
  5. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  8. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  9. Open Layout Editor from the title screen.
  10. Click Export.
  11. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.

Poison and Burn ticks damage enemies without knockback, poise, hurt animation or new hit i-frames. Freeze stops regular enemies for its duration; Slow scales their movement. Elemental hits can apply the default status configured in damage_types.lua. The Boss template's Damage affinities ▸ Status immune adds to boss_types.lua and status_effects.lua immunities. Bosses ignore Freeze by default. To allow it, remove freeze from status_effects.lua's bosses.immune list and any preset or placed immunity. Boss movement is unaffected by statuses; bosses still take fractional DoT, with resistance and elemental factors, without defense, a one-damage floor or per-tick hurt feedback. Statuses remain transient.

Status effects on the player#

Hazards can leave Poison, Burn, Freeze or Slow after contact. The commented developer/config/status_effects.lua controls durations, tick damage, stacking, immunities and walking speed. Freeze strongly slows the player without locking actions. Statuses expire and clear on death or respawn; they are never saved. The feature HUD shows one chip per active effect, with its remaining seconds. Poison stacks share a chip; pause holds the countdown. Labels come from the translatable source labels in status_effects.lua.

To tune effects:

  1. Stop the game.
  2. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  3. Select Enemies and elements.
  4. Find the Status effects section.
  5. Tick Status effects on.
  6. Set Poison: Duration (seconds).
  7. Set Poison: Tick interval (seconds).
  8. Set Poison: Damage per tick.
  9. Set Poison: Speed multiplier.
  10. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  12. Choose Play Game on an empty save slot.

Each effect has the same four fields, generated from the effect catalog. Damage is health per stack before the configured damage factors. A zero damage value disables damage ticks; a speed multiplier of 1 keeps normal walking speed.

To hide status chips:

  1. Stop the game.
  2. Open Project ▸ Game Settings….
  3. Select HUD and prompts.
  4. Find the Show on screen section.
  5. Untick Status chips.
  6. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  7. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Hiding chips leaves the status effects active. To check both effects together, apply Poison and Slow with hazards, then pause: both chips hold their remaining seconds. Resume to watch them count down. After changing Poison's tick damage, check that each poison tick uses the new amount.

To author a hazard status:

  1. Place a Hazard template on the top-level templates layer in Tiled.
  2. Set Hazard type to acid or cold.
  3. Expand Damage and knockback.
  4. Set On contact to damage.
  5. Expand Status.
  6. Choose Status effect (none inherits the hazard preset).
  7. Set Status duration in seconds (-1 inherits the effect configuration).
  8. Set Status chance from 0 to 1.
  9. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  10. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  11. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.

Hazard statuses and slows apply only in effective damage mode, after ability or item protection. Safe-return and kill contacts apply neither. Acid and cold slow walking while occupied. Hazard slows and movement zones share one slot: the stronger slow wins. Status slows use a separate source and multiply together, with a final speed floor of 0.25. Speed does not slow swimming or ladders (speed_mult affects walking). Pause holds ticks and durations. Test on an empty save slot: leave acid and wait for poison to expire, overlap acid with a sticky Movement Zone, then pause while burning. Hazard status application is independent of direct-hit i-frames.

After changing developer/config, developer/ui or editor hooks, run Project ▸ Prepare & Check again before Export Game.

Turn on leveling#

Leveling ships off. Levels give stat growth and optional milestone flags; they do not give skill points. The baseline developer/config/leveling.lua also holds table curves, source XP, energy/stamina/damage growth and milestone flags. Explicit enemy XP takes precedence over the default; only default elite XP uses sources.elite_mult from leveling.lua. XP rewards use xp:25 grant text and are skipped while leveling is off. Journal ▸ Records shows Level and Total XP while leveling is on.

  1. Open Project ▸ Game Settings… with the game stopped.
  2. Select Progression.
  3. Turn on Leveling ▸ Leveling.
  4. Set Maximum level.
  5. Set First level XP.
  6. Set XP growth.
  7. Set Enemy XP.
  8. Set Boss XP.
  9. Set Health per level.
  10. Choose whether Full heal is on.
  11. Click Save and Prepare & Check.
  12. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  13. Start Play Game on an empty save slot.

The HUD and prompts ▸ Show on screen ▸ Level and XP switch controls the level label and XP bar. The minimal HUD preset hides this block.

To set XP for one placed enemy:

  1. Select the Enemy in Tiled.
  2. Set Rewards ▸ XP value to a whole number (0 awards none; -1 inherits).
  3. Choose File ▸ Export Current Room.
  4. Choose Project ▸ Reload Editor Scripts in Defold.
  5. Choose Project ▸ Prepare & Check.
  6. Choose Project ▸ Build.
  7. Open the room collection to inspect the enemy instance's xp_value.

A Boss uses Rewards and persistence ▸ Reward XP: a positive value overrides its preset's on_defeat.xp; 0 inherits the preset, then the project Boss XP.

Defeat enemies and check Journal ▸ Records. Each level crossed shows one notice; a gain crossing two levels shows two. With Full heal on, a gain that crosses a level heals once. Save at a save point, quit, then Continue to check XP, level and health capacity.

Power-ups#

Power-ups are temporary world pickups, switched off by default. They are not stored in the Inventory. Their names, effects, duration and sound come from developer/config/powerups.lua.
